V\otimes_{k}(k\otimes_{A}M^{\prime})\rightarrow M^{\prime}\rightarrow k\otimes_{A}M^{\prime}

and hence the functor
M^{\prime}\mapsto k\otimes_{A}M^{\prime}
is conservative. We obtain a pullback square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Map_{\Mod_{A}(\mathscr{C})^{\simeq}}\big(A\otimes_{k}M,\,A\otimes_{k}M\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Map_{\Mod_{A}(\mathscr{C})}\big(A\otimes_{k}M,\,A\otimes_{k}M\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Map_{\Mod_{k}(\mathscr{C})^{\simeq}}(M,M)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Map_{\Mod_{k}(\mathscr{C})}(M,M).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces k\otimes_{A}-}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces k\otimes_{A}-}

Combining the two pullback squares and using the adjunction equivalence

\Map_{\Mod_{A}(\mathscr{C})}(A\otimes_{k}M,A\otimes_{k}M)\simeq\Map_{\Mod_{k}(\mathscr{C})}(M,A\otimes_{k}M)

for the map
k\rightarrow A
gives the asserted equivalence.
∎

Proposition 3.19
.

If
\mathscr{C}
admits a left complete
t
-structure for which
M\in\Mod_{k}(\mathscr{C})
is connective, then
\Def_{M}^{\mathrm{mod}}
is equivalent to a formal moduli problem. In particular,
\Def_{M}^{\mathrm{mod}}(A)
is essentially small for all
A
.

Proof.

Since the
t
-structure on
\mathscr{C}
is left complete, its connective part
\mathscr{C}_{\geq 0}
is a separated prestable
\infty
-category in the sense of
[
45
, Definition C.1.2.12]
. Just like in the proof of Proposition 16.5.7.1 in [op.cit], we can therefore deduce from Proposition 16.2.2.1 in [op.cit] that
\Def_{M}^{\mathrm{mod}}

satisfies
Definition

3.6
(2).

It remains to verify that each
\Def_{M}^{\mathrm{mod}}(A)
is essentially small.
For
A=\sqz_{k}(V)
a trivial square-zero extension, this follows from
Proposition

3.18
and the equivalence

\Def_{M}^{\mathrm{mod}}(\sqz_{k}(V))\simeq\Omega\Def_{M}^{\mathrm{mod}}(\sqz_{k}(V[1]))

induced by the pullback square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sqz_{k}(V)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces k}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces k}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sqz_{k}(V[1]).}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}

For general
A\in\CAlg_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{an},\mathrm{art}}
, one uses
Corollary

3.4
to obtain a factorisation

A=A_{n}\rightarrow\cdots\rightarrow A_{0}=k,

which implies by induction that
\Def_{M}^{\mathrm{mod}}(A_{i})
is small for all
i
.
∎

We can now rephrase
Proposition

3.18
in its more traditional form:

Corollary 3.20
.

Assume that
\mathscr{C}
admits a left complete
t
-structure for which
M\in\Mod_{k}(\mathscr{C})
is connective. Then for any perfect
V\in\Mod_{k}^{\mathrm{cn}}
, there is an equivalence

\Def_{M}^{\mathrm{mod}}(\sqz_{k}(V))\simeq\Map_{\Mod_{k}(\mathscr{C})}(M,V[1]\otimes_{k}M).

Proof.

Propositions
3.19
and
3.18
give equivalences

\Def_{M}^{\mathrm{mod}}(\sqz_{k}(V))\simeq\Omega\Def_{M}^{\mathrm{mod}}(\sqz_{k}(V[1]))\simeq\Map^{\prime}_{\Mod_{k}(\mathscr{C})}\big(M,\,\sqz_{k}(V[1])\otimes_{k}M\big).

We deduce the result using the decomposition
\sqz_{k}(V)\otimes_{k}M\simeq M\,\oplus\,(V\otimes_{k}M)
.
∎

Remark 3.21

(Tangent fibre of
\Def_{M}^{\mathrm{mod}}
)
.

We will mostly be interested in the case where
\mathscr{C}=\Mod_{\mathbf{Z}}
is the derived
\infty
-category of
\mathbf{Z}
and
M\in\Mod_{k}\simeq\Mod_{k}(\mathscr{C})
is eventually connective (i.e. cohomologically bounded above).
Writing
\underline{\smash{\Map}}
for the mapping complex,

Corollary

3.20
shows that the tangent fibre of
\Def_{M}^{\mathrm{mod}}

is given by

T_{\Def_{M}}\simeq\underline{\smash{\Map}}_{\hskip 1.0ptk}(M,M)[1].

Indeed, the connective chain complexes

\tau_{\geq{0}}(T_{\Def_{M}}[n])
and

\tau_{\geq{0}}(\underline{\smash{\Map}}_{\hskip 1.0ptk}(M,M)[1+n])

are equivalent for all
n\geq 0
, as they
correspond to equivalent presheaves under the identification
\Mod_{k}^{\mathrm{cn}}\simeq\mathscr{P}_{\Sigma}(\Mod_{k}^{\ff})
from
Example

2.1
. These send
V\in\Mod_{k}^{\ff}
to

\Def_{M}(\sqz_{k}(V^{\vee}[n]))\simeq\Map_{k}(M,V^{\vee}[n+1]\otimes_{k}M)\simeq\Map_{k}(V,\tau_{\geq 0}(\underline{\smash{\Map}}_{\hskip 1.0ptk}(M,M)[1+n]))

The above identification of
T_{\Def_{M}}
is related to the classical fact that the tangent space to the (classical) deformation functor of
M
is given by
\Ext^{1}(M,M)
, while the obstruction classes live in
\Ext^{2}(M,M)
.

3.5.
Deformations of sheaves of animated rings

Fix a pair
X=(\mathscr{T},\mathcal{O}_{X})
with
\mathscr{T}
a small Grothendieck site and
\mathcal{O}_{X}\in\Shv(\mathscr{T},\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}_{k})
.

Construction 3.22

(The functor
\Def_{X}
)
.

Consider the
\infty
-category

\mathscr{C}:=\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}\times_{\Shv(\mathscr{T},\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}})}\Fun(\Delta^{1},\Shv(\mathscr{T},\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}))

whose objects are pairs
(A,A\rightarrow\mathcal{B})
with
A\in\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}
and
A\rightarrow\mathcal{B}
a map in
\Shv(\mathscr{T},\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}})
.
Here we use the functor
\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}\rightarrow\Shv(\mathscr{T},\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}})
sending
A
to the sheafification of the corresponding constant presheaf.

Consider the projection
q\colon\mathscr{C}\rightarrow\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}

and write
\mathscr{C}^{\mathrm{cocart}}\subset\mathscr{C}
for the
non-full
subcategory consisting of all objects and only the
q
-cocartesian morphisms. These are precisely the morphisms in
\mathscr{C}
for which the underlying square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{B}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ulcorner}

in
\Shv(\mathscr{T},\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}})
is a pushout. By
[
44
, Corollary 2.4.2.5]
, the restriction of
q
to
\mathscr{C}^{\mathrm{cocart}}
is a left fibration, and the induced functor

\mathscr{C}^{\mathrm{cocart}}_{/(k,\mathcal{O}_{X})}\rightarrow\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}_{/k}

is also a left fibration. Straightening then gives rise to a functor
\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}_{/k}\rightarrow\widehat{\mathscr{S}}
. Composing with the forgetful functor
\CAlg^{\mathrm{an},\mathrm{art}}_{\Lambda}\rightarrow\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}_{/k}
, we obtain the functor

\Def_{X}\colon\CAlg^{\mathrm{an},\mathrm{art}}_{\Lambda}\rightarrow\widehat{\mathscr{S}}.

Remark 3.23

(Informal description)
.

Unravelling the construction, we see that the points of the space
\Def_{X}(A)
correspond to pushout squares

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces k}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}_{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ulcorner}

in
\Shv(\mathscr{T},\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}})
, i.e. to pairs consisting of a
\mathcal{B}\in\Shv(\mathscr{T},\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}_{A})
and an equivalence
k\otimes_{A}\mathcal{B}\xrightarrow{\simeq}\mathcal{O}_{X}
in
\Shv(\mathscr{T},\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}_{k})
.
The
1
-simplices of the space
\Def_{X}(A)
are given by compatible equivalences
\mathcal{B}\rightarrow\mathcal{B}^{\prime}
in
\Shv(\mathscr{T},\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}_{A})
.

For
V\in\Mod^{\mathrm{cn}}_{k}
,
k\rightarrow\sqz_{k}(V)
induces a map
\ast\rightarrow\Def(\sqz_{k}(V))
picking out the ‘trivial deformation’
\mathcal{B}\simeq\mathcal{O}_{X}\otimes_{k}\sqz_{k}(V)
. We compute the based loop space:

Proposition 3.24
.

Let
V\in\Mod^{\mathrm{cn}}_{k}
be perfect. Then there is an equivalence

\Omega\Def(\sqz_{k}(V))\simeq\Map^{\prime}_{\Shv(\mathscr{T},\CAlg_{k})}(\mathcal{O}_{X},\sqz_{k}(V)\otimes_{k}\mathcal{O}_{X})

where the implicit map
\sqz_{k}(V)\otimes\mathcal{O}_{X}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{X}
is the one induced by
\sqz_{k}(V)\rightarrow k
.

Proof.

Analogous to the proof of
Proposition

3.18
.
∎

Proposition 3.25
.

The functor
\Def
is a formal moduli problem. In particular,
\Def(A)
is essentially small for all
A
.

Proof.

First, we show that
\Def_{X}
satisfies the infinitesimal cohesiveness property in
Definition

3.6
(2). The argument is essentially the same as in
[
45
, Theorem 16.3.0.1]
.
For the sake of conciseness, let us write
\mathscr{C}_{A}:=\Shv(\mathscr{T},\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}_{A})
and
\mathscr{D}_{A}:=\Shv(\mathscr{T},\Mod^{\mathrm{cn}}_{A})
. It suffices to show that for every pullback diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{01}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}

in
\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}
with
\pi_{0}A_{0}\rightarrow\pi_{0}A_{01}
and
\pi_{0}A_{1}\rightarrow\pi_{0}A_{01}
surjective, the resulting functor

L:\mathscr{C}_{A}\rightarrow\mathscr{C}_{A_{0}}\times_{\mathscr{C}_{A_{01}}}\mathscr{C}_{A_{1}}

is an equivalence. Objects in the target can be identified with diagrams

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{01}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{B}_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{B}_{01}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{B}_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ulcorner}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\urcorner}

in
\Shv(\mathscr{T},\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}})
, where both squares are pushouts. The functor
L
has a right adjoint
R
, which maps the diagram above to the pullback of
\mathcal{B}_{0}\rightarrow\mathcal{B}_{01}\leftarrow\mathcal{B}_{1}
.

Using that
\mathcal{B}\otimes_{A}-
preserves pushouts in
\mathscr{D}_{A}
, and the fact that the forgetful functor
\mathscr{C}_{A}\rightarrow\mathscr{D}_{A}
is conservative, one sees that the unit
u\colon\mathcal{B}\rightarrow RL\mathcal{B}
is an equivalence. To see that the counit
v\colon LR\mathcal{B}_{\ast}\rightarrow\mathcal{B}_{\ast}
is an equivalence, it suffices to verify that it is an equivalence in
\mathscr{D}_{A_{0}}\times_{\mathscr{D}_{A_{01}}}\mathscr{D}_{A_{1}}
, which follows from
[
45
, Theorem 16.2.0.2]
.

To see that
\Def(A)
is essentially small, we proceed as in the proof of
Proposition

3.19

to deduce it from
Proposition

3.24
.
∎

Corollary 3.26
.

\Def_{X}(\sqz_{k}(V))\simeq\Map_{\Mod(\mathscr{T},\mathcal{O}_{X})}(L_{X/k},\,V[1]\otimes_{k}\mathcal{O}_{X})
.

Proof.

This follows from the equivalence
\Def_{X}(\sqz_{k}(V))\simeq\Omega\Def_{X}(\sqz_{k}(V)[1])
together with Propositions
3.24

and
2.31
, as
\mathcal{O}_{X}\otimes_{k}\sqz_{k}(V)\simeq\sqz_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}(\mathcal{O}_{X}\otimes_{k}V)
.
∎

Remark 3.27

(Tangent fibre of
\Def_{X}
)
.

When
X=(X,\mathcal{O}_{X})
is a smooth scheme, then arguing as in
Remark

3.21
gives an equivalence

T_{\Def_{X}}\simeq R\Gamma(X,T_{X})[1].

Corollary

3.26
is therefore a derived enhancement of classical Kodaira–Spencer theory, asserting that the tangent space of the (classical) deformation functor of
X
is given by
H^{1}(X,T_{X})
, while its obstruction classes belong to
H^{2}(X,T_{X})
.

3.6.
Deformations of schemes

For
X
a derived scheme over
k
, consider

(1)

deformations of the structure sheaf
\mathcal{O}_{X}
in Zariski sheaves;

(2)

deformations of
X
in derived schemes;

(3)

deformations of the étale structure sheaf
\mathcal{O}_{X,\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}}

in étale sheaves;

(4)

deformations of
X
in derived Deligne–Mumford stacks.

We will now show that these contexts
lead to equivalent formal moduli problems.

Zariski sheaves and schemes

We formalise deformations of
X
in
derived schemes
:

Construction 3.28

(Kodaira–Spencer moduli)
.

Consider the
\infty
-category

\mathscr{D}:=\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}\times_{\Sch^{\der,\mathrm{op}}}\Fun(\Delta^{1},\Sch^{\der,\mathrm{op}})

of pairs of animated rings
A\in\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}
and maps of derived schemes

Y\rightarrow\Spec(A)
.

Consider the projection
\mathscr{D}\rightarrow\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}
, which is cocartesian as derived schemes admit pullbacks (cf.
[
65
, Section 1.3.3]
,
[
39
, Proposition 2.3.21]
).
These are computed in
\Topp_{\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}}^{\mathrm{loc}}\subset\Topp_{\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}}
, the (non-full) subcategory of animated ringed spaces
(Y,\mathcal{O}_{Y})
such that
(Y,\pi_{0}\mathcal{O}_{Y})
is locally ringed, and local maps between them.
Cocartesian arrows correspond to morphisms in
\mathscr{D}
for which the underlying square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Spec(A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Spec(A^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}

is a pullback in
\Sch^{\der}
. Let
\mathscr{D}^{\mathrm{cocart}}
be the (non-full) subcategory of all objects and only
q
-cocartesian morphisms. By
[
44
, Corollary 2.4.2.5]
, restricting
q
to
\mathscr{D}^{\mathrm{cocart}}
gives a left fibration.
Then
X
gives an object
(k,X)\in\mathscr{D}
, and the induced functor

\mathscr{D}^{\mathrm{cocart}}_{/(k,X)}\rightarrow\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}_{/k}

is also a left fibration. Straightening then gives rise to a functor
\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}_{/k}\rightarrow\widehat{\mathscr{S}}
, and composing with the forgetful functor
\CAlg^{\mathrm{an},\mathrm{art}}_{\Lambda}\rightarrow\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}_{/k}
, we obtain the functor

\Def^{\sch}_{X}\colon\CAlg^{\mathrm{an},\mathrm{art}}_{\Lambda}\rightarrow\widehat{\mathscr{S}}.

To establish the equivalence

\Def_{X}^{\sch}\simeq\Def_{X}
, we need several
preliminary results.

Proposition 3.29
.

Given a deformation
(Y,\mathcal{O}_{Y})\in\Def^{\sch}_{X}(A)
of
X
over some
A
in
\CAlg^{\mathrm{an},\mathrm{art}}_{\Lambda}
, the underlying map of topological spaces
X\rightarrow Y
is a homeomorphism.

Proof.

The map
\Spec(k)\rightarrow\Spec(A)
is a closed immersion and a homeomorphism on underlying topological spaces. By the derived analogue of
[
40
, Corollary 10.5]
,
the pullback corresponding to
Y
is a pullback in animated ringed spaces.
∎

Proposition 3.30
.

Let
A\in\CAlg^{\mathrm{an},\mathrm{art}}_{\Lambda}
and
consider corresponding squares

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces k}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}_{X}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(X,\mathcal{O}_{X})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(X,\mathcal{B})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Spec(k)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Spec(A).}

The left square is a pushout of
\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}
-valued sheaves on
X

if and only if
the right square is a pullback of derived schemes.

Proof.

The ‘if’ direction follows as in
Proposition

3.29
from the fact that the pullback of

\Spec(k)\rightarrow\Spec(A)\leftarrow(X,\mathcal{B})
is computed in animated ringed spaces.

For the ‘only if’ implication, we assume that the left square is a pushout. We begin by showing that
(X,\mathcal{B})
is a derived scheme and that
(X,\mathcal{O}_{X})\rightarrow(X,\mathcal{B})
is a local morphism.
Choose a factorisation

A=A_{n}\rightarrow\cdots\rightarrow A_{r}\rightarrow\cdots\rightarrow A_{0}=k

as in
Corollary

3.4
; we can arrange that
n_{i}=0
for
i\leq r
and
n_{i}>0
for
i>r
.
Set
\mathcal{B}_{n}:=A_{n}\otimes_{A}\mathcal{B}
, so that we have fibre sequences

V_{i}[n_{i}]\otimes_{k}\mathcal{O}_{X}\rightarrow\mathcal{B}_{i+1}\rightarrow\mathcal{B}_{i},\ \ n_{i}\geq 0.

For each
i
, we obtain a short exact sequence

0\rightarrow I_{i}\rightarrow\pi_{0}\mathcal{B}_{i+1}\rightarrow\pi_{0}\mathcal{B}_{i}\rightarrow 0

with
I_{i}
a quasi-coherent sheaf of
\pi_{0}\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules. It follows by induction, using
[
62
, 05YV]
, that
(X,\pi_{0}\mathcal{B})
is a scheme and that the map
(X,\pi_{0}\mathcal{O}_{X})\rightarrow(X,\pi_{0}\mathcal{B})
is local. By
[
62
, 01LC]
,
\mathcal{O}_{X}
is quasi-coherent over
\pi_{0}\mathcal{B}
.

We also see by induction that
\mathcal{B}
is hypercomplete, and, using the long exact sequence on homotopy sheaves, that each
\pi_{k}\mathcal{B}
is a quasi-coherent
\pi_{0}\mathcal{B}
-module. Hence
(X,\mathcal{B})
is a derived scheme and the map
(X,\mathcal{O}_{X})\rightarrow(X,\mathcal{B})
is local.

The right square is a pullback because as in the proof of
Proposition

3.29
, this pullback in derived schemes is computed in animated ringed spaces.
∎

Proposition 3.31
.

Fix
A\rightarrow A^{\prime}
in
\CAlg^{\mathrm{an},\mathrm{art}}_{\Lambda}
and consider corresponding diagrams

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces k}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{B}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}_{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{(I)}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(X,\mathcal{O}_{X})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(X,\mathcal{B}^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(X,\mathcal{B})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Spec(k)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Spec(A^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Spec(A).}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{(II)}}

Assume that the right and outer square on the left are pushouts in
\Shv(X,\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}})
.
Then
(I)
is a pushout in
\Shv(X,\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}})
if and only if
(II)
is a pullback in
\Sch^{\der}
.

Proof.

Both left and outer square on the right are pullbacks in
\Sch^{\der}
by
Proposition

3.30
.
Square (I) is a pushout if and only if (II) is a pullback in
\Topp_{\CAlg}
. It suffices to check that
the pullback of
\Spec(A^{\prime})\rightarrow\Spec(A)\leftarrow(X,\mathcal{B})
in
locally
animated ringed spaces is computed in
\Topp_{\CAlg}
.
As
(X,\mathcal{B})
is a derived scheme, we can assume without restriction that
(X,\mathcal{B})=\Spec(R)
is affine. Here, the claim follows as the maps
\pi_{0}(R)\rightarrow\pi_{0}(A^{\prime})\otimes_{\pi_{0}(A)}\pi_{0}(R)\rightarrow k\otimes_{\pi_{0}(A)}\pi_{0}(R)
induce homeomorphisms on prime spectra, as the maximal ideals of
\pi_{0}(A)
and
\pi_{0}(A^{\prime})

are nilpotent.

∎

Corollary 3.32
.

For
X
a derived scheme over
k
, there is a canonical equivalence

\Def^{\sch}_{X}\simeq{\Def}_{X}.

In particular,
\Def^{\sch}_{X}
is a formal moduli problem.

Proof.

Define
\mathscr{C}
as in
Construction

3.22
for
\mathscr{T}
the Zariski site of
X
.
Write
\mathscr{C}^{\mathrm{art}}_{/(k,\mathcal{O}_{X})}\subset\mathscr{C}_{/(k,\mathcal{O}_{X})}
for the full subcategory on all objects over animated rings
A\in\CAlg_{/k}
satisfying conditions (1) and (2) of
Definition

3.1
. We define
\mathscr{C}^{\mathrm{cocart},\mathrm{art}}_{/(k,\mathcal{O}_{X})}
,
\mathscr{D}^{\mathrm{art}}_{/(k,X)}
, and

\mathscr{D}^{\mathrm{cocart},\mathrm{art}}_{/(k,X)}

similarly.
By

Proposition

3.30
,
Proposition

3.31
, and
Proposition

3.29
,
the natural functor
\mathscr{C}^{\mathrm{cocart},\mathrm{art}}_{/(k,\mathcal{O}_{X})}\rightarrow\mathscr{D}^{\mathrm{art}}_{/(k,X)}
gives a

fully faithful and essentially surjective functor
\mathscr{C}^{\mathrm{cocart},\mathrm{art}}_{/(k,\mathcal{O}_{X})}\rightarrow\mathscr{D}^{\mathrm{cocart},\mathrm{art}}_{/(k,X)}
.
∎

Definition 3.33
.

A connective module
M
over an animated ring
A
is
flat
if
\pi_{0}M
is flat as a
\pi_{0}A
-module, and if for every
n
the map

\pi_{n}A\otimes_{\pi_{0}A}\pi_{0}M\rightarrow\pi_{n}M

is an isomorphism. We say that a derived scheme
(X,\mathcal{O}_{X})
over
A
is
flat
if for every affine open
U\subset(X,\pi_{0}\mathcal{O}_{X})
the
A
-module
\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)
is flat.

Lemma 3.34
.

Let
A\rightarrow k
be in
\CAlg_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{art},\mathrm{an}}
, and
M
in
\Mod^{\mathrm{cn}}_{A}
. Then
M
is flat if and only if
k\otimes_{A}M
is discrete.

Proof.

By
[
43
, Theorem 7.2.2.15]
, the
A
-module
M
is flat if and only if
M\otimes_{A}N
is discrete for all discrete
A
-modules
N
. But every such
A
-module has a finite filtration whose graded pieces are discrete
k
-modules, so
M
is flat if and only if
k\otimes_{A}M
is flat. Since
k
is a field, this is equivalent with
k\otimes_{A}M
being discrete.
∎

Corollary 3.35
.

Let
X
be a quasi-compact and quasi-separated scheme and assume

\mathcal{B}
is as in
Proposition

3.30
. Then
(X,\mathcal{B})
is flat over
A
.

Proof.

We first show that
the natural map

k\otimes_{A}\mathcal{B}(U)\longrightarrow(k\otimes_{A}\mathcal{B})(U)
is an equivalence for

U\subset X
affine open.
As sheafification is local, we may assume that
U=(X,\mathcal{B})
. Here, the claim follows from
Corollary

2.26
, as the presheaf
V\mapsto k\otimes_{A}\mathcal{B}(V)
satisfies condition
Proposition

2.24
(
2
) on distinguished affines.
Hence
k\otimes_{A}\mathcal{B}(U)\simeq\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)
, and
Lemma

3.34
shows that
\mathcal{B}(U)
is flat over
A
.
∎

Hence
\Def_{X}(A)
is the space (
\infty
-groupoid) of flat derived schemes over
A

lifting
X
.
If
A
is discrete,
\Def_{X}(A)
is the
1
-groupoid of flat
schemes over
A
lifting
(X,\mathcal{O}_{X})
.

Zariski sheaves and étale sheaves

We proceed to the equivalence between
(1) and (3).

Let
(X_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}},\mathcal{O}_{X,\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}})
be the pair of small étale site
and étale structure sheaf of
X
(
Definition

2.44
).
Subsection
3.5
constructs two formal moduli problems
\Def_{X_{\mathrm{zar}}}
and
\Def_{X_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}}}
.

Lemma 3.36
.

For
X
a derived scheme over
k
, there is a canonical equivalence

\Def_{X_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}}}\rightarrow\Def_{X_{\mathrm{zar}}}.

Proof.

By conservativity of the tangent fibre (cf.
Proposition

3.9
), it suffices to verify that for every perfect
V
in
\Mod_{k}^{\mathrm{cn}}
, the natural map

\Def_{X_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}}}(\sqz_{k}(V))\rightarrow\Def_{X_{\mathrm{zar}}}(\sqz_{k}(V))

is an equivalence.
By
Corollary

3.26
, this map is given by the natural map

\Map_{\Mod(X_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}},\mathcal{O}_{X,\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}})}(L^{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}}_{X/k},V\otimes_{k}\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}}_{X})\rightarrow\Map_{\Mod(X,\mathcal{O}_{X})}(L_{X/k},V\otimes_{k}\mathcal{O}_{X}).

We now consider the usual pair of adjoint functors

\iota^{\ast}:\Mod(X_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}},\mathcal{O}_{X})\leftrightarrows\Mod(X_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}},\mathcal{O}_{X,\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}}):\iota_{\ast},

where
\iota_{\ast}
is obtained by evaluating an étale sheaf on Zariski opens.

It is not hard to see that the unit
\mathcal{F}\xrightarrow{\simeq}\iota_{\ast}\iota^{\ast}\mathcal{F}
is an equivalence for all quasi-coherent Zariski sheaves. Indeed, since sheafification is local, it suffices to check this on affine opens
U\subset X
, where the claim is an instance of faithfully flat descent (cf.
[
45
, D.6.3.1]
).
We deduce that
i^{\ast}
is fully faithful on quasi-coherent sheaves, and so the claim follows from
Proposition

2.45
.
∎

Étale sheaves and Deligne–Mumford stacks

To construct our Serre-Tate period map in
Section

8
,
we need a variant of
\Def_{X_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}}}
.
Let
k
be a perfect field of characteristic
p
and set
\Lambda=W=W(k)
. Write
S_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}}
for the small étale site of
\Spec(k)
.

Notation 3.37

(The sheaf
\mathcal{W}
)
.

The sheaf
\mathcal{W}\in\Shv(S_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}},\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}})
sends an étale map

\coprod_{i}\Spec(L_{i})\rightarrow\Spec(k)
with
L_{i}/k
a finite separable extension to
\prod_{i}W(L_{i})
.

Notation 3.38
.

The structure map
f:X\rightarrow\Spec(k)
gives an inverse image functor

f^{-1}:\Shv(S_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}},\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}})\rightarrow\Shv(X_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}},\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}).

As before, we
denote all constant sheaves corresponding to
some
A\in\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}
by
A
.

Construction 3.39

(The functor
\widetilde{\Def}_{X_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}}}
)
.

Consider the
\infty
-category

\widetilde{\mathscr{C}}_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}}:=\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}_{W}\times_{\Fun(\Delta^{1},\Shv(X_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}},\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}))}\Fun(\Delta^{2},\Shv(X_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}},\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}))

whose objects are pairs
(A\ ,\ A\rightarrow f^{-1}\mathcal{W}\otimes_{W}{A}\rightarrow\mathcal{B})
consisting of an animated
W
-algebra
A
and a composition of maps of
\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}
-valued sheaves on
X_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}}
.

We have a natural projection
\widetilde{q}\colon\widetilde{\mathscr{C}}_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}}\rightarrow\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}_{W}
,
and write
\widetilde{\mathscr{C}}^{\mathrm{cocart}}_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}}\subset\widetilde{\mathscr{C}}
for the
non-full
subcategory consisting of all objects and only the
\widetilde{q}
-cocartesian morphisms.
These are the morphisms in
\widetilde{\mathscr{C}}
for which the underlying diagram

(3)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{-1}\mathcal{W}\otimes_{W}{A}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{-1}\mathcal{W}\otimes_{W}{A}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{B}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ \ \ \ulcorner}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ulcorner}

consists of pushouts.
By
[
44
, Corollary 2.4.2.5]
, the restriction of
\widetilde{q}
to
\widetilde{\mathscr{C}}^{\mathrm{cocart}}
is a left fibration.
As
\mathcal{O}_{S,\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}}=\mathcal{W}\otimes_{W}{k}
is the étale structure sheaf of
\Spec(k)
, we obtain a map
k\rightarrow f^{-1}\mathcal{W}\otimes_{W}{k}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{X,\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}}

and hence an object in
\widetilde{\mathscr{C}}
, which
we denote by
(k,\mathcal{O}_{X,\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}})
.

The induced functor

(\widetilde{\mathscr{C}}^{\mathrm{cocart}})_{/(k,\mathcal{O}_{X})}\rightarrow\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}_{W//k}

is also a left fibration. Straightening and restricting, we obtain a functor

\widetilde{\Def}_{X_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}}}:\CAlg^{\mathrm{an},\mathrm{art}}_{W}\rightarrow\widehat{\mathscr{S}}.

Proposition 3.40
.

Restricting along
\Delta^{\{0,2\}}\subset\Delta^{2}
induces a canonical equivalence

\widetilde{\Def}_{X_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}}}\xrightarrow{\simeq}{\Def}_{X_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}}}

of formal moduli problems sending a diagram as in (
3
) to the outer pushout square.

The proof of
Proposition

3.40
relies on a universal property of
the morphism
A\rightarrow f^{-1}\mathcal{W}\otimes_{W}A
. It is naturally formulated in the language of derived Deligne–Mumford stacks, which we will now recall. As it is not needed anywhere else in this article, we encourage the impatient reader to

proceed directly to
Section

4
.

Notation 3.41

(Sheaves on
\infty
-topoi)
.

For
\mathscr{X}
an

\infty
-topos (cf.
[
44
, Ch. 6]
), write
\Shv(\mathscr{X},\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}})
for the
\infty
-category
of limit-preserving functors
\mathscr{X}^{\mathrm{op}}\rightarrow\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}
.

Remark 3.42
.

Every small Grothendieck site
\mathscr{T}
gives an
\infty
-topos
\mathscr{X}=\Shv(\mathscr{T},\mathscr{S})
.
By
[
45
, Corollary 1.3.1.8]
,
\Shv(\mathscr{X},\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}})
is canonically equivalent to
\Shv(\mathscr{T},\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}})
.
Note that the
\infty
-topos
\mathscr{S}
of spaces corresponds to the topological space
\ast
.

Construction 3.43

(Animated ringed
\infty
-topoi)
.

The functor

\mathscr{X}\mapsto\Shv(\mathscr{X},\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}})^{\mathrm{op}}

from the
\infty
-category
\infty\Topp
of
\infty
-topoi to the
\infty
-category
\widehat{\Cat}_{\infty}
of (large)
\infty
-categories is classified by a cocartesian fibration

q:\infty\Topp_{\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}}\rightarrow\infty\Topp.

The fibre over
\mathscr{X}
is the
\infty
-category of pairs
(\mathscr{X},\mathcal{O})
, where
\mathcal{O}
is a
\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}
-valued sheaf on
\mathscr{X}
.

Example 3.44

(Étale spectrum)
.

For every animated ring
R\in\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}
,
the forgetful functor
\CAlg^{\mathrm{an},\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}}_{R}\rightarrow\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}
defines a sheaf
\mathcal{O}_{R,\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}}
on the étale site
\CAlg^{\mathrm{an},\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}}_{R}
.
We obtain an animated ringed
\infty
-topos

\Spet(R):=(\Shv(\CAlg^{\mathrm{an},\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}}_{R},\mathscr{S}),\mathcal{O}_{R,\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}})
and a natural map

\alpha_{R}:\Spet(R)\rightarrow(\mathscr{S},R)
in
\infty\Topp_{\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}}
.

Definition 3.45

(Global sections)
.

The natural inclusion

(\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}})^{\mathrm{op}}\subset\infty\Topp_{\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}}

mapping
A\in\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}
to the pair
(\mathscr{S},A)

admits a left adjoint
(\mathscr{X},\mathcal{O})\mapsto\Gamma(\mathscr{X},\mathcal{O})
.

Notation 3.46

(Strictly Henselian objects)
.

Write
\infty\Topp_{\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}}^{\sHen}\subset\infty\Topp_{\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}}

for the (non-full) subcategory whose objects are those
(\mathscr{X},\mathcal{O})
for which

\pi_{0}\mathcal{O}
is a strictly Henselian sheaf of commutative rings on the underlying topos
\mathscr{X}^{\heartsuit}
of
\mathscr{X}
, cf.
[
45
, Definition 1.2.2.5]
. The morphisms are
those
\phi:(\mathscr{X},\mathcal{O})\rightarrow(\mathscr{X}^{\prime},\mathcal{O}^{\prime})

for which
\pi_{0}\phi^{\ast}\mathcal{O}^{\prime}\rightarrow\pi_{0}\mathcal{O}
is local, see
[
45
, Definition 1.2.1.4]
.

We can now characterise
\alpha_{R}
by a universal property
(cf.
[
45
, 1.4.2.4]

[
39
, 2.2]
):

Proposition 3.47
.

The assignment
R\mapsto\Spet(R)
is part of an adjunction

\Gamma:\infty\Topp_{\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}}^{\sHen}\leftrightarrows(\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}})^{\mathrm{op}}:\Spet

with counit
\alpha
. For
(\mathscr{Y},\mathcal{B})\in\infty\Topp_{\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}}^{\sHen}
, postcomposing with
\alpha_{R}

gives an equivalence

\hskip-10.0pt\Map_{\infty\Topp_{\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}}^{\sHen}}((\mathscr{Y},\mathcal{B}),\Spet(R))\xrightarrow{\simeq}\Map_{\infty\Topp_{\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}}}((\mathscr{Y},\mathcal{B}),(\mathscr{S},R))\simeq\Map_{\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}}(R,\Gamma(\mathscr{Y},\mathcal{B})).

Following
[
45
, Section 1.4.4]
and
[
39
, Definition 4.3.20]
, we define:

Definition 3.48

(Derived Deligne–Mumford stacks)
.

A
derived Deligne–Mumford stack
is an animated ringed
\infty
-topos
(\mathscr{X},\mathcal{O}_{\mathscr{X}})
which is locally an étale spectrum. More precisely, this means that
there is a collection of objects
U_{\alpha}\in\mathscr{X}

such that

(1)

the map
\coprod_{\alpha}U_{\alpha}\rightarrow\mathbf{1}
to the final object of
\mathscr{X}
is an effective epimorphism;

(2)

for all
\alpha
, there is an
R_{\alpha}\in\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}
such that
(\mathscr{X}_{/U_{\alpha}},\mathcal{O}_{\mathscr{X}}|_{U_{\alpha}})\simeq\Spet(R_{\alpha})
.

Let
\DM^{\der}\subset\infty\Topp_{\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}}^{\sHen}
be the full subcategory of
derived Deligne–Mumford stacks.

Given a derived scheme
X
over
k
, write
(\mathscr{X},\mathcal{O}_{X,\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}})
for the corresponding derived Deligne–Mumford stack, where
\mathscr{X}=\Shv(X_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}},\mathcal{S})
.
We proceed to prove versions of Propositions
3.29
,
3.30
, and
3.31
in the setting of derived Deligne–Mumford stacks:

.

a pullback of derived Deligne–Mumford stacks
