with
A\in\CAlg^{\mathrm{an},\mathrm{art}}_{\Lambda}
, the underlying geometric morphism
\mathscr{X}\rightarrow\mathscr{Y}
is an equivalence.

Proof.

The lower horizontal map is a closed immersion by the derived analogue of
[
40
, Theorem 4.4]
, and an equivalence on underlying
\infty
-topoi by
[
62
, 04DY]
.
By the derived analogue of
[
40
, Corollary 10.5]
, it is a pullback in
\infty\Topp_{\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}}
. The claim follows by combining Propositions 4.3.1.5, 4.3.1.9, and 4.3.1.10 in
[
44
]
. ∎

Proposition 3.50
.

Let
A\in\CAlg^{\mathrm{an},\mathrm{art}}_{W}
and
consider corresponding squares

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces k}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}_{X,\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathscr{X},\mathcal{O}_{X,\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathscr{X},\mathcal{B})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathscr{S},k)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathscr{S},A)}

The left square is a pushout in
\Shv(\mathscr{X},\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}})

if and only if
the right square is a pullback in
\infty\Topp_{\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}}
.
If this holds, then

(\mathscr{X},\mathcal{O}_{X,\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}})\rightarrow(\mathscr{X},\mathcal{B})

belongs to
\DM^{\der}
.

Proof.

The equivalence follows by combining Propositions 4.3.1.5, 4.3.1.9, and 4.3.1.10 in
[
44
]
.
To see that
(\mathscr{X},\mathcal{O}_{X,\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}})\rightarrow(\mathscr{X},\mathcal{B})
lies in
\DM^{\der}
, pick a

factorisation

A=A_{n}\rightarrow\cdots\rightarrow A_{1}\rightarrow A_{0}=k

as in
Corollary

3.4
. Then
A^{\prime}:=A_{n-1}
sits in a pullback
A\xrightarrow{\simeq}k\times_{\sqz_{k}(V[n+1])}A^{\prime}

with
n\geq 0
. We may assume the claim has been proven for
A^{\prime}
.
The induced square

(4)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathscr{X},\mathcal{B}\otimes_{A}\sqz_{k}V[n+1])}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathscr{X},\mathcal{B}\otimes_{A}A^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathscr{X},\mathcal{O}_{X,\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathscr{X},\mathcal{B})}

of animated ringed
\infty
-topoi is a pushout, cf. e.g.
[
45
, Sec. 21.4.4]
, and the maps

(\mathscr{X},\mathcal{O}_{X,\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}})\rightarrow(\mathscr{X},\mathcal{B}\otimes_{A}\sqz_{k}V[n+1])\rightarrow(\mathscr{X},\mathcal{O}_{X,\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}})

both belong to
\DM^{\der}
since
\mathcal{B}\otimes_{A}\sqz_{k}V[n+1]\simeq\mathcal{O}_{X,\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}}\otimes_{k}\sqz_{k}V[n+1]
.

By induction,

(\mathscr{X},\mathcal{O}_{X,\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}})\rightarrow(\mathscr{X},\mathcal{B}\otimes_{A}A^{\prime})
belongs to
\DM^{\der}
, hence the top map in (
4
) is local, see
[
45
, Remark 1.2.1.7(b)]
.
This top map is also a closed immersion. By the derived variant of
[
45
, Theorem 16.1.0.1]
, square (
4
)
is a pushout in
\DM^{\der}
.
∎

Proposition 3.51
.

Fix
A\rightarrow A^{\prime}
in
\CAlg^{\mathrm{an},\mathrm{art}}_{\Lambda}
and consider corresponding diagrams

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces k}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{B}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}_{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{(I)}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathscr{X},\mathcal{O}_{X,\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathscr{X},\mathcal{B}^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathscr{X},\mathcal{B})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathscr{S},k)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathscr{S},A^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathscr{S},A).}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{(II)}}

Assume
both right and outer square
in the left diagram are pushouts in
\Shv(\mathscr{X},\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}})
.

Square (I) is a pushout if and only if (II) is a
pullback in
\infty\Topp_{\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}}
.
If this holds, then the morphism
(\mathscr{X},\mathcal{B}^{\prime})\rightarrow(\mathscr{X},\mathcal{B})
belongs to
\DM^{\der}
and the induced square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathscr{X},\mathcal{B}^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathscr{X},\mathcal{B})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Spet(A^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Spet(A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{(III)}}

is a pullback of derived Deligne–Mumford stacks.

Proof.

The asserted equivalence between (I) being a pushout and (II) being a pullback follows again by combining Propositions 4.3.1.5, 4.3.1.9, and
4.3.1.10 in
[
44
]
.

Hence assume that
(II) is a pullback in
\infty\Topp_{\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}}
, and let us show that
the map
(\mathscr{X},\mathcal{B}^{\prime})\rightarrow(\mathscr{X},\mathcal{B})
is local and the induced square
(III) is a pullback in
\DM^{\der}
.

Proposition

3.50
implies that
in the right diagram, both left and outer square are pullback squares in
\infty\Topp_{\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}}
whose top horizontal maps belong to
\DM^{\der}
.

First, let us assume that
(\mathscr{X},\mathcal{B})=\Spet(R)
is affine, and set
R^{\prime}:=A^{\prime}\otimes_{A}R
.
As
\Spet
is a right adjoint, we obtain a pullback square in
\infty\Topp_{\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}}^{\sHen}

(5)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Spet(R^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Spet(R)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Spet(A^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Spet(A),}

which is also a pullback in
\DM^{\der}
by
[
39
, Proposition 2.3.21]
.

As in the proof of
Proposition

3.31
, the map
\pi_{0}(R)\rightarrow\pi_{0}(A^{\prime})\otimes_{\pi_{0}(A)}\pi_{0}(R)
induces a homeomorphism on prime spectra.
By
[
39
, Corollary 4.3.12]
and the topological invariance of the étale site (cf.
[
25
, Théorème 18.1.2]
), the induced map of
\infty
-topoi
\Shv(\CAlg^{\mathrm{an},\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}}_{R^{\prime}},\mathscr{S})\rightarrow\Shv(\CAlg^{\mathrm{an},\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}}_{R},\mathscr{S})
is therefore an equivalence, which implies that (
6
) is a pullback in
\infty\Topp_{\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}}
.
Pasting with the pullback square

(6)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Spet(A^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Spet(A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathscr{S},A^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathscr{S},A),}

in
\infty\Topp_{\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}}
shows that
(X,\mathcal{B}^{\prime})\rightarrow(X,\mathcal{B})
is local and (III) is a
pullback in
\DM^{\der}
.

If
(X,\mathcal{B})
is a general derived Deligne–Mumford stack, we can pick an effective epimorphism
\coprod_{\alpha}U_{\alpha}\rightarrow\mathbf{1}
to the final object of
\mathscr{X}
such that for each
\alpha
,
there is an equivalence

(\mathscr{X}_{/U_{\alpha}},\mathcal{B}|_{U_{\alpha}})\simeq\Spet(R_{\alpha})
with
R_{\alpha}\in\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}
.

A square of sheaves on
\mathscr{X}
is a pullback if and only if it is a pullback after restriction to each affine
U_{\alpha}
.
As the locality of
\mathcal{B}\rightarrow\mathcal{B}^{\prime}
is a pullback condition, it is therefore implied by our earlier discussion.

We obtain an induced square (III) in
\infty\Topp_{\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}}^{\sHen}
and a comparison morphism to the pullback
\Spet(A^{\prime})\times_{\Spet(A)}(\mathscr{X},\mathcal{B})
in
\DM^{\der}
.
By
[
39
, 2.3.22]
, this morphism is an equivalence as its pullback to each
\Spet(R_{\alpha})
is one by our earlier discussion.
∎

We are now ready to prove
Proposition

3.40
. We will use the following notation:

Notation 3.52
.

As before, we will use the superscript
(-)^{\mathrm{cocart}}
to denote subcategories containing all objects and only cocartesian morphisms. We use
(-)^{\mathrm{art}}
to denote full subcategories
containing only those objects lying over animated rings
A\in\CAlg_{/k}
which satisfy conditions (1) and (2) of
Definition

3.1
.

Proof of
Proposition

3.40
.

Define
\mathscr{C}_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}}
as in
Construction

3.22
for
\mathscr{T}
the étale site of the derived scheme
X
over
k
.
Consider the
\infty
-category

\mathscr{E}=\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}\mathbin{\operatorname*{\times}_{\infty\Topp_{\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}}^{\mathrm{op}}}}\Fun(\Delta^{1},\infty\Topp_{\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}}^{\mathrm{op}})\mathbin{\operatorname*{\times}_{\infty\Topp_{\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}}^{\mathrm{op}}}}\DM^{\der,\mathrm{op}}

opposite to the
\infty
-category of arrows

(\mathscr{Y},\mathcal{B})\rightarrow(\mathscr{S},A)

with

(\mathscr{Y},\mathcal{B})\in\DM^{\der}
, as well as the
\infty
-category

\hskip-12.0pt\widetilde{\mathscr{E}}=\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}\mathbin{\operatorname*{\times}_{\Fun(\Delta^{1},\infty\Topp_{\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}}^{\mathrm{op}})}}\Fun(\Delta^{2},\infty\Topp_{\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}}^{\mathrm{op}})\mathbin{\operatorname*{\times}_{\Fun(\Delta^{1},\infty\Topp_{\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}}^{\mathrm{op}})}}\Fun(\Delta^{1},\DM^{\der,\mathrm{op}})

opposite to the
\infty
-category of arrows

(\mathscr{Y},\mathcal{B})\rightarrow\Spet(A)\rightarrow(\mathscr{S},A)

with
(\mathscr{Y},\mathcal{B})\rightarrow\Spet(A)
a morphism in
\DM^{\der}
.
Projection to the first coordinate turns
\mathscr{E}
and
\widetilde{\mathscr{E}}
into cocartesian fibrations over
\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}
. The derived scheme
X
gives two objects in
\mathscr{E}
and

\widetilde{\mathscr{E}}
, which we will both denote by
(k,X)
.

Proposition

3.49
,

Proposition

3.50
, and

Proposition

3.51
combine to give equivalences

(\widetilde{\mathscr{C}}_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}})^{\mathrm{cocart},\mathrm{art}}_{/(k,\mathcal{O}_{X})}\simeq\widetilde{\mathscr{E}}^{\mathrm{cocart},\mathrm{art}}_{/(k,X)}\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ ({\mathscr{C}}_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}})^{\mathrm{cocart},\mathrm{art}}_{/(k,\mathcal{O}_{X})}\simeq{\mathscr{E}}^{\mathrm{cocart},\mathrm{art}}_{/(k,X)}.

Here, we have used
the equivalence
\Spet(A)\simeq(\Shv(S_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}},\mathscr{S}),\mathcal{W}\otimes_{W}{A})
induced by the equivalence of sites
S_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}}=\Spec(k)_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}}\simeq\Spec(A)_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}}
.

The claim then follows since restriction along
\Delta^{\{0,2\}}\subset\Delta^{2}
gives an equivalence of cocartesian fibrations
\widetilde{\mathscr{E}}\xrightarrow{\simeq}\mathscr{E}
by

Proposition

3.47
.
∎

4.
Animated divided power rings

In this section, we will discuss the basic theory of animated divided power rings, following Mao
[
47
]
. These play a key role in our definition of the period map.

4.1.
Divided power rings

To begin with, recall the following classical definition:

Definition 4.1

(Divided power rings,

[
62
,
Definition 07GL
]
)
.

A
divided power structure
on an ideal
I
in a (commutative) ring
R

is given by a collection of maps
(\gamma_{n}\colon I\rightarrow I)_{n\geq 0}
such that
for all
n,m\geq 0
and all
r\in R
and
x,y\in I
, we have:

(1)

\gamma_{0}(x)=1,\gamma_{1}(x)=x
;

(2)

\gamma_{n}(x)\gamma_{m}(x)=\frac{(n+m)!}{n!m!}\gamma_{n+m}(x)
;

(3)

\gamma_{n}(rx)=r^{n}\gamma_{n}(x)
;

(4)

\gamma_{n}(x+y)=\sum_{i=0}^{n}\gamma_{i}(x)\gamma_{n-i}(y)
;

(5)

\gamma_{n}(\gamma_{m}(x))=\frac{(nm)!}{n!(m!)^{n}}\gamma_{nm}(x)
.

A
divided power ring
is a triple
R=(R,I,\gamma)
. A morphism of divided power rings is a morphism of rings
f\colon R\rightarrow R^{\prime}
mapping
I\rightarrow I^{\prime}
and such that
\gamma^{\prime}_{n}(fx)=f\gamma_{n}(x)
for all
x\in I
and
n\geq 0
.
Denote the category of divided power rings by

\PDRing
.

The axioms express that
\gamma_{n}(x)
‘behaves like
\frac{x^{n}}{n!}
’.

Example 4.2

(Free divided power algebras)
.

Given a ring
R
and an
R
-module
M
, we define
the
free divided power algebra
\Gamma_{R}(M)
of
M
over
R
(cf. e.g.
[
37
, Section 3.1]
) as the quotient of the free commutative
R
-algebra on symbols

\{y^{[n]}\ |\ y\in M,n\geq 0\}

by the following relations

(1)

y^{[0]}=1
;

(2)

y^{[n]}y^{[m]}=\frac{(n+m)!}{n!m!}y^{[n+m]}
;

(3)

(ry)^{[n]}=r^{n}y^{[n]}
;

(4)

(y+z)^{[n]}=\sum_{i+j=n}y^{[i]}z^{[j]}
;

There is a natural surjection
\Gamma_{R}(M)\twoheadrightarrow R
whose kernel
I
is generated by the symbols
y^{[n]}
with
n\geq 1
.
The ideal
I
carries a unique divided power structure
\gamma

satisfying
\gamma_{n}(y):=y^{[n]}.

Hence, we obtain a divided power ring

\Gamma_{R}(M):=\big(\Gamma_{R}(M),I,\gamma\big).

Note that
\Gamma_{R}(M)=\bigoplus_{n}\Gamma_{R}^{n}(M)
admits a multiplicative weight decomposition placing
y^{[n]}
in weight
n
.
If
M
is a free
R
-module on generators
y_{1},\ldots,y_{i}
, then

\Gamma_{R}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{i}):=\Gamma_{R}(M)
has
underlying
R
module freely generated by symbols

y_{1}^{[n_{1}]}\cdot\ldots\cdot y_{i}^{[n_{i}]}
with
n_{i}\geq 0
.

Example 4.3

(Trivial divided power structures)
.

Given a ring
R
, the zero ideal carries a unique divided power structure, making
R
into a divided power ring
(R,0,0)
. This construction identifies the category of rings with a full subcategory of the category of divided power rings.

4.2.
Animated divided power rings

Write
\PDPoly\subset\PDRing
for the full subcategory
spanned by all divided power rings of the form

\Gamma_{\mathbf{Z}[x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}]}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{m})

with
n,m\geq 0
, see
Example

4.2
. These are the divided power rings of the form
\Sym_{\mathbf{Z}}(N)\otimes\Gamma_{\mathbf{Z}}(M)
with
N
and
M
finite free
\mathbf{Z}
-modules.
Following
[
47
]
, we define:

Definition 4.4

(Animated divided power rings)
.

The
\infty
-category of
animated divided power rings

\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}:=\mathscr{P}_{\Sigma}(\PDPoly)
is obtained by freely adjoining sifted colimits to
\PDPoly
.

The inclusion
\Poly\rightarrow\PDPoly

induces a fully faithful functor
\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}\hookrightarrow\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}
. We will often identify
\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}
with its essential image, thinking of animated rings as being equipped with the trivial divided power structure (see also
Example

4.3
).

Notation 4.5

(Animated surjections)
.

An
animated surjection
is a map of animated rings
R\rightarrow R^{\prime}
which induces a surjection after applying
\pi_{0}
.
We write
\Fun(\Delta^{1},\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}})_{\surj}\subset\Fun(\Delta^{1},\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}})
for the full subcategory spanned by all
animated surjections.

Proposition 4.6

(Forgetful functors)
.

There is an essentially unique sifted-colimit-preserving functor

\forget\colon\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}\rightarrow\Fun(\Delta^{1},\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}})_{\surj}

mapping a
divided power ring
(A,I,\gamma)
in
\PDPoly
to the ring map
A\rightarrow A/I
. The functor
\forget
is conservative, and preserves small limits and colimits.

Proof.

This can be found in
[
47
, §3]
. Since our notation differs from
loc. cit.
, we guide the reader in extracting these statements.

By
[
47
, Theorem 3.23]
, we can write
\Fun(\Delta^{1},\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}})_{\surj}\simeq\mathscr{P}_{\Sigma}(\SurjPoly)
, where
\SurjPoly\subset\Fun(\Delta^{1},\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}})_{\surj}
consists of all ‘standard
surjections’ of ordinary polynomial rings of the form
\mathbf{Z}[X_{1},\ldots,X_{n},Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{m}]\xrightarrow{}\mathbf{Z}[X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}].

The ‘classical PD-envelope’
gives a functor
\SurjPoly\rightarrow\PDPoly
which is
essentially surjective and preserves coproducts.
Extending
\SurjPoly\rightarrow\PDPoly\rightarrow\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}
in a sifted-colimit-preserving way gives a functor
\Env:\Fun(\Delta^{1},\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}})_{\surj}\rightarrow\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}
.

By
[
47
, Proposition 2.2]
,
\Env
admits a right adjoint

\forget:\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}\rightarrow\Fun(\Delta^{1},\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}})_{\surj}

which is conservative and preserves small colimits, cf.
[
47
, Proposition 3.34]
.

Finally,
F
sends
(A,I,\gamma)
in
\PDPoly
to
A\rightarrow A/I
by

[
47
, Proposition 3.17]
.
∎

Corollary 4.7

(Underlying animated rings)
.

There is an essentially unique sifted-colimit-preserving functor

\mathrm{und}\colon\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}\rightarrow\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}

mapping
(A,I,\gamma)
in
\PDPoly
to
A
. It preserves small limits and colimits.

Proof.

The functor is defined by postcomposing
F\colon\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}\rightarrow\Fun(\Delta^{1},\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}})_{\surj}
with
\mathrm{ev}_{0}:\Fun(\Delta^{1},\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}})_{\surj}\rightarrow\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}
,
(A\rightarrow A^{\prime})\mapsto A
. The functor
\mathrm{ev}_{0}
admits a left adjoint
A\mapsto(\id:A\xrightarrow{}A)
and it preserves small colimits, as this is true for the inclusion
\Fun(\Delta^{1},\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}})_{\surj}\hookrightarrow\Fun(\Delta^{1},\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}})
.
∎

Remark 4.8
.

The (underived) ‘underlying ring’ functor from divided power rings to rings does not preserve colimits, see
[
62
, 07GY]
for an example.

Proposition 4.9
.

The functor
\mathrm{und}
is right adjoint to
\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}\hookrightarrow\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}
.

Proof.

This follows from
[
47
, Corollary 2.2]
applied to the coproduct preserving functor
\Poly\hookrightarrow\PDPoly
.
∎

Notation 4.10

(
I_{A}
and
\overline{A}
)
.

We will refer to
\mathrm{und}(A)
as the
underlying animated ring of
A
, and will usually just denote it by
A
. We write
\forget(A)=(A\rightarrow\overline{A})
, and denote the (connective) fibre of
A\rightarrow\overline{A}
by
I_{A}
.
There is a fibre sequence

I_{A}\rightarrow A\rightarrow\overline{A}

in
\Mod_{\mathbf{Z}}^{\mathrm{cn}}
, functorial in
A\in\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}
. For
A=(A,I,\gamma)
in
\PDPoly
, it is given by the short exact sequence

0\rightarrow I\rightarrow A\rightarrow A/I\rightarrow 0.

As its formation preserves sifted colimits, this determines the fibre
sequence in general.

Proposition 4.11
.

The construction
A\mapsto\overline{A}
determines a colimit-preserving idempotent endofunctor of
\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}
.

Proof.

The first claim holds by
Proposition

4.6
as the inclusion
\Fun(\Delta^{1},\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}})_{\surj}\rightarrow\Fun(\Delta^{1},\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}})
and the embedding
\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}\rightarrow\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}
both
preserve small colimits. The idempotence follows as
\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}\rightarrow\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}\xrightarrow{\overline{(-)}}\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}
is the identity.
∎

By
[
47
, Lemma 3.13]
and
[
47
, Proposition 3.32]
, we have:

Lemma 4.12

(Classical and animated divided power rings)
.

The functor
\PDPoly\rightarrow\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}
extends to a fully faithful embedding
\PDRing\rightarrow\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}
whose image consists of all
A\in\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}
for which both
\mathrm{und}(A)
and
\overline{A}
are discrete.

Remark 4.13
.

The above functor factors over
\tau_{\leq 0}\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}
, the category of finite-product-preserving functors
\PDPoly\rightarrow\Set
. Note that
\PDRing\rightarrow\tau_{\leq 0}\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}
is
not
an equivalence and
\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}
is
not
the ‘animation’ of
\PDRing
, see also
[
47
, §3.2]
.

This is related to the fact that the theory of divided power rings is not an algebraic theory in the style of Lawvere. A divided power ring consists of sets
I
and
R
together with various operations satisfying various relations, and the additional condition that the operation
I\rightarrow R
is injective. If one drops the injectivity condition, then one obtains a Lawvere theory, and its animation is
\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}
.

Notation 4.14

(Pushouts as tensor products)
.

We will denote the pushout of maps
A\rightarrow B
and
A\rightarrow C
in
\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}
by
B\otimes_{A}C
. By
Corollary

4.7
, the underlying animated ring is the pushout in
\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}
, whose underlying object in
\Mod^{\mathrm{cn}}_{\mathbf{Z}}
is the relative tensor product
B\otimes_{A}C
.

Construction 4.15

(
\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}_{A}
)
.

For
A
in
\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}
, we write
\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}_{A}:=\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}_{A/}
. For every map
A\rightarrow A^{\prime}
in
\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}
, the functor
\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}_{A^{\prime}}\rightarrow\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}_{A}
admits a left adjoint, given by the pushout
A^{\prime}\otimes_{A}-
. Proceeding as in
Construction

2.6
, we obtain a functor
\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}\rightarrow\PrL
mapping
A
to
\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}_{A}
.

4.3.
Induced divided power structure

If
(A,I,\gamma)
is a classical divided power ring and
A\rightarrow B
a
flat
morphism of rings, then the ideal
BI\cong B\otimes_{A}I
inherits a divided power structure, given by

\gamma_{n}(b\otimes x)=b^{n}\otimes\gamma_{n}(x),

see
e.g.

[
62
, 07H1]
. In this section, we give a derived analogue of this construction.

Notation 4.16

(
A_{\mathrm{triv}}
)
.

Consider the composite

\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}\overset{\mathrm{und}}{\longrightarrow}\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}\hookrightarrow\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}},\,A\mapsto A_{\mathrm{triv}}.

It is the sifted-colimit-preserving extension of the functor mapping an

A=(A,I,\gamma)
in
\PDPoly
to the divided power ring
A_{\mathrm{triv}}=(A,0,0)
.
The counit of the adjunction of
Proposition

4.9
gives a natural transformation
(-)_{\mathrm{triv}}\rightarrow\id
. For
(A,I,\gamma)
in
\PDPoly
, it is given by the identity map
(A,0,0)\rightarrow(A,I,\gamma)
.

Definition 4.17

(The functor
\ind
)
.

Consider the
\infty
-category

\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}\times_{\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}}\Fun(\Delta^{1},\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}})

whose objects are pairs consisting of an animated divided power ring
A
and a morphism of animated rings
\mathrm{und}(A)\rightarrow B
. We have a functor

\displaystyle\ind\colon\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}\times_{\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}}\Fun(\Delta^{1},\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}),

\displaystyle\rightarrow

\displaystyle\Fun(\Delta^{1},\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}),\,

\displaystyle(A,\mathrm{und}(A)\rightarrow B)

\displaystyle\mapsto

\displaystyle(A\rightarrow\ind_{A}(B))

given by sending
(A,\mathrm{und}(A)\rightarrow B)
to the following pushout in
\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{\mathrm{triv}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ind_{A}(B),}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ulcorner}

where we identify
B
with an object of
\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}
via
\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}\hookrightarrow\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}
as usual.

Lemma 4.18
.

Let
A\in\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}
and let
\mathrm{und}(A)\rightarrow B
be a map in
\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}
. Then
B^{\prime}:=\ind_{A}(B)
satisfies

(1)

the map
B\rightarrow\mathrm{und}_{A}B^{\prime}
is an equivalence in
\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}
;

(2)

the map
B\otimes_{A}\overline{A}\rightarrow\overline{B^{\prime}}
is an equivalence in
\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}
;

(3)

the map
B\otimes_{A}I_{A}\rightarrow I_{B^{\prime}}
is an equivalence in
\Mod_{B}
.

Proof.

Follows from
Proposition

4.6
.
∎

Alternatively, one could define the functor
\ind
as the sifted-colimit-preserving extension of the functor that maps a pair consisting of an
A=(A,I,\gamma)
in
\PDPoly
, and a
B
in
\Poly_{A}
to the divided power ring

\ind_{A}(B):=(B,B\otimes_{A}I,\tilde{\gamma})

with
\tilde{\gamma}_{n}(b\otimes x)=b^{n}\otimes\gamma_{n}(x)
, equipped with the obvious map
A\rightarrow\ind_{A}(B)
.

4.4.
Divided power cotangent complex

We recall
[
62
,
07HQ
]
:

Definition 4.19

(Divided power derivations)
.

Let
A
be a ring and
B=(B,I,\gamma)
a (classical) divided power
A
-algebra. An
A
-linear
divided power derivation
of
B
into a
B
-module
M
is an
A
-linear derivation
\theta:B\rightarrow M
satisfying

\theta(\gamma_{n}(x))=\gamma_{n-1}(x)\theta(x)

for all
n\geq 1
and all
x\in I
.

There is a universal
A
-linear divided power derivation

d=d_{B/A}\colon B\rightarrow\Omega^{1,\mathrm{pd}}_{B/A}

such that for all
B
-modules
M
, precomposition with
d
identifies the set of
A
-linear divided power derivations
B\rightarrow M
with
\Hom_{B}(\Omega^{1,\mathrm{pd}}_{B/A},M)
, cf.
[
62
,
Tag 07HQ
]
.

Remark 4.20
.

The module
\Omega^{1,\mathrm{pd}}_{B/A}
is a quotient of the module
\Omega^{1}_{B/A}
of Kähler differentials of the underlying
A
-algebra
B
by all relations
d(\gamma_{n}(x))-\gamma_{n-1}d(x)
.
If
B
carries the trivial divided power structure,
\Omega^{1}_{B/A}\rightarrow\Omega^{1,\mathrm{pd}}_{B/A}
is an isomorphism.

Example 4.21
.

If
B=\Gamma_{A[x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}]}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{m})
then
\Omega^{1,\mathrm{pd}}_{B/A}
is the free
B
-module generated
by
dx_{i}
and
dy_{i}
,
and the universal derivation satisfies
dy_{i}^{[n]}=y_{i}^{[n-1]}dy_{i}
.

Any commutative square of rings

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B^{\prime}}

with
B\rightarrow B^{\prime}
a map of divided power rings induces
a homomorphism

\Omega^{1,\mathrm{pd}}_{B/A}\rightarrow\Omega^{1,\mathrm{pd}}_{B^{\prime}/A^{\prime}}

and this makes
\Omega^{1,\mathrm{pd}}_{B/A}
into a functor of
A\rightarrow B
. To derive this functor, we will use compact projective arrows of animated divided power rings:

Definition 4.22

(
\ArrPDPoly
)
.

Write
\ArrPDPoly
for the ordinary category of maps of divided power rings
of the form

\Gamma_{\mathbf{Z}[x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}]}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{m})\longrightarrow\Gamma_{\mathbf{Z}[x_{1},\ldots,x_{n+n^{\prime}}]}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{m+m^{\prime}}).

Lemma 4.23
.

The natural functor
\ArrPDPoly\rightarrow\Fun(\Delta^{1},\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}})
induces an equivalence
\mathscr{P}_{\Sigma}(\ArrPDPoly)\simeq\Fun(\Delta^{1},\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}})
.

Proof.

See
[
47
, Proposition A.16]
.
∎

Definition 4.24

(Cotangent complex for animated divided power rings)
.

Let us write
\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}\Mod^{\mathrm{cn}}
for the
\infty
-category of pairs
(A,M)
consisting of an animated divided power ring
A
and an object
M
of
\Mod_{A}^{\mathrm{cn}}
. More formally, we have

\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}\Mod^{\mathrm{cn}}:=\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}\times_{\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}}\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}\Mod^{\mathrm{cn}}

The
divided power cotangent complex functor

\underline{L}^{\mathrm{pd}}:\Fun(\Delta^{1},\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}})\rightarrow\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}\Mod^{\mathrm{cn}}

is the unique sifted-colimit-preserving
extension of the functor sending

A\rightarrow B
in
\ArrPDPoly
to the pair
(B,\Omega^{1,\mathrm{pd}}_{B/A})
.

Remark 4.25
.

As in
Notation

2.12
,
we see that the composition

\Fun(\Delta^{1},\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}})\xrightarrow{\underline{L}^{\mathrm{pd}}}\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}\Mod^{\mathrm{cn}}\xrightarrow{}\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}

sends
A\rightarrow B
to
B
.
Hence, we can write
\underline{L}^{\mathrm{pd}}_{B/A}=(B,L_{B/A}^{\mathrm{pd}})
for some
L_{B/A}^{\mathrm{pd}}\in\Mod_{B}^{\mathrm{cn}}
.

Definition 4.26

(Hurewicz Map)
.

Given a morphism
A\rightarrow B
in
\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}
, there is a canonical map
B\rightarrow L_{B/A}^{\mathrm{pd}}
, the
universal derivation
, together with a nullhomotopy of the composite
A\rightarrow B\rightarrow L_{B/A}^{\mathrm{pd}}
. Both are constructed by extending from
\ArrPDPoly
under sifted colimits. The
Hurewicz map
is the induced map of
A
-modules
B/A\rightarrow L_{B/A}^{\mathrm{pd}}
, where
B/A
denotes the cofibre of
A\rightarrow B
.

Proposition 4.27
.

If
A\rightarrow B
is a map of animated rings, interpreted as a map of animated divided power rings, then
{L}_{B/A}^{\mathrm{pd}}\simeq L_{B/A}
.

Proof.

By construction, the composition

\Fun(\Delta^{1},\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}})\hookrightarrow\Fun(\Delta^{1},\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}})\overset{\underline{L}^{\mathrm{pd}}}{\rightarrow}\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}\Mod^{\mathrm{cn}}\rightarrow\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}\Mod^{\mathrm{cn}}

agrees with
\underline{L}
on
\ArrPoly
and both functors preserve sifted colimits.
∎

Proposition 4.28
.

The functor
\underline{L}^{\mathrm{pd}}
preserves small colimits.

Proof.

It suffices to show that the functor preserves finite coproducts in
\ArrPDPoly
. This follows from an explicit computation using
Example

4.21
.
∎

As observed by Mao (see
[
47
, Examples 2.32,2.25]
),

Proposition

2.19
and
Proposition

2.20
admit the following immediate generalisations:

Corollary 4.29

(Transitivity sequence)
.

Given a diagram
A\rightarrow B\rightarrow C
of animated divided power rings, there is a cofibre sequence in
\Mod_{C}
:

C\otimes_{B}L_{B/A}^{\mathrm{pd}}\rightarrow L_{C/A}^{\mathrm{pd}}\rightarrow L_{C/B}^{\mathrm{pd}}.

Corollary 4.30

(Base change)
.

Let

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ulcorner}

be a pushout square in
\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}
. Then following natural map is an equivalence:

A^{\prime}\otimes_{A}L_{B/A}^{\mathrm{pd}}\rightarrow L_{B^{\prime}/A^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{pd}}.

Together with
Proposition

2.21
, these two propositions imply:

Proposition 4.31

(Localisation)
.

Given
A\in\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}
and
f\in\pi_{0}(A)
, we have
L_{A[f^{-1}]/A}=0,
where

A[f^{-1}]=A_{\mathrm{triv}}[f^{-1}]\otimes_{A_{\mathrm{triv}}}A
.

We exhibit a right adjoint to the functor
\underline{L}^{\mathrm{pd}}
, analogous to
Proposition

2.14
.

Definition 4.32

(Divided power trivial square-zero functor)
.

The functor

\underline{\sqz}^{\mathrm{pd}}\colon\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}\Mod^{\mathrm{cn}}\rightarrow\Fun(\Delta^{1},\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}})

is the sifted-colimit-preserving extension of the functor that maps a pair consisting of
(R,I,\gamma)
in
\PDPoly
and
M
a finite free
R
-module to

(R,I,\gamma)\rightarrow(R\oplus M,I\oplus M,\gamma^{\prime}),

where the multiplication vanishes on
M
, and the divided powers are given by

\gamma^{\prime}_{n}(x+y):=\gamma_{n}(x)+\gamma_{n-1}(x)y,\ \ x\in I,y\in M.

We write
\underline{\sqz}^{\mathrm{pd}}(R,M)=(R\rightarrow\sqz_{R}^{\mathrm{pd}}M)
.

Proposition 4.33
.

The functor
\underline{\sqz}^{\mathrm{pd}}
is a right adjoint to
\underline{L}^{\mathrm{pd}}
.

Proof.

The proof is completely analogous to the proof of
Proposition

2.14
. For every map
A\rightarrow B
of (discrete) divided power rings, we have a commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sqz_{B}^{\mathrm{pd}}(\Omega^{1,\mathrm{pd}}_{B/A})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota+d}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota}

of divided power rings, where
\sqz_{B}^{\mathrm{pd}}(\Omega^{1,\mathrm{pd}}_{B/A})=B\oplus\Omega^{1,\mathrm{pd}}_{B/A}
has the divided power structure used in
Definition

4.32
.
For
A\rightarrow B
in
\ArrPDPoly
, the vertical maps in the square
define a map

\underline{u}\colon(A\rightarrow B)\rightarrow\underline{\sqz}^{\mathrm{pd}}(\underline{L}^{\mathrm{pd}}_{B/A}),

functorial in
A\rightarrow B
, and extending by sifted colimits, we obtain a transformation
\id\rightarrow\underline{\sqz}^{\mathrm{pd}}\circ\underline{L}^{\mathrm{pd}}
defined on all of
\Fun(\Delta^{1},\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}})
.

It now suffices to show for all
A\rightarrow B
in
\Fun(\Delta^{1},\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}})
and
(R,M)
in
\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}\Mod^{\mathrm{cn}}
the induced map

\varphi\colon\Map\big(\underline{L}_{B/A},\,(R,M)\big)\xrightarrow{\underline{u}\circ\underline{\sqz}^{\mathrm{pd}}}\Map\big((A\rightarrow B),\,\underline{\sqz}^{\mathrm{pd}}(R,M)\big)

is an equivalence. First assume that
A\rightarrow B
is of the form

\Gamma_{\mathbf{Z}[X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}]}(Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{m})\longrightarrow\Gamma_{\mathbf{Z}[X_{1},\ldots,X_{n+n^{\prime}}]}(Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{m+m^{\prime}}),

and also that
(R,I,\gamma)
belongs to
\PDPoly
and that
M
is finite free. By
Example

4.21
, we have
\Omega^{1,\mathrm{pd}}_{B/A}\simeq B^{n^{\prime}+m^{\prime}}
, and the source of
\varphi
is the discrete space

R^{n+n^{\prime}}\times I^{m+m^{\prime}}\times M^{n^{\prime}+m^{\prime}}.

Similarly, the target computes to the discrete space

R^{n}\times I^{m}\times(R\oplus M)^{n^{\prime}}\times(I\oplus M)^{m^{\prime}}.

Chasing the definitions, we see that
\varphi
corresponds to the obvious bijection. It follows as in
Proposition

2.14
that
\varphi
is an equivalence
for all
A\rightarrow B
and
(R,M)
.
∎

Notation 4.34
.

Denote the counit of the above adjunction by
\underline{v}:\underline{L}^{\mathrm{pd}}\circ\underline{\sqz}^{\mathrm{pd}}\rightarrow\id
. As in
Remark

2.16
, its first component
on some
(B,M)\in\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}\Mod^{\mathrm{cn}}

is the augmentation
\epsilon:\sqz^{\mathrm{pd}}_{B}(M)\rightarrow B
. We write
v\colon L_{\sqz^{\mathrm{pd}}_{B}(M)/A}^{\mathrm{pd}}\rightarrow\epsilon^{\ast}M
for the map in
\Mod_{\sqz^{\mathrm{pd}}_{B}(M)}
induced by the second component of the counit.

With the same proof as in
Corollary

2.18
, we obtain:

Corollary 4.35
.

Given
A\rightarrow B
in
\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}
and
M\in\Mod_{B}^{\mathrm{cn}}
, there is an equivalence

\Map_{\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}_{A//B}}(B,\,\sqz^{\mathrm{pd}}_{B}(M))\simeq\Map_{B}(L^{\mathrm{pd}}_{B/A},\,M)

sending

\phi:L^{\mathrm{pd}}_{B/A}\rightarrow M
to the composite
B\xrightarrow{\id\oplus d}\sqz^{\mathrm{pd}}_{B}(L^{\mathrm{pd}}_{B/A})\xrightarrow{\sqz(\phi)}\sqz^{\mathrm{pd}}_{B}(M)
and

s:B\rightarrow\sqz^{\mathrm{pd}}_{B}(M)
to

L^{\mathrm{pd}}_{B/A}\rightarrow s^{\ast}L^{\mathrm{pd}}_{\sqz^{\mathrm{pd}}_{B}(M)/A}\rightarrow s^{\ast}L^{\mathrm{pd}}_{\sqz^{\mathrm{pd}}_{B}(M)/B}\rightarrow s^{\ast}\epsilon^{\ast}M\simeq M
.

4.5.
Cotangent complex of sheaves of divided power rings

Let
\mathscr{T}
be a
small
\infty
-category with a Grothendieck topology.
Proceeding as in §
2.7
, we
obtain a functor

\underline{L}^{\mathrm{pd}}\colon\Fun(\Delta^{1},\Shv(\mathscr{T},\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}))\rightarrow\Shv(\mathscr{T},\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}\Mod),

and we have
\underline{L}^{\mathrm{pd}}_{\mathcal{B}/\mathcal{A}}=(\mathcal{B},L^{\mathrm{pd}}_{\mathcal{B}/\mathcal{A}})
. Completely analogously to
Proposition

2.31
, we obtain the following sheafy version of
Corollary

4.35
(using analogous notation):

Proposition 4.36
.

Let
\mathcal{A}\rightarrow\mathcal{B}
be a map in
\Shv(\mathscr{T},\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}})
, and
\mathcal{M}\in\Mod^{\mathrm{cn}}_{\mathcal{B}}
, there is an equivalence

\Map_{\Shv(\mathscr{T},\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}})_{\mathcal{A}//\mathcal{B}}}\big(\mathcal{B},\sqz^{\mathrm{pd}}_{\mathcal{B}}(\mathcal{M})\big)\simeq\Map_{{\mathcal{B}}}(L^{\mathrm{pd}}_{\mathcal{B}/\mathcal{A}},\,\mathcal{M})

sending

\phi:L_{\mathcal{B}/\mathcal{A}}^{\mathrm{pd}}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}
to the composite
\mathcal{B}\xrightarrow{\id\oplus d}\sqz^{\mathrm{pd}}_{\mathcal{B}}(L^{\mathrm{pd}}_{\mathcal{B}/\mathcal{A}})\xrightarrow{\sqz(\phi)}\sqz^{\mathrm{pd}}_{\mathcal{B}}(\mathcal{M})
and

s:\mathcal{B}\rightarrow\sqz_{\mathcal{B}}^{\mathrm{pd}}(\mathcal{M})
to

L^{\mathrm{pd}}_{\mathcal{B}/\mathcal{A}}\rightarrow s^{\ast}L^{\mathrm{pd}}_{\sqz^{\mathrm{pd}}_{\mathcal{B}}(\mathcal{M})/\mathcal{A}}\rightarrow s^{\ast}L^{\mathrm{pd}}_{\sqz^{\mathrm{pd}}_{\mathcal{B}}(\mathcal{M})/\mathcal{B}}\rightarrow s^{\ast}\epsilon^{\ast}\mathcal{M}\simeq\mathcal{M}
.

Notation 4.37
.

Given a map
A\rightarrow B
in
\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}
, write
\mathcal{O}_{B}
for the
\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}_{A}
-valued sheaf on
\Spec(B)
sending a distinguished affine
U_{f}
to
B[f^{-1}]
, cf.
Corollary

2.26
.

We proceed as in
Proposition

2.41
to check consistency with
Definition

4.24
:

Proposition 4.38
.

Fix a map of animated divided power rings
A\rightarrow B
. Then
L^{\mathrm{pd}}_{\mathcal{O}_{B}/A}
is the quasi-coherent sheaf on
\Spec(B)
with global sections

L^{\mathrm{pd}}_{B/A}
.

4.6.
Divided power cotangent complex of a surjective map

Let
I\subset R
be an ideal in a ring and
\gamma
a divided power structure on
I
. Given
n\geq 1
, write
I^{[n]}
for the ideal generated by all divided powers
\gamma_{m}(x)
with
x\in I
and
m\geq n
.

Proposition 4.39
.

Let
A\rightarrow B
be a surjective map in
\PDRing
such that the induced map
A/I_{A}\xrightarrow{\cong}B/I_{B}
is an isomorphism. Denote the kernel of
A\rightarrow B
by
I
.

Then
\pi_{0}L_{B/A}^{\mathrm{pd}}\cong 0
and
\pi_{1}L_{B/A}^{\mathrm{pd}}\cong I/I^{[2]}
, functorially in
A\rightarrow B
.

Moreover, the map
I=\pi_{1}(B/A)\rightarrow\pi_{1}L_{B/A}^{\mathrm{pd}}\cong I/I^{[2]}

induced by the Hurewicz map
(see
Definition

4.26
) is given by projection.

The rest of this subsection is dedicated to the proof of
Proposition

4.39
.

Notation 4.40
.

Given a divided power ring
A=(A,I_{A},\gamma)
, we write
\PDRing^{\mathrm{aug}}_{A}
for the category of divided power algebras
A\rightarrow B
such that
A/I_{A}\rightarrow B/I_{B}
is an isomorphism. More generally, given an animated divided power ring
A\in\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}
, we write
\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd},\mathrm{aug}}_{A}
for the
\infty
-category of maps of animated divided power rings
A\rightarrow B
such that the induced map
\overline{A}\rightarrow\overline{B}
is an equivalence.

Given an animated divided power ring
A\in\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}
, the composite

R:\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd},\mathrm{aug}}_{A}\xrightarrow{I}\Mod_{\mathbf{Z}}^{\mathrm{cn}}\xrightarrow{\Omega^{\infty}}\mathscr{S}

is conservative and moreover preserves small limits and sifted colimits.
It therefore admits a left adjoint
L
, and by the Barr–Beck–Lurie theorem
[
43
, 4.7.3.5]
, the resulting adjunction is in fact monadic. Let us denote the associated monad by
T=RL
. For every
B\in\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd},\mathrm{aug}}_{A}
, we obtain an augmented simplicial diagram

(7)

(\ \ldots\ \begin{subarray}{c}\longrightarrow\vskip-4.0pt\\
\leftarrow\vskip-4.0pt\ \\
\longrightarrow\vskip-4.0pt\\
\leftarrow\vskip-4.0pt\ \\
\longrightarrow\end{subarray}\ T(T(B))\ \begin{subarray}{c}\longrightarrow\vskip-4.0pt\\
\leftarrow\vskip-4.0pt\ \\
\longrightarrow\end{subarray}\ T(B))\rightarrow B

which exhibits
B
as a geometric realisation of objects in the image of
L
.

We will now describe this resolution more explicitly when
A
is discrete.

Construction 4.41
.

Let
A\in\PDRing
be a divided power ring.
Given a set
S
, we write
A\{S\}
for the free
A
-module with generators
\{s\in S\}
. We
then equip the ring
\Gamma_{A}(A\{S\})
(see
Example

4.2
) with the divided power ideal
I_{A}\oplus\Gamma^{+}_{A}(A\{S\})
, and the natural divided power structure
\gamma^{\prime}
given by

\gamma^{\prime}_{n}(x,y)=\bigg(\gamma_{n}(x),\,\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}\gamma_{i}(x)\gamma_{n-i}(y)\bigg)\ ,\ \ x\in I_{A},y\in\Gamma^{+}_{A}(A\{S\}).

Lemma 4.42
.

The functor
S\mapsto\Gamma_{A}(A\{S\})
is naturally equivalent to the composite

\Set\rightarrow\mathscr{S}\xrightarrow{L}\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd},\mathrm{aug}}_{A}.

Proof.

First, note that the functor
\Fin\rightarrow\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}
,
S\mapsto\Gamma_{\mathbf{Z}}(\mathbf{Z}\{S\})
preserves finite coproducts, where
\Fin
is the category of finite sets. Left Kan extending along
\Fin\rightarrow\mathscr{S}\simeq\mathscr{P}_{\Sigma}(\Fin)

gives the left adjoint of an
adjoint pair

\mathscr{S}\leftrightarrows\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}},

and the right adjoint sends
B\in\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}
to
\Map_{\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}}(\Gamma_{\mathbf{Z}}(\mathbf{Z}\{S\}),B)
, see e.g.
[
47
, Lemma 2.1]
.
But
\Map_{\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}}(\Gamma_{\mathbf{Z}}(\mathbf{Z}\{S\}),B)
is naturally equivalent to
\Map_{\mathscr{S}}(S,\Omega^{\infty}IB)
; this is checked by hand for
B\in\PDPoly
, and extends under sifted colimits as each
\Gamma_{\mathbf{Z}}(\mathbf{Z}\{S\})\in\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}
is compact projective.

To prove the result, we factor the right adjoint
R
as a composite of right adjoints

\CAlg_{A}^{\mathrm{pd},\mathrm{aug}}\subset\CAlg_{A}^{\mathrm{pd}}\rightarrow\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}\xrightarrow{B\mapsto\Omega^{\infty}I_{B}}\mathscr{S}.

Using that
\Gamma_{\mathbf{Z}}(\mathbf{Z}\{S\})
is a free
\mathbf{Z}
-module, we deduce that the left adjoint sends a finite set
S\in\Fin
to
A\otimes\Gamma_{\mathbf{Z}}(\mathbf{Z}\{S\})\simeq\Gamma_{A}(A\{S\})
. Extending under filtered colimits, we obtain the result for general sets.

∎

We prove an analogue of
[
62
, 08RA]

in the divided power setting:

Lemma 4.43
.

Let
A\rightarrow P\rightarrow B
be surjective maps in
\PDRing
, with
P\simeq\Gamma_{A}(M)
for
M=A\{S\}
a free
A
-module on a set
S
.
Assume that
A/I_{A}\rightarrow B/I_{B}
is an isomorphism. Denote the kernel of
A\rightarrow B
by
I
, and the kernel of
P\rightarrow B
by
J
. Then the universal divided power derivation
d\colon P\rightarrow\Omega^{1,\mathrm{pd}}_{P/A}
induces a short exact sequence of

B
-modules:

0\rightarrow I/I^{[2]}\rightarrow J/J^{[2]}\xrightarrow{[x]\mapsto 1\otimes dx}B\otimes_{P}\Omega^{1,\mathrm{pd}}_{P/A}\rightarrow 0.

Proof.

Given
s\in S
, we write
x_{s}\in P
for the corresponding generator.

By the surjectivity assumption, we can pick an element
\lambda_{s}\in A
whose image under
A\rightarrow B
agrees with the image of
x_{s}
under
P\rightarrow B
.
Hence
(A\rightarrow P\rightarrow B)
is isomorphic to
(A\rightarrow P\xrightarrow{x_{s}\mapsto 0}B)
via the isomorphism
P\rightarrow P
sending
x_{s}
to
x_{s}+\lambda_{s}
, and we may assume without restriction that

P\rightarrow B
sends each
x_{s}
to zero.

Note that
\Omega^{1,\mathrm{pd}}_{P/A}\cong P\otimes_{A}M
.
We also observe that
J^{[2]}=I^{[2]}\oplus IM\oplus\Gamma^{\geq 2}_{A}(M)

inside

J=I\oplus M\oplus\Gamma^{\geq 2}_{A}(M).

Hence the sequence in the lemma is isomorphic to

0\rightarrow I/I^{[2]}\rightarrow I/I^{[2]}\oplus(B\otimes_{A}M)\rightarrow B\otimes_{A}M\rightarrow 0.\vskip-14.0pt

∎

Proof of
Proposition

4.39
.

Given
B\in\PDRing^{\mathrm{aug}}_{A}
,
Lemma

4.42
shows that each term in the monadic resolution
P_{\bullet}\rightarrow B
of
B
in (
7
) is of the form
\Gamma_{A}(M)
for
M=A\{S\}
a free
A
-module.

Lemma

4.43
gives a short exact sequence

(8)

0\rightarrow I/I^{[2]}\rightarrow J_{\bullet}/J_{\bullet}^{[2]}\rightarrow B\otimes_{P_{\bullet}}\Omega^{1,\mathrm{pd}}_{P_{\bullet}/A}\rightarrow 0

of simplicial
B
-modules. To prove
\pi_{0}(L_{B/A}^{\mathrm{pd}})\cong 0
and

\pi_{1}(L_{B/A}^{\mathrm{pd}})\cong I/I^{[2]}
,
it suffices to show that the geometric realisation
|J_{\bullet}/J_{\bullet}^{[2]}|
is
2
-connective.

First, note that
|J_{\bullet}|\simeq 0
as
|P_{\bullet}|\rightarrow B
is an equivalence; in particular
\pi_{0}(|J_{\bullet}|)\cong 0
.

Applying the normalised chain complex functor to
J_{\bullet}
, we deduce that the map
d_{1}:\ker(d_{0}:J_{1}\rightarrow J_{0})\rightarrow J_{0}
is surjective.
Using
Example

4.2
, this shows that

\Gamma_{A}^{\geq 2}(d_{1}):\ker(\Gamma_{A}^{\geq 2}(d_{0}):\Gamma_{A}^{\geq 2}(J_{1})\rightarrow\Gamma_{A}^{\geq 2}(J_{0}))\xrightarrow{\ \ \ \ }\Gamma_{A}^{\geq 2}(J_{0})

is also surjective, which in turn implies that
\pi_{0}(|\Gamma_{A}^{\geq 2}(J_{\bullet})|)\cong 0
.

But for each
n
, we have a surjection
\Gamma_{A}^{\geq 2}J_{n}\rightarrow J_{n}^{[2]}
. Denoting its kernel by
K_{n}
, we obtain a short exact sequence of simplicial
A
-modules

0\rightarrow K_{\bullet}\rightarrow\Gamma_{A}^{\geq 2}J_{\bullet}\rightarrow J_{\bullet}^{[2]}\rightarrow 0.

Using the associated long exact sequence, we deduce that

\pi_{0}|J_{\bullet}^{[2]}|\cong 0
.

Using the
long exact sequence associated to

0\rightarrow J^{[2]}_{\bullet}\rightarrow J_{\bullet}\rightarrow J/J^{[2]}_{\bullet}\rightarrow 0,

we conclude from
|J_{\bullet}|\simeq 0
and
\pi_{0}|J_{\bullet}^{[2]}|\cong 0

that
|J/J^{[2]}|
is
2
-connective.

The final claim about compatibility with projection follows from a diagram chase as (
8
) receives a map from the short exact sequence
0\rightarrow I\rightarrow J_{\bullet}\rightarrow P_{\bullet}/A\rightarrow 0
.
∎

5.
Derived divided power de Rham complex

In this section, we define the derived de Rham cohomology of divided power rings and compare it with crystalline cohomology, following Mao
[
47
]

and Bhatt
[
7
]
.

We will set out the theory only in the level of generality needed for our main theorems; a more comprehensive study of the derived de Rham cohomology of derived divided power schemes will appear in
[
10
]
.

5.1.
Definition and basic properties

In general, derived de Rham cohomology
can behave badly,
and we cannot control it via the cotangent complex as the
Hodge filtration need not be complete.
We obtain a nicer theory by either completing with respect to the Hodge filtration or working in a
p
-completed setting, where
the Cartier isomorphism is at our disposal. The former approach is more suitable in characteristic zero, whereas the latter provides a well-behaved theory in the mixed characteristic setting of interest to us.

So let us fix a prime
p
and work in a
p
-completed setting.

Notation 5.1
.

Given an animated ring
A\in\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}
,
write

\Mod_{A}^{\wedge}\subset\Mod_{A}

for the full subcategory spanned by all (derived)
p
-complete
A
-modules, i.e. those
M
for which the morphism
M\rightarrow\lim_{n}(M/p^{n})
is an equivalence (see

[
45
, Section 7.3]
). The inclusion
\Mod_{A}^{\wedge}\subset\Mod_{A}
admits a left adjoint, the (derived) completion functor
(-)^{\wedge}
, and

[
45
, Corollary 7.3.5.2]
shows that there is an essentially unique symmetric monoidal structure
{\otimes}_{A}
on
\Mod_{A}^{\wedge}
for which
(-)^{\wedge}
is symmetric monoidal.

Let
\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}\Mod^{\wedge}\subset\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}\Mod
be the full subcategory spanned by all
(A,B)
for which
B\in\Mod_{A}^{\wedge}
is
p
-complete.

Convention 5.2
.

In Sections 5-7, we will often suppress the completion functor
(-)^{\wedge}
on objects from our notation,
denoting
M\in\Mod_{A}
and
M^{\wedge}\in\Mod_{A}^{\wedge}
by the same name.
We will, however, carefully distinguish between
\Mod_{A}
and
\Mod_{A}^{\wedge}
.

Remark 5.3
.

If
\pi_{0}(A)
is a local Artinian ring with residue field
k
of characteristic
p
, then every
A
-module is automatically
p
-complete since
p
is nilpotent.
Completing at
p
is, however, a nontrivial operation over bases like
W(k)
, where it ‘corrects’ pathological behaviours exhibited by the uncompleted derived de Rham complex.

Definition 5.4

(Filtered
p
-complete modules)
.

Let
\mathbf{N}
be the partially ordered set of non-negative integers.
We consider the
\infty
-category

\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}\Mod^{\wedge\mathrm{fil}}:=\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}\times_{\Fun(\mathbf{N}^{\mathrm{op}},\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}})}\Fun(\mathbf{N}^{\mathrm{op}},\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}\Mod^{\wedge})

of pairs
(A,M)
with
A
in
\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}
and

M=\big[\cdots\rightarrow\Fil^{1}M\rightarrow\Fil^{0}M\ ]\in\Mod_{A}^{\wedge\mathrm{fil}}:=\Fun(\mathbf{N}^{\mathrm{op}},\Mod^{\wedge}_{A}).

Definition 5.5

(de Rham complex of classical divided power rings)
.

Let
A\rightarrow B
be a morphism of classical divided power rings. Given
n\geq 0
, set
\Omega^{n,\mathrm{pd}}_{B/A}:=\Lambda^{n}_{B}\Omega^{1,\mathrm{pd}}_{B/A}
.
The (uncompleted)
de Rham complex
of
A\rightarrow B
is the chain complex of
A
-modules

\Omega^{\bullet,\mathrm{pd}}_{B/A}=\left[\cdots\rightarrow 0\rightarrow B\overset{d}{\rightarrow}\Omega^{1,\mathrm{pd}}_{B/A}\overset{d}{\rightarrow}\Omega^{2,\mathrm{pd}}_{B/A}\overset{d}{\rightarrow}\cdots\right]

with the usual de Rham differential

d(f_{0}\wedge df_{1}\wedge\ldots\wedge df_{n})=df_{0}\wedge df_{1}\wedge\ldots\wedge df_{n}.

We equip this complex with the
Hodge filtration
, whose
n^{th}
piece
\Fil^{m}\Omega^{\bullet,\mathrm{pd}}
is

\left[\ 0\rightarrow\cdots 0\rightarrow\Omega^{m,\mathrm{pd}}_{B/A}\rightarrow\Omega^{m+1,\mathrm{pd}}_{B/A}\rightarrow\cdots\right].

The filtered chain complex
\Omega^{\bullet,\mathrm{pd}}_{B/A}
is functorial in the morphism
A\rightarrow B
.

Definition 5.6

(Derived de Rham complex for animated divided power rings
[
47
]
)
.

The (
p
-completed)
Hodge-filtered de Rham complex

\underline{\dR}^{\mathrm{pd}}\colon\Fun(\Delta^{1},\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}})\rightarrow\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}\Mod^{\wedge\mathrm{fil}}

is the unique sifted-colimit-preserving extension of the composition

\ArrPDPoly\xrightarrow{}\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}\Mod^{\mathrm{fil}}\rightarrow\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}\Mod^{\wedge\mathrm{fil}}

of the functor
(A\rightarrow B)\mapsto(A,\Omega^{\bullet,\mathrm{pd}}_{B/A})
in
Definition

5.5
and the
p
-completion functor.

Remark 5.7
.

As in
Remark

4.25
, we check that the composition

\Fun(\Delta^{1},\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}})\rightarrow\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}\Mod^{\wedge\mathrm{fil}}\rightarrow\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}

sends
A\rightarrow B
to
A
. We can therefore write

\underline{\dR}^{\mathrm{pd}}_{B/A}=(A,\dR_{B/A}^{\mathrm{pd}})
.

Convention 5.8
.

We denote the Hodge filtration on
\dR_{B/A}^{\mathrm{pd}}
by
\Fil^{\bullet}\dR_{B/A}^{\mathrm{pd}}
.
We will sometimes suppress the functor
\Fil^{0}
on objects from our notation,
but will carefully distinguish between
\Mod_{A}^{\wedge\mathrm{fil}}
and
\Mod_{A}^{\wedge}
.

Warning 5.9
.

In
[
47
]
, the above filtration is referred to as the ‘stupid filtration’ (and the term ‘Hodge filtration’ is reserved for something else). Moreover, note that the symbol
\dR_{B/A}^{\mathrm{pd}}
in
[
47
]
stands for the
un-
p
-completed
derived de Rham complex.

Proceeding as in the proof of
Proposition

4.27
, we obtain:

Proposition 5.10
.

If
A\rightarrow B
is a map of animated rings (seen as animated divided power rings), then
\dR_{B/A}^{\mathrm{pd}}
agrees with
Bhatt’s
p
-completed variant of Illusie’s
Hodge-filtered derived de Rham cohomology
\dR_{B/A}
(cf.
[
7
, Definition 2.1]
,
[
31
, VIII.2]
).

Proposition 5.11

(Base change)
.

Every pushout square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ulcorner}

in
\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}
induces a natural equivalence
A^{\prime}\ {\otimes}_{A}\dR_{B/A}^{\mathrm{pd}}\rightarrow\dR_{B^{\prime}/A^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{pd}}
in
\Mod_{A^{\prime}}^{\wedge\mathrm{fil}}
.

Proof.

The
\infty
-category of such pushout squares is generated under sifted colimits by those diagrams for which both
A\rightarrow A^{\prime}
and
A\rightarrow B
belong to
\ArrPDPoly
(it then follows that
A^{\prime}\rightarrow B^{\prime}
and
B\rightarrow B^{\prime}
also lie in
\ArrPDPoly
). On such diagrams, the claim follows from an explicit computation. Since everything in sight preserves sifted colimits, the proposition follows.
Alternatively, the proposition follows from
[
47
, Lemma 4.12]
and
[
47
, Example 2.25]
, see also
Corollary

4.30
.
∎

Applying this to the construction of
Definition

4.17
, we obtain:

Corollary 5.12
.

For
A\in\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}
and
A\rightarrow B
a map in
\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}
, the natural map

\dR_{B/A_{\mathrm{triv}}}^{\mathrm{pd}}\rightarrow\dR^{\mathrm{pd}}_{\ind_{A}(B)/A}

is an equivalence in
\Mod_{A}^{\wedge\mathrm{fil}}
.

Definition 5.13

(Derived exterior powers)
.

Let

\Lambda^{n}\colon\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}\Mod^{\mathrm{cn}}\rightarrow\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}\Mod^{\mathrm{cn}}

be the unique sifted-colimit-preserving extension of the functor that maps a pair
(A,M)
in
\PolyProj
to
(A,\Lambda^{n}_{A}M)
. We write
\Lambda^{n}(A,M)=(A,\Lambda^{n}_{A}M)
.

Remark 5.14
.

By
[
11
, Section 3]
, this can be extended to non-connective modules.

Using
Convention

5.2
as always, we have:

Proposition 5.15

(Associated graded of the Hodge filtration)
.

Given a map of animated divided power rings
A\rightarrow B
, there is an equivalence

\mathrm{gr}^{n}\dR_{B/A}^{\mathrm{pd}}\simeq\Lambda^{n}(L_{B/A}^{\mathrm{pd}})[-n]

in
\Mod_{A}^{\wedge}
. This defines an equivalence of functors

\Fun(\Delta^{1},\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}})\rightarrow\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}\Mod^{\wedge}
.

Proof.

Both functors preserve sifted colimits, and they agree on
\ArrPDPoly
.
∎

In particular, we have maps
\pi^{0}\colon\dR_{B/A}^{\mathrm{pd}}\rightarrow B
and
\pi^{1}\colon\Fil^{1}\dR_{B/A}^{\mathrm{pd}}\rightarrow L_{B/A}^{\mathrm{pd}}[-1]
.

Proposition 5.16
.

There is a map of fibre sequences

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Fil^{1}\dR_{B/A}^{\mathrm{pd}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dR_{B/A}^{\mathrm{pd}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{B/A}^{\mathrm{pd}}[-1]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{B/A}^{\mathrm{pd}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d}

in
\Mod_{A}^{\wedge}
, functorial in
A\rightarrow B
in
\Fun(\Delta^{1},\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}})
.

Proof.

For
A\rightarrow B
in
\ArrPDPoly
, we construct this diagram by hand, using that the differential
d\colon B\rightarrow\Omega^{1,\mathrm{pd}}_{B/A}
in the cochain complex
\Omega^{\bullet,\mathrm{pd}}_{B/A}
is the universal divided power derivation
B\rightarrow\Omega^{1,\mathrm{pd}}_{B/A}
. We then extend under sifted colimits.
∎

The de Rham complex
in
Definition

5.6
admits a multiplicative refinement

\dR^{\mathrm{pd}}:\Fun(\Delta^{1},\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}})\rightarrow\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}\CAlg^{\wedge\mathrm{fil}}_{\mathbf{Z}_{p}},

where
\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}\CAlg^{\wedge\mathrm{fil}}_{\mathbf{Z}_{p}}\subset\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}\times_{\CAlg}\Fun(\Delta^{1},\CAlg_{\mathbf{Z}}^{\mathrm{fil}})
denotes the
full subcategory spanned by pairs
(A,B)
of an animated ring
A
and a
p
-complete filtered
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-
A
-algebra
B
.
We first construct this functor on objects in
\ArrPDPoly
using the wedge product of forms, and then extend under sifted colimits as usual.

We will give a more refined treatment of multiplicative refinements in
[
10
]
. All we need in this paper is the following result, which follows from
[
47
, Lemma 4.12]
:

Proposition 5.17
.

The functor
\dR^{\mathrm{pd}}:\Fun(\Delta^{1},\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}})\rightarrow\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}\CAlg^{\wedge\mathrm{fil}}_{\mathbf{Z}_{p}}
preserves small colimits.

5.2.
Global version

Let
\mathscr{T}
be a Grothendieck site. Then the divided power de Rham functor induces a functor

\underline{\dR}^{\mathrm{pd}}\colon\Fun(\Delta^{1},\Shv(\mathscr{T},\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}))\rightarrow\Shv(\mathscr{T},\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}\Mod).

The image of
\underline{\dR}_{\mathcal{B}/\mathcal{A}}^{\mathrm{pd}}
in
\Shv(\mathscr{T},\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}})
is
\mathcal{A}
, and we can write
\underline{\dR}_{\mathcal{B}/\mathcal{A}}^{\mathrm{pd}}=(\mathcal{A},\dR_{\mathcal{B}/\mathcal{A}}^{\mathrm{pd}})
.

Notation 5.18

(
\dR^{\mathrm{pd}}_{X/A}
)
.

Let
A
be an animated divided power ring. Given a
pair
X=(\mathscr{T},\mathcal{O}_{X})
of a small Grothendieck site
\mathscr{T}
and a sheaf
\mathcal{O}_{X}
in
\Shv(\mathscr{T},\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}_{A})
, we set
\dR^{\mathrm{pd}}_{X/A}:=\dR^{\mathrm{pd}}_{\mathcal{O}_{X}/A}
.

Using
Notation

4.37
, we check that this is consistent with
Definition

5.6
:

Proposition 5.19
.

Fix a map
A\rightarrow B
in
\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}
,
and write
q:\Spec(B)\rightarrow\Spec(A)
for the induced map of affine derived schemes. Then
q_{\ast}\dR^{\mathrm{pd}}_{\mathcal{O}_{B}/A}
is the quasi-coherent
p
-complete filtered
\mathcal{O}_{A}
-module with
global sections
\dR^{\mathrm{pd}}_{B/A}
.

To prove this result, the following lemma will be useful:

Lemma 5.20

(Cotangent criterion for de Rham complexes)
.

Fix maps
A\rightarrow A^{\prime}\rightarrow B
in
\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}
.
If
L_{\overline{A^{\prime}}/\overline{A}}\simeq 0
and

L^{\mathrm{pd}}_{A^{\prime}/A}\simeq 0
, then
\dR_{B/A}\xrightarrow{}\dR_{B/A^{\prime}}

is an equivalence.

Proof.

As
L^{\mathrm{pd}}_{A^{\prime}/A}\simeq 0
, the transitivity sequence
Corollary

4.29
and
Proposition

5.15
show that the map induces an equivalence on graded pieces of the Hodge filtration.

It therefore suffices to prove that
\Fil^{0}\dR_{B/A}\xrightarrow{}\Fil^{0}\dR_{B/A^{\prime}}
is an equivalence. As we work in a
p
-completed setting, it is by
Proposition

5.11

enough to check that

\Fil^{0}\dR_{B\otimes\mathbb{F}_{p}/A\otimes\mathbb{F}_{p}}\xrightarrow{}\Fil^{0}\dR_{B\otimes\mathbb{F}_{p}/A^{\prime}\otimes\mathbb{F}_{p}}

is an equivalence.
Domain and target admit compatible
exhaustive conjugate filtrations (
[
47
, 3.4]
).
The associated graded pieces of this filtration are a functor of the map
L_{\overline{B}/\overline{A}}\rightarrow L_{\overline{B}/\overline{A}^{\prime}}
, which is an equivalence as

L_{\overline{A^{\prime}}/\overline{A}}\simeq 0
by assumption.
∎

Proof of
Proposition

5.19
.

We first check that the
presheaf
U\mapsto\mathcal{F}(U):=\mathbf{\dR}_{\mathcal{O}_{B}(U)/A}
already satisfies the sheaf property on
distinguished affines
U_{1},\ldots,U_{n}\subset\Spec(B)
, i.e. that for
U=\cup_{i=1}^{n}U_{i}
, the following map is an equivalence:

(9)

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}\left(U\right)\rightarrow\lim_{\emptyset\neq S\subset\{1,\ldots,n\}}\mathcal{F}\left(\cap_{i\in S}U_{i}\right).

The equivalence on graded pieces of the Hodge filtration follows from

Proposition

5.15
, Proposition 25.2.3.1 in
[
45
]
, and
Proposition

4.38
.

By
p
-completeness, it is enough to check
the equivalence for
\mathcal{F}^{0}\simeq\Fil^{0}\mathbf{\dR}_{\mathcal{O}_{B}(-)/A}
after applying
\mathbb{F}_{p}\otimes(-)
. Here, it follows by observing that for a distinguished affine open
U_{f}
, we can identify
\mathbb{F}_{p}\otimes\Fil^{0}\mathbf{\dR}_{\mathcal{O}_{B}(U_{f})/A}\simeq\Fil^{0}\mathbf{\dR}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}\otimes B[f^{-1}]/\mathbb{F}_{p}\otimes A}
with

\phi^{\ast}_{A\twoheadrightarrow\overline{A}}(\mathbb{F}_{p}\otimes\overline{B}[f^{-1}])\otimes_{\phi^{\ast}_{A\twoheadrightarrow\overline{A}}(\mathbb{F}_{p}\otimes\overline{B})}\Fil^{0}\dR_{\mathbb{F}_{p}\otimes B/\mathbb{F}_{p}\otimes A},

where the maps
\phi^{\ast}
are defined as in
[
47
, Section 4.3]
. Indeed, this is readily checked on associated gradeds of the (exhaustive) conjugate filtration.

Corollary

2.26
therefore shows that sheafification does not change the value on distinguished affines, and we obtain an equivalence

\dR_{\mathcal{O}_{B}/A}(U_{f})\simeq\mathbf{\dR}_{B[f^{-1}]/A}
.

To prove that
q_{\ast}\dR_{\mathcal{O}_{B}/A}
is quasi-coherent on
\Spec(A)
,
fix an element
g\in\pi_{0}(A)
and write
\overline{g}\in\pi_{0}(B)
for the image of
g
under
\pi_{0}(A)\rightarrow\pi_{0}(B)
.
Using
Proposition

5.11
and the equivalence
\mathcal{O}_{B}(U_{\overline{g}})\simeq B[\overline{g}^{-1}]\simeq B\otimes_{A}A[g^{-1}]
, we compute

q_{\ast}\dR_{\mathcal{O}_{B}/A}(U_{g})\simeq\dR_{\mathcal{O}_{B}/A}(U_{\overline{g}})\simeq\dR_{B\otimes_{A}A[{g}^{-1}]/A}\simeq\dR_{B/A}\otimes_{A}A[{g}^{-1}].

Quasi-coherence then follows from
Proposition

2.40
.
∎

5.3.
Comparison with de Rham and crystalline cohomology

We begin by recalling the following result (see
[
7
, Corollary 3.10]
):

Proposition 5.21

(Bhatt)
.

If
A\rightarrow B
is a smooth map of discrete commutative

\mathbf{Z}/p^{n}
-algebras, then
\dR_{B/A}^{\mathrm{pd}}\simeq\dR_{B/A}\simeq\Omega^{\bullet}_{B/A}
in
\Mod_{A}^{\wedge\mathrm{fil}}
.

Next, we state a remarkable ‘crystalline’ property of the functor
\dR
, due to Mao. Recall from
Notation

4.10
that for any animated divided power ring
B
, we have an animated ring
\overline{B}
. Interpreting
\overline{B}
as an object of
\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}
(equipped with the trivial divided power ideal), we obtain a map
B\rightarrow\overline{B}
in
\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}
. This defines a functor

\Fun(\Delta^{1},\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}})\rightarrow\Fun(\Delta^{1},\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}),\,(A\rightarrow B)\mapsto(A\rightarrow\overline{B}).

More formally, this can be defined as the sifted-colimit-preserving extension of the functor that maps
(A,I,\gamma)\rightarrow(B,J,\delta)
in
\ArrPDPoly
to
(A,I,\gamma)\rightarrow(B/J,0,0)
.
There is a natural map
(A\rightarrow B)\rightarrow(A\rightarrow\overline{B})
, functorial in
A\rightarrow B
.

Theorem 5.22

(
[
47
, 4.16]
)
.

For every map
A\rightarrow B
in
\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}
, the induced map

\dR_{B/A}^{\mathrm{pd}}\rightarrow\dR_{\overline{B}/A}^{\mathrm{pd}}

is an equivalence in
\Mod_{A}^{\wedge}
.

Warning 5.23
.

Theorem

5.22
is a statement about the unfiltered de Rham complex, and fails if we take Hodge filtrations into account.

Now let
k
be a perfect field of characteristic
p
, and denote by
W
the ring of Witt vectors of
k
. Equip
W
with the unique divided power structure on the ideal
pW
.

Definition 5.24

(Derived crystalline cohomology,
[
47
, Definition 4.17]
)
.

Derived crystalline cohomology
is the functor

\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{\mathrm{cris}}(-/W)\colon\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}_{k}\rightarrow\Mod_{W}^{\wedge}

defined as

\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}_{k}\hookrightarrow\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}_{k}\longrightarrow\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}_{W}\xrightarrow{\ \dR^{\mathrm{pd}}\ }\Mod_{W}^{\wedge},

where the middle map is obtained by precomposing with
W\rightarrow k
.

We then have the following comparison (see

[
47
, Proposition 4.87, 4.90]
):

Proposition 5.25
.

The restriction of
\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{\mathrm{cris}}(-/W)
to smooth
k
-algebras is isomorphic to the crystalline cohomology functor of Berthelot
[
6
]
.

We can globalise this result:

Proposition 5.26
.

If
X/k
is a smooth and separated scheme over
k
, then
\RGamma(X,\dR_{X/W}^{\mathrm{pd}})
is quasi-isomorphic to Berthelot’s
[
6
]
crystalline cohomology
\RGamma_{\mathrm{cris}}(X/W)
.

Proof.

The affine crystalline site
\Cris(X/W)
of
X
has objects

(10)

(U/S)=\left(\hbox to134.58pt{\vbox to84.79pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 67.28932pt\lower-42.39589pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{

{}{}}{}{}{{}{}}}}}{{}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.39998pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -18.01 -46.68 L 12.1 -46.68}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@transformcm{-1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-13.01633pt}{-33.73616pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform=matrix(-1.0 0.0 0.0 1.0 -18.01 -46.68)} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray=none,stroke-dashoffset=0.0pt} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin=miter} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linecap=round} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 2.71 C 0.95 2.71 1.72 2.1 1.72 1.36 C 1.72 0.61 0.95 0 0 0}{fill:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}{{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{8.94376pt}{-33.73616pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform=matrix(1.0 0.0 0.0 1.0 12.38 -46.68)} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray=none,stroke-dashoffset=0.0pt} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linecap=round} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin=round} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -2.88 3.32 C -2.35 1.33 -1.18 0.39 0 0 C -1.18 -0.39 -2.35 -1.33 -2.88 -3.32}{fill:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\right).

Here
(A,I,\gamma)
is a divided power ring under
W
with
\overline{A}=A/I
, the underlying map of topological spaces of

\Spec(\overline{A})\rightarrow\Spec(A)
is a homeomorphism, and

\Spec(\overline{A})\subset X
is a Zariski open subscheme.
The
0^{th}
piece of the Hodge filtration gives a map

(11)

(U/S)\ \ \mapsto\ \ (\dR_{A/W}^{\mathrm{pd}}\rightarrow A)

of
\Mod_{W}^{\wedge}
-valued sheaves on
\Cris(X/W)
.

We have a natural map
u_{X/S}:\Cris(X/S)\rightarrow\Zar(X)
to the affine Zariski site, sending the diagram
(U/S)
as in (
10
) to
\Spec(\overline{A})\subset X
.
Right Kan extending along
u_{X/S}
gives a
functor

u_{X/S\ast}
from crystalline to Zariski sheaves. It sends
\mathcal{F}
to the sheaf

U\mapsto\lim_{V/S\in\Cris(U/S)^{\mathrm{op}}}\mathcal{F}(V)
.

By
Proposition

5.19
and
Theorem

5.22
, applying
u_{X/S\ast}
to (
11
) gives a map of sheaves on the affine Zariski site sending
U\subset X
to

\dR_{\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)/W}^{\mathrm{pd}}\xrightarrow{\simeq}\RGamma_{\mathrm{cris}}(U/W),
which is an equivalence by
Proposition

5.25
.
The result follows by separability.
∎

Remark 5.27
.

This result in fact holds without the separability assumption, and for locally quasi-syntomic morphisms of finite type; we refer to
[
10
]
for details.

6.
The period map

In this section, we will use deformations of the Hodge filtration to
construct a period map, and moreover compute its effect on tangent fibres. This will play a crucial role in the proof of
Theorem

A
, where it will enable us to ‘transport’
divided power unobstructedness from one formal moduli problem to another.

6.1.
Divided power formal moduli problems

Let
\Lambda
be a local Noetherian ring with residue field
k
and a chosen divided power structure on
its augmentation ideal.

Definition 6.1

(Artinian divided power algebras)
.

We call
A\in\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}_{\Lambda}

Artinian
if

(1)

the map
k\cong\overline{\Lambda}\rightarrow\overline{A}
is an equivalence;

(2)

the underlying object in
\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}_{\Lambda//k}
is Artinian (see
Definition

3.1
).

Let
\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd},\mathrm{art}}_{\Lambda}\subset\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}_{\Lambda}
be the full subcategory spanned by the Artinian objects

Informally, an object of
\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}_{\Lambda}
is an Artinian animated
\Lambda
-algebra with
residue field
k
, equipped with a divided power structure on its augmentation ideal.

Definition 6.2
.

A functor
F\colon\CAlg_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{pd},\mathrm{art}}\rightarrow\mathscr{S}
is a
divided power formal moduli problem
if it satisfies:

(1)

F(k)
is contractible;

(2)

for every pullback square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}

in
\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd},\mathrm{art}}_{W}
for which the maps
\pi_{0}A\rightarrow\pi_{0}B
and
\pi_{0}B^{\prime}\rightarrow\pi_{0}B
are surjective, applying
F
gives a pullback square in
\mathscr{S}
.

Write
\Moduli^{\mathrm{pd}}_{\Lambda}\subset\Fun(\CAlg_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{pd},\mathrm{art}},\mathscr{S})
for the full subcategory of divided power formal moduli problems.

Since the functor
\mathrm{und}\colon\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}\rightarrow\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}
preserves limits, we have:

Lemma 6.3
.

If
F\colon\CAlg_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{an},\mathrm{art}}\rightarrow\mathscr{S}
is a formal moduli problem, then

\CAlg_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{pd},\mathrm{art}}\rightarrow\CAlg_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{an},\mathrm{art}}\overset{F}{\rightarrow}\mathscr{S}

is a divided power formal moduli problem.

Deforming sheaves of animated divided power rings gives another example.

Construction 6.4

(The functor
\Def_{X}^{\mathrm{pd}}
)
.

Let
X=(\mathscr{T},\mathcal{O}_{X})
with
\mathscr{T}
be a small Grothendieck site and
\mathcal{O}_{X}

an object of
\Shv(\mathscr{T},\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}_{k})
. Consider the
\infty
-cagegory

\mathscr{D}:=\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}\times_{\Shv(\mathscr{T},\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}})}\Fun(\Delta^{1},\Shv(\mathscr{T},\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}))

and the projection
q\colon\mathscr{D}\rightarrow\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}
. Analogously to
Construction

3.22
, we obtain a left fibration

q\colon(\mathscr{D}^{\mathrm{cocart}})_{/(k,\mathcal{O}_{X})}\rightarrow\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}_{/k}

and a functor
\Def^{\mathrm{pd}}_{X}\colon\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd},\mathrm{art}}\rightarrow\widehat{\mathscr{S}}.

Remark 6.5

(Informal description)
.

The objects of the space
\Def^{\mathrm{pd}}_{X}(A)
are pushout squares in
\Shv(\mathscr{T},\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}})
of the form

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces k}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}_{X}.}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ulcorner}

Recall from
Notation

3.17
that
\Map^{\prime}(x,y)
is the space of sections of a given
y\rightarrow x
.

Proposition 6.6
.

The functor
\Def^{\mathrm{pd}}_{X}
is a divided power formal moduli problem, and for every perfect
V
in
\Mod_{k}^{\mathrm{cn}}
there is a natural equivalence

\Omega\Def^{\mathrm{pd}}_{X}(\sqz_{k}^{\mathrm{pd}}V)\simeq\Map^{\prime}_{\Shv(\mathscr{T},\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}_{k})}\big(\mathcal{O}_{X},\,\sqz_{k}^{\mathrm{pd}}V\otimes_{k}\mathcal{O}_{X}\big),

where
\sqz_{k}^{\mathrm{pd}}V
is defined as in
Definition

4.32
.

Proof.

The proof is essentially the same as for Propositions
3.24
and
3.25
. We only elaborate on the point in which the argument differs. Consider a pullback diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{01}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}

in
\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}
with
\pi_{0}A_{1}\rightarrow\pi_{0}A_{01}
and
\pi_{0}A_{1}\rightarrow\pi_{0}A_{01}
surjective, and such that all maps induce equivalences after applying the functor
\overline{(-)}
.
Writing
\mathscr{C}_{A}:=\Shv(\mathscr{T},\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}_{A})
, we have an adjunction

L\colon\mathscr{C}_{A}\leftrightarrows\mathscr{C}_{A_{0}}\times_{\mathscr{C}_{A_{01}}}\mathscr{C}_{A_{1}}:R,

and we need to verify that the unit and counit are equivalences. By
Proposition

4.6
, it suffices to check that they are equivalences on underlying sheaves of animated rings, and that they are equivalences after applying the functor
\overline{(-)}
. The first follows as in the proof of
Proposition

3.25
, and the second follows trivially since all maps involved are equivalences.
∎

Using
Proposition

4.36
, we deduce the following analogue of
Corollary

3.26
:

Corollary 6.7
.

\Def^{\mathrm{pd}}_{X}(\sqz_{k}^{\mathrm{pd}}V)\simeq\Map_{\Mod(X,\,\mathcal{O}_{X})}(L^{\mathrm{pd}}_{X/k},\,V[1]\otimes_{k}\mathcal{O}_{X})
.

Construction 6.8

(
\ind\colon\Def_{X}\rightarrow\Def_{X}^{\mathrm{pd}}
)
.

Consider the
\infty
-category

\mathscr{C}:=\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}\times_{\Shv(\mathscr{T},\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}})}\Fun(\Delta^{1},\Shv(\mathscr{T},\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}))

whose objects are given by pairs
(A,\mathcal{B})
with
A
in
\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}
and
\mathcal{B}
in
\Shv(\mathscr{T},\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}_{\mathrm{und}(A)})
. Similarly, consider the
\infty
-category

\mathscr{D}:=\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}\times_{\Shv(\mathscr{T},\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}})}\Fun(\Delta^{1},\Shv(\mathscr{T},\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}))

whose objects are pairs
(A,\mathcal{B})
with
A
in
\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}
and
\mathcal{B}
in
\Shv(\mathscr{T},\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}_{A})
.

The functor
\ind
defined in Section
4.3
induces a functor
\ind\colon\mathscr{C}\rightarrow\mathscr{D}
over
\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}
mapping
(A,\mathcal{B})
to
(A,\ind_{A}\mathcal{B})
, where
\ind_{A}\mathcal{B}
is the sheafification of the presheaf
U\mapsto\ind_{A}\mathcal{B}(U)
. For every
A\rightarrow A^{\prime}
in
\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}
and
\mathcal{B}
in
\Shv(\mathscr{T},\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}_{A})
, the map

A^{\prime}\otimes_{A}\ind_{A}(\mathcal{B})\rightarrow\ind_{A^{\prime}}(\mathrm{und}(A^{\prime})\otimes_{\mathrm{und}(A)}\mathcal{B})

is an equivalence, so
\ind\colon\mathscr{C}\rightarrow\mathscr{D}
preserves arrows that are cocartesian over
\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}
.

Now if
X=(\mathscr{T},\mathcal{O}_{X})
with
\mathcal{O}_{X}
in
\Shv(\mathscr{T},\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}_{k})
, then using
Construction

3.22
and
Construction

6.4
, we obtain a map
\ind\colon\Def_{X}\rightarrow\Def_{X}^{\mathrm{pd}}

in
\Moduli_{W}^{\mathrm{pd}}
.

6.2.
Construction of the period map

The functor
\overline{(-)}
induces a functor

(12)

\Fun(\Delta^{1},\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}})\rightarrow\Fun(\Delta^{1},\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}})

informally given by mapping
A\rightarrow B
to the composite
A\rightarrow B\rightarrow\overline{B}
. More formally, this can be defined by extending by sifted colimits from
\ArrPDPoly
.

Setup 6.9
.

Throughout this section, we fix a
coherent
topological space
X
, i.e. a topological space
for which the collection of quasi-compact open subsets is closed under finite intersections and
forms a basis of the topology.

Construction 6.10

(The functor
\theta
)
.

Write
\mathcal{K}
for the category
\bullet\overset{\sim}{\leftarrow}\bullet\rightarrow\bullet

and consider

\mathscr{E}:=\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}\times_{\Fun(\mathcal{K},\Shv(X,\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}))}\Fun(\mathcal{K},\Shv(X,\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}\Mod^{\wedge})),

the
\infty
-category of pairs consisting of an
A
in
\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}
and a diagram
N_{0}\overset{\sim}{\leftarrow}N\rightarrow M
in
\Shv(X,\Mod_{A}^{\wedge})
. Using (
12
), one constructs a functor
\theta\colon\mathscr{D}\rightarrow\mathscr{E}

informally given by

\theta\colon\big(A,\,\mathcal{B}\big)\mapsto\big(A,\,\dR^{\mathrm{pd}}_{\overline{\mathcal{B}}/A}\overset{\sim}{\leftarrow}\dR^{\mathrm{pd}}_{\mathcal{B}/A}\rightarrow\mathcal{B}\big),

where
\overline{\mathcal{B}}
denotes the sheafification of the presheaf
U\mapsto\overline{\mathcal{B}(U)}
.

To see that the left map is an equivalence, we note that by

Construction

2.23

and
Proposition

5.17
, the map
\dR^{\mathrm{pd}}_{\mathcal{B}/A}\rightarrow\dR^{\mathrm{pd}}_{\overline{\mathcal{B}}/A}
is obtained by sheafifying the map
of presheaves
U\mapsto(\dR^{\mathrm{pd}}_{\mathcal{B}(U)/A}\rightarrow\dR^{\mathrm{pd}}_{\overline{\mathcal{B}(U)}/A})
, which is an equivalence by

Theorem

5.22
.

We then consider also the
\infty
-category

\mathscr{F}:=\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}\times_{\Fun(\mathcal{K},\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}})}\Fun(\mathcal{K},\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}\Mod^{\wedge}),

whose objects are pairs of an
A
in
\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}
and a diagram

N_{0}\overset{\sim}{\leftarrow}N\rightarrow M
in
\Mod_{A}^{\wedge}
.

Taking global sections induces a functor
\RGamma\colon\mathscr{E}\rightarrow\mathscr{F}
, where we have precomposed with the canonical map from
R
to the global sections
R\Gamma(X,R)
of the corresponding constant sheaf on
X
. We obtain two functors lying over
\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}
:

\mathscr{D}\overset{\theta}{\longrightarrow}\mathscr{E}\overset{\RGamma}{\longrightarrow}\mathscr{F}.

Construction 6.11

(The fundamental diagram)
.

Thinking of
\overline{(-)}
from
Notation

4.10
as a functor

:\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}\rightarrow\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}
, we construct the
\infty
-category

\mathscr{D}^{\prime}:=\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}\times_{\Shv(X,\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}})}\Fun(\Delta^{1},\Shv(X,\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}))

whose objects are pairs
(A,\mathcal{B})
with
A\in\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}
and
\mathscr{B}
in
\Shv(X,\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}_{\overline{A}})
. We have a functor

\overline{(-)}:\mathscr{D}\rightarrow\mathscr{D}^{\prime}

sending a pair
(A,\mathcal{B})
to the pair

(A,\overline{\mathcal{B}})
.
Let

\mathscr{E}^{\prime}:=\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}\times_{\Shv(X,\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}})}\Shv(X,\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}\Mod^{\wedge})

be the
\infty
-category of pairs
(A,N_{0})
with
A\in\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}
and
N_{0}
in
\Shv(X,\Mod_{A}^{\wedge})
, and

\dR^{\mathrm{pd}}:\mathscr{D}^{\prime}\rightarrow\mathscr{E}^{\prime}

the functor informally given by
(A,\mathcal{B})\mapsto\dR^{\mathrm{pd}}_{\mathcal{B}/A}
. Finally, consider the
\infty
-category

\mathscr{F}^{\prime}:=\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}\times_{\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}}\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}\Mod^{\wedge}\simeq\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}\Mod^{\wedge}

and the functors
\mathrm{ev}_{0}\colon\mathscr{E}\rightarrow\mathscr{E}^{\prime}
and
\mathscr{F}\rightarrow\mathscr{F}^{\prime}
that map
(A,N_{0}\overset{\sim}{\leftarrow}N\rightarrow M)
to
(A,N_{0})
. Summarising all constructions so far gives a
commutative diagram over
\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}
:

(13)

\hbox to185.25pt{\vbox to50.45pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 91.52766pt\lower-23.49504pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{

}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{46.20824pt}{-14.98254pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform=matrix(1.0 0.0 0.0 1.0 63.94 -20.73)} \pgfsys@hbox{58}\lxSVG@closescope }}}

\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}.

Tracing a pair
(A,\mathcal{B})
in
\mathscr{D}
under the maps in the left square in (
13
) gives

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(A,\,\mathcal{B})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\big(A,\,\dR^{\mathrm{pd}}_{\overline{\mathcal{B}}/A}\overset{\sim}{\leftarrow}\dR^{\mathrm{pd}}_{\mathcal{B}/A}\rightarrow\mathcal{B}\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(A,\,\overline{\mathcal{B}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(A,\,\dR^{\mathrm{pd}}_{\overline{\mathcal{B}}/A}).}

Lemma 6.12
.

If
f\colon A\rightarrow A^{\prime}
is a map in
\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}
such that
\overline{A}\rightarrow\overline{A^{\prime}}
is an equivalence, then all functors in diagram (
13
) preserve cocartesian arrows over
f
.

In particular, this applies to all maps
f\colon A\rightarrow A^{\prime}
in
\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd},\mathrm{art}}_{W}
.

Proof of
Lemma

6.12
.

For
\ind
, this has already been observed in
Construction

6.8
.

Given any
\mathcal{B}\in\Shv(X,\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}_{A})
, we
note that by
Proposition

4.11
, all maps in the following square become equivalences after applying the functor
\overline{(-)}
:

(14)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A^{\prime}\otimes_{A}\mathcal{B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A^{\prime}\otimes_{A}\overline{\mathcal{B}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{A^{\prime}}\otimes_{\overline{A}}\mathcal{B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{A^{\prime}}\otimes_{\overline{A}}\overline{\mathcal{B}}\simeq\hskip-10.0pt}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hskip-32.0pt\overline{A^{\prime}\otimes_{A}\mathcal{B}}}

Together with
Proposition

5.11
and
Theorem

5.22
, this implies the claim for
\theta
.

Note that an arrow
(A,\mathcal{B})\rightarrow(A^{\prime},\mathcal{B}^{\prime})
in
\mathscr{D}^{\prime}
is cocartesian over
\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}
if and only if the map
\overline{A^{\prime}}\otimes_{\overline{A}}\mathcal{B}\rightarrow\mathcal{B}^{\prime}
is an equivalence.

Proposition

4.11
therefore implies that the functor
\overline{(-)}
in (
13
) preserves cocartesian arrows over
A\rightarrow A^{\prime}
.

To prove the statement about
\dR^{\mathrm{pd}}
, we need to verify that the composition

A^{\prime}\otimes_{A}\dR^{\mathrm{pd}}_{\mathcal{B}/A}\rightarrow\dR^{\mathrm{pd}}_{A^{\prime}\otimes_{A}\mathcal{B}/A^{\prime}}\rightarrow\dR^{\mathrm{pd}}_{\mathcal{B}^{\prime}/A^{\prime}}

is an equivalence as soon as
(A,\mathcal{B})\rightarrow(A^{\prime},\mathcal{B}^{\prime})
in
\mathscr{D}^{\prime}
is cocartesian. The first map is an equivalence by
Proposition

5.11
, the second by (
14
) and
Theorem

5.22
.

To see that the functors
R\Gamma
preserve cocartesian edges, we
note that as
A^{\prime}\otimes_{A}-
preserves finite limits and
X
is coherent, the functor
A^{\prime}\otimes_{A}-
preserves sheaves by
Corollary

2.26
. The functors
\mathrm{ev}_{0}
evidently preserve cocartesian edges.
∎

Now fix a quasi-compact quasi-separated scheme
X=(X,\mathcal{O}_{X})
over
k
. By
[
62
, 0A4G]
, the underlying topological space is coherent (cf.
Setup

6.9
).

In analogy with
Notation

2.32
and
5.18
, we write
\theta_{X/k}
for
\theta_{\mathcal{O}_{X}/k}
. Taking slice categories in (
13
), using
Lemma

6.12
, and applying the straightening functor we obtain a commutative diagram in
\Fun(\CAlg_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{pd},\mathrm{art}},\widehat{\mathscr{S}})
:

(15)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Def_{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Def_{X}^{\mathrm{pd}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Def^{\mathrm{mod}}_{\theta_{X/k}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Def^{\mathrm{mod}}_{\RGamma(X,\theta_{X/k})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\{\ast\}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Def^{\mathrm{mod}}_{\dR^{\mathrm{pd}}_{X/k}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Def^{\mathrm{mod}}_{\RGamma(X,\dR^{\mathrm{pd}}_{X/k})}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ind}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{ev}_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\RGamma}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{ev}_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dR^{\mathrm{pd}}_{X/-}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\RGamma}

When
X
is moreover smooth and proper, the two right corners of the above diagram
are formal moduli problems by
Proposition

3.19
, since
\RGamma(X,\dR_{X/k})
is eventually connective (cf.
[
62
, 0FM0]
). Under these conditions, we can define:

Definition 6.13

(Period domain and period map)
.

The
period domain
of
X

is the divided power formal moduli problem

\Per_{X}\colon\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd},\mathrm{art}}_{W}\rightarrow\mathscr{S}
obtained as the following pullback square in
\Moduli_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{pd}}
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Per_{X}}
