{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Def^{\mathrm{mod}}_{\RGamma(X,\theta_{X/k})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\{\ast\}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Def^{\mathrm{mod}}_{\RGamma(X,\dR_{X/k})}.}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{ev}_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\RGamma(\dR^{\mathrm{pd}}_{X/-})}

The
period map
is the

map

\per_{X}\colon\Def_{X}\rightarrow\Per_{X}

in
\Moduli_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{pd}}
induced
by the commutative diagram (
15
).

Remark 6.14

(Informal description)
.

Proposition

5.26
provides an equivalence

A\otimes_{W}\RGamma_{\mathrm{cris}}(X/W)\rightarrow\RGamma(X,\dR_{X/A}^{\mathrm{pd}})

for any
A
in
\CAlg_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{pd},\mathrm{art}}
. Hence
\Per_{X}(A)
is the space of maps
A\otimes_{W}\RGamma_{\mathrm{cris}}(X/W)\rightarrow P

in
\Mod_{A}
lifting the map

k\otimes_{W}\RGamma_{\mathrm{cris}}(X/W)\simeq\RGamma_{\dR}(X/k)\rightarrow\RGamma(X,\mathcal{O}_{X}).

The image of a lift
(X,\mathcal{B})
under
\per_{X}
can then be identified with the composite

A\otimes_{W}\RGamma_{\mathrm{cris}}(X/W)\simeq\RGamma(X,\dR_{X/A}^{\mathrm{pd}})\simeq\RGamma(X,\dR^{\mathrm{pd}}_{\mathcal{B}/A})\rightarrow\RGamma(X,\mathcal{B}),

where the second equivalence follows from
Theorem

5.22
.

Remark 6.15

(Analogy with classical period map)
.

The map
\per_{X}
is an analogue of the (formal) classical period map, which associates to a lift
(X,\mathcal{B})
of a smooth variety
X/\mathbf{C}
over some local Artinian
\mathbf{C}
-algebra
A
the composite

A\otimes_{\mathbf{Z}}\RGamma(X(\mathbf{C}),\mathbf{Z})\simeq\RGamma(X,\Omega^{\bullet}_{\mathcal{B}/A})\rightarrow\RGamma(X,\mathcal{B}).

Here the equivalence is an avatar of the Gauss–Manin connection.

Remark 6.16

(Higher parts of the Hodge filtration)
.

We have restricted ourselves to the variation of the first step of the Hodge filtration. One could similarly define period maps tracking the variation of the higher pieces of the Hodge filtration.

Remark 6.17

(Non-formal deformations)
.

The period map can be extended to non-formal deformations over divided power rings; we will explore this

further in
[
10
]
.

6.3.
Tangent to the period map

Let
X=(X,\mathcal{O}_{X})
be a smooth and proper scheme over
k
.
Given
V\in\Mod_{k}^{\mathrm{cn}}
perfect, we constructed
\sqz_{k}^{\mathrm{pd}}V\in\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd},\mathrm{art}}_{W}
with underlying animated ring
\sqz_{k}V\in\CAlg^{\mathrm{an},\mathrm{art}}_{W}
. In this section, we will compute

\per_{X}(\sqz_{k}^{\mathrm{pd}}V)\colon\Def_{X}^{\mathrm{an}}(\sqz_{k}^{\mathrm{pd}}V)\rightarrow\Per_{X}(\sqz_{k}^{\mathrm{pd}}V).

Theorem 6.18
.

The map
\per_{X}(\sqz_{k}^{\mathrm{pd}}V)
sits in a commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Def_{X}(\sqz_{k}^{\mathrm{pd}}V)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Map_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}(L_{X/k},\,V[1]\otimes_{k}\mathcal{O}_{X})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Per_{X}(\sqz_{k}^{\mathrm{pd}}V)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Map_{k}\!\big(\RGamma(X,\Fil^{1}\dR_{X/k}),\,V\otimes_{k}\RGamma(X,\mathcal{O}_{X})\big),}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

where the right map is induced by the map
\pi^{1}\colon\Fil^{1}\dR_{X/k}\rightarrow L_{X/k}[-1]
, and the horizontal maps are equivalences.

Remark 6.19
.

The statement is entirely analogous to the classical computation of the derivative of the complex period map due to Griffiths
[
23
]
.

The rest of this section is devoted to the proof of
Theorem

6.18
.

Notation 6.20
.

In this section, we write
A:=\sqz_{k}^{\mathrm{pd}}(V)
for
V\in\Mod_{k}^{\mathrm{cn}}
perfect and
\mathcal{B}:=A\otimes_{k}\mathcal{O}_{X}
. Abusing notation, write
A_{\mathrm{triv}}=\mathrm{und}(A)\otimes_{k}\sqz_{k}(V)=A\otimes_{k}\sqz_{k}(V)
, interpreted as an object of either
\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}
or
\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}
. Set
\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{triv}}:=A_{\mathrm{triv}}\otimes_{k}\mathcal{O}_{X}
.

Informally,
\mathcal{B}
is equipped with the divided power ideal
V\otimes_{k}\mathcal{O}_{X}
, while
\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{triv}}
is equipped with the zero divided power ideal.

The counit of the adjunction of
Proposition

4.9
defines a map
v\colon A_{\mathrm{triv}}\rightarrow A
in
\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}
and hence a map
v\colon\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{triv}}\rightarrow\mathcal{B}

in
\Shv(X,\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}_{k})
.

Lemma 6.21
.

There is a commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega\Def_{X}(A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Map^{\prime}_{\Shv(X,\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}_{k})}\big(\mathcal{O}_{X},\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{triv}}\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega\Def^{\mathrm{pd}}_{X}(A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Map^{\prime}_{\Shv(X,\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}_{k})}\big(\mathcal{O}_{X},\,\mathcal{B}\big)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ind}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces v}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

in which the horizontal maps are the equivalences from
Proposition

3.24
and
Proposition

6.6
, respectively, and the right map is induced by the map
v\colon\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{triv}}\rightarrow\mathcal{B}
.

Proof.

This is rather formal from the definitions.
We have a commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Map_{\Shv(X,\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}_{A_{\mathrm{triv}}})}(\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{triv}},\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{triv}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Map_{\Shv(X,\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}_{k})}(\mathcal{O}_{X},\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{triv}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Map_{\Shv(X,\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}_{A})}(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{B})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Map_{\Shv(X,\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}_{k})}(\mathcal{O}_{X},\mathcal{B}).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A\otimes_{A_{\mathrm{triv}}}-}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces v}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

Here the horizontal equivalences are the base change adjunctions for
k\rightarrow A_{\mathrm{triv}}
and
k\rightarrow A
, respectively. The diagram lies over
\Map_{\Shv(X,\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}_{k})}(\mathcal{O}_{X},\mathcal{O}_{X})
, and taking the fibres over the identity map yields the desired commutative square.
∎

Lemma 6.22
.

There is a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega\Def^{\mathrm{pd}}_{X}(A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Map^{\prime}_{\Shv(X,\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}_{k})}\big(\mathcal{O}_{X},\mathcal{B}\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega\Per_{X}(A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Map^{\prime}_{\Fun(\mathcal{K},\Mod_{k})}\big(\RGamma(X,\theta_{X/k}),\,\RGamma(X,\theta_{\mathcal{B}/k})\big),}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\RGamma\circ\theta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

in which the top horizontal map is the equivalence from
Proposition

6.6
, and the lower horizontal
map is an equivalence.

Proof.

Lemma

6.12
gives an equivalence
\theta_{\mathcal{B}/A}\simeq A\otimes_{k}\theta_{\mathcal{O}_{X}/k}
. Consider
the
composite

\alpha:\theta_{\mathcal{B}/k}\rightarrow\theta_{\mathcal{B}/A}\simeq A\otimes_{k}\theta_{X/k}.

in
\Fun(\mathcal{K},\Shv(X,\Mod_{k}))
and write

\alpha_{0}:\dR^{\mathrm{pd}}_{X/k}\rightarrow\dR^{\mathrm{pd}}_{X/A}\simeq A\otimes_{k}\dR_{X/k}

for its component in
\Shv(X,\Mod_{k})
. Under the equivalences

\Omega\Def_{?}(A)\simeq\Map^{\prime}(?,A\otimes_{k}?)

of Propositions
6.6
and
3.18
, the commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega\Def_{X}^{\mathrm{pd}}(A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\{\ast\}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega\Def_{\RGamma(\theta_{X/k})}^{\mathrm{mod}}(A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega\Def_{\RGamma(\dR_{X/k})}^{\mathrm{mod}}(A)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\RGamma\circ\theta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\RGamma(\dR^{\mathrm{pd}}_{X/A})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{ev}_{0}}

becomes equivalent to the outer square in the commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Map^{\prime}\big(\mathcal{O}_{X},\,\mathcal{B}\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\{*\}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Map^{\prime}\big(\RGamma(\theta_{X/k}),\,\RGamma(\theta_{\mathcal{B}/k})\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Map^{\prime}\big(\RGamma(\dR_{X/k}),\,\RGamma(\dR_{X/k})\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Map^{\prime}\big(\RGamma(\theta_{X/k}),A\otimes_{k}\RGamma(\theta_{X/k})\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Map^{\prime}\big(\RGamma(\dR_{X/k}),\,A\otimes_{k}\RGamma(\dR_{X/k})\big).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\RGamma\circ\theta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{ev}_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{ev}_{0}}

Here
\Map^{\prime}
is computed in
\Shv(X,\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}_{k})
,
\Fun(\mathcal{K},\Mod_{k})
, and
\Mod_{k}
, respectively.

Note that the top right map is an equivalence.
We claim that the bottom square is a pullback. To verify this, we compare the cofibres of
\alpha
and
\alpha_{0}
.
As
\dR_{\mathcal{B}/k}\xrightarrow{\simeq}\dR_{X/k}
, we find

\cofib(\alpha)\simeq\Map\!\big(\RGamma(\theta_{X/k}),\,V\otimes_{k}\RGamma(\dR_{X/k})\overset{\sim}{\leftarrow}V\otimes_{k}\RGamma(\dR_{X/k})\rightarrow 0\big),

with the mapping space computed in
\Fun(\mathcal{K},\Mod_{k})
, and

\cofib(\alpha_{0})\simeq\Map_{k}\!\big(\RGamma(\dR_{X/k}),\,V\otimes_{k}\RGamma(\dR_{X/k})\big).

Since
0
is final in
\Mod_{k}
, the induced map
\mathrm{ev}_{0}\colon\cofib(\alpha)\rightarrow\cofib(\alpha_{0})
is an equivalence, and we conclude that the lower square above is a pullback.

The claim now follows by comparing the above pullback diagram with the pullback square in
Definition

6.13
.
∎

By
Proposition

5.16
, we have a commutative square

(16)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dR_{X/k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}_{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{X/k},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d}

which induces a map

\Psi_{X/k}\colon\theta_{X/k}\rightarrow\big[0\overset{\sim}{\leftarrow}0\rightarrow L_{X/k}\big]

in
\Fun(\mathcal{K},\Mod(X,k))
.
The trivial section
\mathcal{O}_{X}\rightarrow\mathcal{B}
induces a decomposition

(17)

\theta_{\mathcal{B}/k}\simeq\theta_{X/k}\,\oplus\,\,\big[0\overset{\sim}{\leftarrow}0\rightarrow V\otimes_{k}\mathcal{O}_{X}\big]

in
\Fun(\mathcal{K},\Mod(X,k))
. The following lemma is the key to the proof of
Theorem

6.18
:

Lemma 6.23
.

There is a commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Map^{\prime}_{\Shv(X,\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}_{k})}\!\big(\mathcal{O}_{X},\,\mathcal{B}\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Map\!_{\Mod(X,\mathcal{O}_{X})}\big(L_{X/k},\,V\otimes_{k}\mathcal{O}_{X}\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Map^{\prime}\!\big(\RGamma(\theta_{X/k}),\,\RGamma(\theta_{\mathcal{B}/k})\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Map\!\big(\RGamma(\theta_{X/k}),\,0\overset{\sim}{\leftarrow}0\rightarrow V\otimes_{k}\RGamma(\mathcal{O}_{X})\big)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\RGamma\circ\theta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Psi_{X/k}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

in which the top map is the equivalence from
Proposition

4.36
, and the bottom equivalence
is induced by (
17
). The lower mapping spaces are computed in
\Fun(\mathcal{K},\Mod_{k})
.

Proof.

The formation of the square (
16
) is functorial in the sheaf
\mathcal{O}_{X}
. In particular, it
provides a commutative square

(18)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Map^{\prime}_{\Shv(X,\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}_{k})}\big(\mathcal{O}_{X},\,\mathcal{B}\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Map_{\Shv(X,\Mod_{k})}\big(L_{X/k},\,L_{\mathcal{B}/k}\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Map^{\prime}\big(\theta_{X/k},\,\theta_{\mathcal{B}/k}\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Map\big(\theta_{X/k},\,0\overset{\sim}{\leftarrow}0\rightarrow L_{\mathcal{B}/k}\big)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Psi_{X/k}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Psi_{\mathcal{B}/k}}

By the adjunction of
Proposition

4.36
, the projection
\mathcal{B}\rightarrow V\otimes_{k}\mathcal{O}_{X}
factors as

\mathcal{B}\overset{d}{\longrightarrow}L_{\mathcal{B}/k}\overset{\delta}{\longrightarrow}V\otimes_{k}\mathcal{O}_{X},

where we have used that
\mathcal{B}\simeq\sqz^{\mathrm{pd}}_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}(V\otimes_{k}\mathcal{O}_{X})
.
Composing the square (
18
) on the right with the map induced by
\delta
, we obtain a commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Map^{\prime}_{\Shv(X,\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}_{k})}\big(\mathcal{O}_{X},\,\mathcal{B}\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Map_{\Mod(X,k)}\big(L_{X/k},\,V\otimes_{k}\mathcal{O}_{X}\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Map^{\prime}\big(\theta_{X/k},\,\theta_{\mathcal{B}/k}\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Map\big(\theta_{X/k},\,0\overset{\sim}{\leftarrow}0\rightarrow V\otimes_{k}\mathcal{O}_{X}\big)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Psi_{X/k}}

By construction, the top map is the adjunction equivalence of
Proposition

4.36
. Composing with
\RGamma(X,-)
gives the desired commutative square.
∎

Proof of
Theorem

6.18
.

Lemmas
6.21
,
6.22
, and
6.23
give the following diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega\Def_{X}(A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Map_{\Mod(X,\mathcal{O}_{X})}\!\big(L_{X/k},\,V\otimes_{k}\mathcal{O}_{X}\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega\Per_{X}(A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Map_{\Fun(\mathcal{K},\Mod_{k})}\big(\RGamma(\theta_{X/k}),\,0\overset{\sim}{\leftarrow}0\rightarrow V\otimes_{k}\RGamma(\mathcal{O}_{X})\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Map_{k}\!\big(\RGamma(\Fil^{1}\dR_{X/k})[1],\,V\otimes_{k}\RGamma(\mathcal{O}_{X})\big).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\per_{X}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Psi_{X/k}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

The bottom right vertical map is the equivalence induced by passing to cofibres in the domain. To compute the composition of the right vertical maps, note that by
Proposition

5.16
, diagram (
16
) extends to a map of cofibre sequences

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dR_{X/k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}_{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Fil^{1}\dR_{X/k}[1]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{X/k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{X/k}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{1}}

This gives a commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega\Def_{X}(A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Map_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}(L_{X/k},\,V\otimes_{k}\mathcal{O}_{X})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega\Per_{X}(A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Map_{k}(\RGamma(\Fil^{1}\dR_{X/k})[1],\,V\otimes_{k}\RGamma(\mathcal{O}_{X})).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\per_{X}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

The claim follows as
\Omega F(\sqz^{\mathrm{pd}}V[1])\simeq F(\sqz^{\mathrm{pd}}V)
for
F
in
\Moduli^{\mathrm{pd}}_{W}
.
∎

7.
Unobstructedness

In this section, we leverage the period map of Section
6

to prove
Theorem

A
.
First, we establish conditions under which the period domain
\Per_{X}
is unobstructed. Next, we check that
\per_{X}

often induces an injection on ‘divided power obstruction classes’, which lets us deduce that
\Def_{X}
is ‘divided power unobstructed’. Finally, we use a bootstrapping technique (inspired by Schröer
[
59
]
) to establish a general unobstructedness criterion in
Theorem

7.16
, from which we then deduce
Theorem

A
(using a result of Achinger–Suh
[
2
]
at
p=2
).

Fix a perfect

field
k
of characteristic
p
and write
W
for its ring of Witt vectors.

7.1.
Unobstructedness of the period domain

Let
M
and
N
be eventually connective objects in
\Mod_{W}
, and write
M_{0}:=k\otimes_{W}M

and
N_{0}:=k\otimes_{W}N
.
Using
Construction

3.15
, we consider the formal moduli problem
F\colon\CAlg^{\mathrm{an},\mathrm{art}}_{W}\rightarrow\mathscr{S}
whose
A
-points are given by the pullback square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Def^{\mathrm{mod}}_{M_{0}\oplus N_{0}\rightarrow N_{0}}(A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\{\ast\}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Def^{\mathrm{mod}}_{M_{0}\oplus N_{0}}(A).}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{ev}_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A\otimes_{W}(M\oplus N)}

One can think of
F
as the formal neighbourhood of a point in a derived Grassmannian; the following lemma provides local coordinates on this Grassmannian:

Lemma 7.1
.

There is a fibre sequence

F(A)\rightarrow\Map_{W}(M,A\otimes_{W}N)\rightarrow\Map_{W}(M,N_{0}),

functorial in
A\in\CAlg^{\mathrm{an},\mathrm{art}}_{W}
.

Notation 7.2
.

If
c
is an object in an
\infty
-category
\mathscr{C}
, let
\mathscr{C}_{c/}^{\simeq}
be the
subcategory of
\mathscr{C}_{c/}
whose objects are arrows
c\rightarrow d
and whose morphisms are equivalences
between them.

Proof of
Lemma

7.1
.

Chasing the definitions,
we see that
F(A)
sits in a pullback

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Mod_{A,A\otimes_{W}(M\oplus N)/}^{\simeq}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\{\ast\}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Mod_{k,(M_{0}\oplus N_{0})/}^{\simeq},}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces k\otimes_{A}-}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N_{0}}

functorially in
A
. Here the bottom map picks out the projection
\pr_{1}\colon M_{0}\oplus N_{0}\rightarrow N_{0}
.

Let
\Mod_{A,A\otimes_{W}(M\oplus N)/}^{\simeq,\ast}
be the full subcategory of those
A\otimes_{W}(M\oplus N)\rightarrow Q
such that the induced map
A\otimes_{W}N\rightarrow Q
is an equivalence. By the conservativity of
k\otimes_{A}-
, the above square factors as the composition of two pullback squares:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Mod_{A,A\otimes_{W}(M\oplus N)/}^{\simeq,\ast}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Mod_{A,A\otimes_{W}(M\oplus N)/}^{\simeq}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\{\ast\}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Mod_{k,(M_{0}\oplus N_{0})/}^{\simeq,\ast}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Mod_{k,(M_{0}\oplus N_{0})/}^{\simeq}.}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces k\otimes_{A}-}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces k\otimes_{A}-}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N_{0}}

We note that the middle vertical map is given by the map

\Map_{A}(A\otimes_{W}M,A\otimes_{W}N)\rightarrow\Map_{k}(M_{0},N_{0}),

which in turn identifies with the map

\Map_{W}(M,A\otimes_{W}N)\rightarrow\Map_{W}(M,N_{0}).\vskip-14.0pt

∎

Lemma 7.3
.

Assume moreover that the
W
-module
\pi_{0}\Map_{W}(N,M)
is projective and that
N
is perfect. Then
F^{\mathrm{cl}}\colon\CRing^{\mathrm{art}}_{W}\rightarrow\Set
(cf.
Notation

3.8
) is unobstructed.

Proof.

Since
N
is perfect,
Lemma

7.1
gives an equivalence

F(A)\simeq\Map_{W}(\Hom_{W}(N,M),I_{A}),

where
I_{A}
denotes the fibre of the augmentation map
A\rightarrow k
. The lemma now follows from the following general fact applied to the mapping complex
H:=\Hom_{W}(N,M)
.

Let
H
be an object of
\Mod_{W}
with
\pi_{0}H
a projective
W
-module, and assume
P\rightarrow Q
is a surjective map of discrete
W
-modules. Then the induced map

\pi_{0}\Map_{W}(H,P)\rightarrow\pi_{0}\Map_{W}(H,Q)

is surjective. Indeed, by the universal coefficient theorem, it sits in a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Ext^{1}_{W}(\pi_{-1}H,P)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{0}\Map_{W}(H,P)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Hom_{W}(\pi_{0}H,P)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Ext^{1}_{W}(\pi_{-1}H,Q)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{0}\Map_{W}(H,Q)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Hom_{W}(\pi_{0}H,Q)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}

with exact rows. The left vertical map is surjective as
\Ext^{2}_{W}(-.-)
vanishes since
W
is a principal ideal domain.
The right vertical map is surjective since
\pi_{0}H
is projective. We conclude
that also the middle vertical map must be surjective.
∎

Using that finitely generated torsion-free modules over PIDs are free, we deduce:

Corollary 7.4
.

Let
X
be a smooth and proper scheme over
k
. Assume that there exists a decomposition

\RGamma_{\mathrm{cris}}(X/W)\simeq M\oplus N

in
\Mod_{W}^{\wedge}
such that the following composition is an equivalence:

k\otimes_{W}N\rightarrow k\otimes_{W}\RGamma_{\mathrm{cris}}(X/W)\overset{\simeq}{\rightarrow}\RGamma_{\dR}(X/k)\rightarrow\RGamma(X,\mathcal{O}_{X}).

Assume that
\pi_{0}\Map_{W}(N,M)
is torsion-free. Then
\Per_{X}
is unobstructed.

We record the following special case:

Corollary 7.5
.

Let
X
be a smooth and proper scheme over
k
such that

(1)

\mathrm{H}^{i}_{\mathrm{cris}}(X/W)
is torsion-free for all
i
;

(2)

the map
\mathrm{H}_{\dR}^{i}(X/k)\rightarrow\mathrm{H}^{i}(X,\mathcal{O}_{X})
is
surjective for all
i
.

Then
\Per_{X}
is unobstructed.

Proof.

As
W
is a PID and
X
is smooth and proper,
\mathrm{H}^{i}_{\mathrm{cris}}(X/W)
is a finitely generated free
W
-module and there is an equivalence

\RGamma_{\mathrm{cris}}(X/W)\simeq\bigoplus_{i}\mathrm{H}^{i}_{\mathrm{cris}}(X/W)[-i]

in
\Mod_{W}
. For all
i
, there is a canonical isomorphism
k\otimes_{W}\mathrm{H}^{i}_{\mathrm{cris}}(X/W)\rightarrow\mathrm{H}^{i}_{\dR}(X/k)
.
Moreover, we can pick a splitting
\mathrm{H}^{i}_{\mathrm{cris}}(X/W)=M_{i}\oplus N_{i}
such that the induced map
k\otimes_{W}N_{i}\rightarrow\mathrm{H}^{i}_{\dR}(X/k)\rightarrow\mathrm{H}^{i}(X,\mathcal{O}_{X})
is an isomorphism. Taking
M=\oplus_{i}M_{i}[-i]
and
N=\oplus_{i}N_{i}[-i]
gives a decomposition as in
Corollary

7.4
.
∎

7.2.
Injectivity on obstruction classes

Proposition 7.6
.

Let
X
be a smooth and proper scheme over
k
such that

(1)

the map

\mathrm{H}^{d-1}(X,\Fil^{1}\Omega_{X/k})\rightarrow\mathrm{H}^{d-2}(X,\Omega^{1}_{X/k})

is surjective;

(2)

the map

\Ext^{2}_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}(\Omega^{1}_{X/k},\mathcal{O}_{X})\rightarrow\Hom_{k}\big(\mathrm{H}^{d-2}(X,\Omega^{1}_{X/k}),\,\mathrm{H}^{d}(X,\mathcal{O}_{X})\big)

is injective.

Then for all finite (discrete)
k
-modules
V
the map

\pi_{0}\Def_{X}(\sqz_{k}V[1])\rightarrow\pi_{0}\Per_{X}(\sqz^{\mathrm{pd}}_{k}V[1])

induced by
\per_{X}
is injective.

Proof.

Using that
\per_{X}
is a map of divided power formal moduli problems, we reduce to the case
V=k
.
By
Theorem

6.18
, the map then identifies with the map

\Hom_{X}\big(\Omega^{1}_{X/k}[-1],\,\mathcal{O}_{X}[1]\big)\longrightarrow\Hom_{k}\big(\RGamma(\Fil^{1}\Omega^{\bullet}_{X/k}),\,\RGamma(\mathcal{O}_{X})[1]\big)

induced by
\Fil^{1}\Omega^{\bullet}_{X/k}\rightarrow\Omega^{1}_{X/k}[-1]
.
This is the top map in the commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Hom_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\big(\Omega^{1}_{X/k}[-1],\,\mathcal{O}_{X}[1]\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Hom_{k}\big(\RGamma(\Fil^{1}\Omega^{\bullet}_{X/k}),\,\RGamma(\mathcal{O}_{X}[1])\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Hom_{k}\big(\mathrm{H}^{d-2}(\Omega^{1}_{X}),\,\mathrm{H}^{d}(\mathcal{O}_{X})\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Hom_{k}\big(\mathrm{H}^{d-1}(\Fil^{1}\Omega^{\bullet}_{X}),\,\mathrm{H}^{d}(\mathcal{O}_{X})\big),}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{1-d}\circ R\Gamma}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{1-d}}

where the horizontal maps are induced by
\Fil^{1}\Omega^{\bullet}_{X/k}\rightarrow\Omega^{1}_{X/k}[-1]
.
As the left and bottom maps are injective by assumption, so is the top map.
∎

7.3.
Bootstrapping from divided power rings

We will now prove a criterion which allows us to test unobstructedness of a deformation functor on its restriction to divided power rings. This criterion is a variation on a theorem of Schröer
[
59
]
.

Definition 7.7
.

A map
A\rightarrow B
in
\PDRing^{\mathrm{art}}_{W}
is called a
divided power elementary extension
if it is surjective, and if the kernel
I:=\ker(A\rightarrow B)
satisfies

(1)

\mathfrak{m}_{A}I=0
;

(2)

\gamma_{n}(x)=0
for all
x\in I
and
n\geq 2
.

The first condition guarantees that the
A
-module
I
is in fact a
k
-module. Note that the second condition is not implied by the first. For example,
\mathbf{Z}/4\mathbf{Z}\rightarrow\mathbf{F}_{2}
is not a divided power elementary extension, since
\gamma_{2}(2)=2
. This is in some sense the only counterexample relevant to us. Indeed, we have the following result:

Proposition 7.8
.

Assume that
k
is infinite.
Let
F\colon\CRing_{W}^{\mathrm{art}}\rightarrow\Set
be a deformation functor (see
Definition

3.5
) such that

(1)

F(k[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2})
is finite-dimensional;

(2)

for all divided power elementary extensions
A^{\prime}\rightarrow A
in
\PDRing^{\mathrm{art}}_{W}
, the induced map
F(A^{\prime})\rightarrow F(A)
is surjective;

(3)

if
p=2
then
F(W/4)
is non-empty.

Then
F
is unobstructed.

Remark 7.9
.

It is crucial to consider mixed characteristic divided power rings, even if one is only interested in equal characteristic deformations. For example, let
F\colon\CRing_{k}^{\mathrm{art}}\rightarrow\Set

be the functor represented by
k[t]/t^{p}
. Then

F(A^{\prime})\rightarrow F(A)
is surjective for all surjective
A^{\prime}\rightarrow A
in
\PDRing^{\mathrm{art}}_{k}
, yet
F
is clearly obstructed.

The remainder of this section is devoted to the proof of
Proposition

7.8
.

Notation 7.10
.

Let us denote the quotient
W/p^{n}
by
W_{n}
, and write
W_{n,d}
for the truncated divided power polynomial ring

W_{n,d}=W_{n}\langle t\rangle/(\gamma_{i}(t):i\geq d).

Note that as a
W_{n}
-module,
W_{n,d}
is free of rank
d
on generators
1,t,\ldots,\gamma_{d-1}(t)
. We proceed to the technical heart of the proof of
Proposition

7.8
:

Lemma 7.11
.

Assume that
k
is infinite.
Let
F\colon\CRing^{\mathrm{art}}_{W}\rightarrow\Set
be a deformation functor. Assume that
F(k[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2})
is finite-dimensional, and assume that for all
n\geq 1
and
d\geq 2
the map
F(W_{n,d})\rightarrow F(W_{1,2})

is surjective. Then
F
is unobstructed.

Proof.

First assume that
F
is pro-represented by a complete Noetherian
W
-algebra
R
. By
[
58
, Lemma 1.1]
, we can write
R=W[[x_{1},\ldots,x_{m}]]/I
, and after possibly eliminating variables,
we may further assume that

I\subset(x_{1},\ldots,x_{m})^{2}+(p).

We then need to show that
I=0
.

For the sake of contradiction, suppose that there exists a non-zero element
f\in I
. Write
f=p^{e}g+h

with
g
a homogeneous degree
d
polynomial which is
non-zero mod
p
and
h
consisting of terms of higher degree. Choose
a_{1},\ldots,a_{m}\in k
such that
g(a_{1},\ldots,a_{m})\not\equiv 0
mod
p
.
By our assumption on
I
, the map

W[[x_{1},\ldots,x_{m}]]\rightarrow k[t]/t^{2},\,x_{i}\mapsto a_{i}t

factors through a map
\varphi\colon R\rightarrow k[t]/t^{2}
. Now choose
n\gg 0
such that
p^{e}d!
is non-zero in
W_{n}
.
By our hypothesis,
\varphi
admits a lift
\widetilde{\varphi}\colon R\rightarrow W_{n,d+1}
.
This lift must satisfy

\widetilde{\varphi}(x_{i})=\widetilde{a}_{i}t+c_{i,2}\gamma_{2}(t)+\cdots

where
\widetilde{a}_{i}
reduces to
a_{i}
mod
p
. As
\gamma_{d+1}(t)=0
in
W_{n,d+1}
, we see that

\widetilde{\varphi}(f)=p^{e}t^{d}g(\widetilde{a}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{a}_{m})=p^{e}d!g(\widetilde{a}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{a}_{m})\gamma_{d}(t)

in
W_{n,d+1}
. By construction,
g(\widetilde{a}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{a}_{m})
is a
p
-adic unit, and
p^{e}d!
is non-zero in
W_{n}
, so
\widetilde{\varphi}(f)\neq 0
in
W_{n,d+1}
. But this contradicts our assumption that
f
lies in the ideal
I
. This finishes the proof in the pro-representable case.

Now if
F
is not pro-representable, then Schlessinger’s criterion
[
58
]
implies that
F
admits a hull, i.e. a smooth map
G\rightarrow F
with
G
pro-representable by some
R
as above, and such that
G(k[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2})\rightarrow F(k[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2})
is a bijection. For every
n\geq 1
and
d\geq 2
, we have a commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G(W_{n,d})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G(W_{1,2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(W_{n,d})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(W_{1,2}).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}

The bottom map is surjective by assumption. The right map is a bijection since
G
is a hull of
F
(note that
W_{1,2}\cong k[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}
).
An easy induction on the length shows that
G(A)\rightarrow F(A)
is surjective for all
A
in
\CRing^{\mathrm{art}}_{W}
. In particular, the left map is surjective. We conclude that the top map is surjective. Hence
G
is unobstructed, and therefore
F
is also unobstructed.
∎

To finish the proof of
Proposition

7.8
, we observe that the maps
W_{n,d}\rightarrow W_{1,2}
can (almost) be factored as a composition of divided power elementary extensions.

Lemma 7.12
.

Let
A\rightarrow B
be a surjective map in
\PDRing^{\mathrm{art}}_{W}
with the property that
\gamma_{m}(x)=0
for all
m\geq 2
and all

x
in
I:=\ker(A\rightarrow B)
. Then
A\rightarrow B
can be factored as a composition of divided power elementary extensions.

Proof.

Assume
I\neq 0
. As
I
is an Artinian
A
-module, there is a nonzero submodule
J\subset I
with
\mathfrak{m}_{A}J=0
. The map
A\rightarrow B
then factors over a divided power elementary extension
A\rightarrow A/J
. Repeating this process gives the desired factorisation.
∎

Corollary 7.13
.

Let
d\geq 0
and
n\geq 1
. Then the map
W_{n,d+1}\rightarrow W_{n,d}
can be factored as a composition of divided power elementary extensions.

Corollary 7.14
.

Let
d\geq 0
, and
n\geq 1
such that
p\neq 2
or
n>1
. Then the map
W_{n+1,d}\rightarrow W_{n,d}
is a
composition of divided power elementary extensions.

Proof.

The corresponding ideal is generated by
p^{n}
, so the corollary follows from the fact that
\gamma_{m}(p^{n})=p^{mn}/m!
is divisible by
p^{n+1}
as soon as
p^{n}\neq 2
and
m\geq 2
, which can be readily verified using Legendre’s formula for the valuation
\nu_{p}(m!)
.
∎

Proof of
Proposition

7.8
.

Observe that we can factor the map
W_{n,d}\rightarrow W_{1,2}
as

W_{n,d}\rightarrow W_{n,{d-1}}\rightarrow\cdots\rightarrow W_{n,2}\rightarrow W_{n-1,2}\rightarrow\cdots\rightarrow W_{1,2}.

For
p>2
, the assumption on
F
together with
Corollaries
7.13
and
7.14
imply that
F(W_{n,d})\rightarrow F(W_{1,2})
is surjective for all
n,d
.
Lemma

7.11
then implies that
F
is unobstructed.

For
p=2
, it remains to show that
F(W_{2,2})\rightarrow F(W_{1,2})
is surjective. The map
W_{2,2}\rightarrow W_{1,2}
factors as

W_{2,2}\cong W_{2}[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}\twoheadrightarrow W_{2}[\epsilon]/(\epsilon^{2},2\epsilon)\twoheadrightarrow k[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}\cong W_{1,2}.

The first map is divided power elementary, hence
F(W_{2}[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2})\rightarrow F(W_{2}[\epsilon]/(2\epsilon,\epsilon^{2})
is surjective. The second map sits in a pullback square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W_{2}[\epsilon]/(\epsilon^{2},2\epsilon)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces k[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces k}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}

By the assumptions, there exists a lift
y
in
F(W_{2})
. Since
F
is a deformation functor, for every
x\in F(k[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2})
there exists an
\tilde{x}
in
F(W_{2}[\epsilon]/(\epsilon^{2},2\epsilon))
lifting both
x
and
y
. We conclude that
F(W_{2}[\epsilon]/(\epsilon^{2},2\epsilon))\rightarrow F(k[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2})
is surjective, and therefore
F(W_{2,2})\rightarrow F(W_{1,2})
is surjective as well.
∎

7.4.
Divided power elementary extensions as homotopy pullbacks

In
Definition

7.7
, we introduced the notation of a
divided power elementary extension
. We now relate this to divided power trivial square-zero functor
\sqz_{k}^{\mathrm{pd}}
of
Definition

4.32
.

Proposition 7.15
.

Let
A\rightarrow B
be a divided power elementary extension in
\PDRing^{\mathrm{art}}_{W}
with kernel
I
. Then
A\rightarrow B
sits in a pullback square in
\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd},\mathrm{art}}_{W}
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces k}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sqz^{\mathrm{pd}}_{k}(I[1]).}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}

Proof.

Proposition

4.39
provides a map

L_{B/A}^{\mathrm{pd}}\rightarrow\tau_{\leq 1}L_{B/A}^{\mathrm{pd}}\simeq I[1].

Using
Corollary

4.35
and the fact that
I
comes from a
k
-module, we obtain
a map

B\longrightarrow\sqz^{\mathrm{pd}}_{B}(I[1])\longrightarrow\sqz^{\mathrm{pd}}_{k}(I[1])

in
\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}_{A/}
. Define
A^{\prime}
as the following pullback square in in
\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}_{A/}
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces k}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sqz^{\mathrm{pd}}_{k}(I[1]).}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}

It suffices to show that the induced map
f\colon A\rightarrow A^{\prime}
is an equivalence in
\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}
. By construction, we know that
A^{\prime}
is discrete and that

A/I_{A}\rightarrow A^{\prime}/I_{A^{\prime}}
is an equivalence.
By
Proposition

4.6
, it then suffices to verify that the induced map
B/A\rightarrow B/A^{\prime}
on cofibres is an equivalence. Unravelling the definitions, we see that it is equivalent to the canonical map
B/A\rightarrow L_{B/A}^{\mathrm{pd}}\rightarrow\tau_{\leq 1}L_{B/A}^{\mathrm{pd}}
.

But
Proposition

4.39
shows that this map induces an equivalence on homotopy groups, since

I=\pi_{1}(B/A)\rightarrow\pi_{1}(L_{B/A}^{\mathrm{pd}})\cong I/I^{[2]}=I
given by the identity.
∎

7.5.
Unobstructedness of Calabi–Yau varieties

Our results on injectivity of the period map on tangent fibres, unobstructedness of the period domain, and bootstrapping combine to give the following general criterion for unobstructedness:

Theorem 7.16
.

Let
X
be a smooth and proper scheme over
k
such that:

(1)

there is a decomposition
\RGamma_{\mathrm{cris}}(X/W)\simeq M\oplus N
such that the induced map

k\otimes_{W}N\rightarrow\RGamma_{\dR}(X/k)\rightarrow\RGamma(X,\mathcal{O}_{X})

is an equivalence;

(2)

the
W
-module
\pi_{0}\Map_{W}(N,M)
is torsion-free;

(3)

the map
\mathrm{H}^{d-1}(X,\Fil^{1}\Omega_{X/k})\rightarrow\mathrm{H}^{d-2}(X,\Omega^{1}_{X/k})
is surjective;

(4)

the map

\Ext^{2}_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}(\Omega^{1}_{X/k},\mathcal{O}_{X})\rightarrow\Hom_{k}(\mathrm{H}^{d-2}(X,\Omega^{1}_{X/k}),\,\mathrm{H}^{d}(X,\mathcal{O}_{X}))

is injective;

(5)

If
p=2
then
X
admits a flat lift to
W_{2}=W/4
.

Then deformations of
X
over local Artinian
W
-algebras are unobstructed.

Proof.

Let us first assume that
k
is infinite.
We will verify that
\pi_{0}\Def_{X}
satisfies the hypotheses of
Proposition

7.8
.

Let
A^{\prime}\twoheadrightarrow A
be an elementary extension in
\PDRing^{\mathrm{art}}_{W}
. By
Proposition

7.15
, we have a pullback square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces k}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sqz_{k}^{\mathrm{pd}}I[1]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}

in
\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd},\mathrm{art}}_{W}
. The period map
\per_{X}

induces a morphism of fibre sequences

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Def_{X}(A^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Def_{X}(A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Def_{X}(\sqz_{k}I[1])}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Per_{X}(A^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Per_{X}(A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Per_{X}(\sqz_{k}I[1])}

Let
x\in\pi_{0}\Def_{X}(A)
and consider its image
y\in\pi_{0}\Per_{X}(A)
.
Corollary

7.4
and
conditions (1) and (2) guarantee that
\Per_{X}
is unobstructed, so
y
admits a lift to
\pi_{0}\Per_{X}(A^{\prime})
. Its image

\ob_{\Per_{X}}(y,A^{\prime}\rightarrow A)
in
\pi_{0}\Per_{X}(\sqz_{k}I[1])
therefore vanishes.

Conditions (3) and (4), together with
Proposition

7.6
implies that the right vertical map is injective, so that also
\ob_{\Def_{X}}(x,A^{\prime}\rightarrow A)
vanishes. Using
Proposition

3.14
,
we conclude that
x
admits a lift to
\pi_{0}\Def_{X}(A^{\prime})
.

When
k
is finite,
write
\overline{X}
for the base change of
X
to the algebraic closure
\overline{k}
. Using flat base change
[
62
, 02KH]
, we check that
\overline{X}
satisfies the conditions of the theorem; it is therefore unobstructed.
The maps
\Def_{X}(A)\rightarrow\Def_{\overline{X}}(W(\overline{k})\otimes_{W(k)}A)
assemble into a map of formal moduli problems inducing an injection on obstruction spaces. We conclude by an argument as above that
X
is unobstructed.
∎

Remark 7.17
.

Using
Corollary

7.5
instead of
Corollary

7.4
, we can replace conditions (1) and (2) by the conjunction of the conditions:

(1’)

\mathrm{H}^{i}_{\mathrm{cris}}(X/W)
is torsion-free for all
i
;

(2’)

the map
\mathrm{H}^{i}_{\dR}(X/k)\rightarrow\mathrm{H}^{i}(X,\mathcal{O}_{X})
is surjective for all
i
.

Condition (5) in
Theorem

7.16
is always satisfied for Calabi–Yau varieties with degenerating Hodge–de Rham spectral sequence. This follows from a more general result of Achinger–Suh; we thank Sasha Petrov for bringing it to our attention.

Theorem 7.18

(Achinger–Suh)
.

Let
X
be a smooth, proper, geometrically irreducible scheme of dimension
d
over
k
such that

\omega_{X/k}\cong\mathcal{O}_{X}
. Assume moreover that the Hodge–de Rham spectral sequence for
X
degenerates at the
E_{1}
-page. Then
X
admits a flat lift to
W_{2}(k)
.

Proof.

Let
\ob(X,W_{2}(k))\in H^{2}(X,T_{X})
be the obstruction class to
lifting
X
to
W_{2}(k)
.

As the Hodge–de Rham spectral sequence degenerates, the conjugate spectral sequence also degenerates.
By
[
2
, Theorem 1.3]
, this implies that
\ob(X,W_{2}(k))
lies in the kernel of the cup product map

H^{2}(X,T_{X})\rightarrow\Map(H^{d-2}(X,\Omega^{1}_{X}),H^{d}(X,\mathcal{O}_{X}))
, which implies the claim by Serre duality.
∎

Combining
Theorem

7.16
and
Theorem

7.18
, we obtain:

Corollary 7.19

(
Theorem

A
)
.

Let
X
be a smooth and proper, geometrically irreducible scheme of dimension
d
over
k
such that

(1)

\omega_{X/k}\cong\mathcal{O}_{X}
;

(2)

\mathrm{H}^{i}_{\mathrm{cris}}(X/W)
is torsion-free for all
i
;

(3)

the Hodge–de Rham spectral sequence for
X
degenerates at the
E_{1}
-page.

Then deformations of
X
over
W
are unobstructed.

Proof.

We will verify that
X
satisfies conditions (1’) and (2’) from
Remark

7.17
and (3)–(5) from
Theorem

7.16
.

Condition (1’) is satisfied by assumption, and conditions (2’) and (3) follow from the degeneration of the Hodge–de Rham spectral sequence. Finally, using the assumption that the canonical bundle of
X
is trivial, we can identify the map in (4) with
the natural map

\Ext^{2}_{X}(\Omega^{1}_{X/k},\omega_{X/k})\rightarrow\Hom_{k}\big(\mathrm{H}^{d-2}(X,\Omega^{1}_{X/k}),\,\mathrm{H}^{d}(X,\omega_{X/k})\big),

which is an isomorphism by Serre duality.

For
p=2
, condition (5) follows from
[
2
, Theorem 1.3]
by Serre duality, as the conjugate spectral sequence degenerates.
∎

8.
Serre–Tate coordinates

The main aim of this section is to prove that the deformation space of a Bloch–Kato
2
-ordinary Calabi–Yau variety is a formal group of multiplicative type. To this end, we will use sheaves of multiplicative units to construct a second
period map.

8.1.
The multiplicative group

We begin by introducing the derived multiplicative group, following
[
45
, 25.1.5]
.
Consider the functor

\mathbf{Z}[-]\colon\Mod_{\mathbf{Z}}^{\mathrm{cn}}\rightarrow\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}

that preserves sifted colimits and maps a finite free abelian group
G
to its group ring
\mathbf{Z}[G]
. This functor preserves colimits, as it preserves coproducts of finite free abelian groups. We denote its right adjoint by

\mathbf{G}_{m}\colon\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}\rightarrow\Mod_{\mathbf{Z}}^{\mathrm{cn}}.

Proposition 8.1
.

The multiplicative group
\mathbf{G}_{m}
has the following properties:

(1)

the composition of
\mathbf{G}_{m}
with the forgetful functor
\Omega^{\infty}:\Mod_{\mathbf{Z}}^{\mathrm{cn}}\rightarrow\mathscr{S}
is corepresented by
\mathbf{Z}[t,t^{-1}]
;

(2)

the canonical arrow
A^{\times}\xrightarrow{\simeq}\mathbf{G}_{m}(A)
is an equivalence for all
A\in\CRing
;

(3)

the functor
\mathbf{G}_{m}
is left Kan extended from its restriction to the category
\CRing^{\sm}
of (discrete) smooth
\mathbf{Z}
-algebras.

Proof.

The first statement follows from the equivalences

\Omega^{\infty}\mathbf{G}_{m}(-)\simeq\Map_{\Mod_{\mathbf{Z}}^{\mathrm{cn}}}(\mathbf{Z},\mathbf{G}_{m}(-))\simeq\Map_{\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}}(\mathbf{Z}[\mathbf{Z}],-)

in
\Fun(\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}},\mathscr{S})
.
The second statement is a formal consequence of the first.
For the final statement, first note that
\Omega^{\infty}\mathbf{G}_{m}
is left Kan extended from
\CRing^{\sm}
, as
\mathbf{Z}[t,t^{-1}]
is smooth. The claim then follows from the colimit formula for left Kan extensions, using
that
\Omega^{\infty}
reflects sifted colimits and that for any
B\in\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}
, the
\infty
-category
\CRing^{\sm}\times_{\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}}\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}_{/B}
is sifted, as it admits finite coproducts.
∎

For every smooth
\mathbf{Z}
-algebra
A\in\CRing^{\sm}
and every discrete
A
-module
M
, we have a direct sum decomposition

(\sqz_{A}M)^{\times}=(A\oplus M)^{\times}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}A^{\times}\oplus M,\,\ (a,m)\mapsto(a,a^{-1}m),

which is functorial in
(A,M)
. By left Kan extension, this induces decompositions

(19)

\mathbf{G}_{m}(\sqz_{A}M)\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathbf{G}_{m}(A)\oplus M,

defining a direct sum decomposition in
\Fun(\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}\Mod^{\mathrm{cn}},\,\Mod^{\mathrm{cn}}_{\mathbf{Z}})
.

Definition 8.2

(
\dlog
)
.

The map
\dlog\colon\mathbf{G}_{m}\rightarrow L_{-/\mathbf{Z}}
in
\Fun(\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}},\Mod^{\mathrm{cn}}_{\mathbf{Z}})
is the left Kan extension of the following natural transformation on smooth
\mathbf{Z}
-algebras
A
:

\dlog\colon A^{\times}\rightarrow\Omega^{1}_{A/\mathbf{Z}}\colon a\mapsto\frac{da}{a}.

Recall from
Notation

3.17
that we write
\Map^{\prime}(x,y)
for the space of sections of a given map
y\rightarrow x
.

Proposition 8.3
.

Let
A
be an animated ring, and
M
a connective
A
-module. Then there is a commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Map^{\prime}_{\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}}(A,\sqz_{A}M)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Map_{\Mod_{A}^{\mathrm{cn}}}(L_{A/\mathbf{Z}},M)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Map^{\prime}_{\Mod_{\mathbf{Z}}^{\mathrm{cn}}}(\mathbf{G}_{m}(A),\mathbf{G}_{m}(\sqz_{A}M))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Map_{\Mod_{\mathbf{Z}}^{\mathrm{cn}}}(\mathbf{G}_{m}(A),M),}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{G}_{m}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dlog}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

functorial in
(A,M)\in\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}\Mod^{\mathrm{cn}}
. Here the upper horizontal map is the equivalence from
Corollary

2.18
, and the lower horizontal map is the equivalence induced by the decomposition (
19
).

Proof.

If
A
is smooth and
M
discrete, then all the mapping spaces in the statement are discrete and the resulting diagram of sets commutes by a direct inspection. The general statement follows by left Kan extension.
∎

If
X
is a scheme,
we denote the small étale site of
X
by
X_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}}
(cf.
Definition

2.44
).
In this section, we will simply write
\mathcal{O}_{X}
for the
étale structure sheaf of
X
. Let

\Mod(X_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}},\mathbf{Z}_{p}):=\lim_{n}\Mod(X_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}},\mathbf{Z}/p^{n})

be the stable
\infty
-category of
p
-adic sheaves on
X_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}}
.

Remark 8.4
.

We slightly abuse notation, as
\Mod(X_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}},\mathbf{Z}_{p})
is different from the
\infty
-category of sheaves of modules over the constant sheaf
\mathbf{Z}_{p}
in the sense of
Section

2.6
.

Objects of
\Mod(X_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}},\mathbf{Z}_{p})
are systems
M_{\bullet}
with
M_{n}
in
\Mod(X_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}},\mathbf{Z}/p^{n}\mathbf{Z})
together with equivalences
\mathbf{Z}/p^{n-1}\mathbf{Z}\otimes_{\mathbf{Z}/p^{n}\mathbf{Z}}M_{n}\simeq M_{n-1}
. Any map of schemes
f:X\rightarrow S
defines a pushforward functor

\mathrm{R}f_{\ast}\colon\Mod(X_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}},\mathbf{Z}_{p})\rightarrow\Mod(S_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}},\mathbf{Z}_{p}),

which admits a left adjoint

f^{-1}\colon\Mod(S_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}},\mathbf{Z}_{p})\rightarrow\Mod(X_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}},\mathbf{Z}_{p}).

We also have a limit-preserving completion functor

(-)^{\wedge}\colon\Mod(X_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}},\mathbf{Z})\rightarrow\Mod(X_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}},\mathbf{Z}_{p}),\,\ M\mapsto(\mathbf{Z}/p^{n}\mathbf{Z}\otimes_{\mathbf{Z}}M)_{n}.

Completion also preserves colimits, and restricts to an equivalence between
\Mod(X_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}},\mathbf{Z}_{p})
and the full subcategory of all
p
-complete objects in
\Mod(X_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}},\mathbf{Z})
, see
[
26
, 2.3.1.8]
.

Definition 8.5
.

The
p
-completed multiplicative units functor

\mathbf{G}^{\wedge}_{m}
is the
composite

\Shv(X_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}},\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}})\overset{\mathbf{G}_{m}}{\longrightarrow}\Mod(X_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}},\mathbf{Z})\overset{(-)^{\wedge}}{\longrightarrow}\Mod(X_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}},\mathbf{Z}_{p}).

We will need the following observation about the sheaf
\mathcal{W}
from
Notation

3.37
:

Proposition 8.6
.

Given any
A\in\CAlg^{\mathrm{an},\mathrm{art}}_{W}
, there are canonical equivalences

\mathbf{G}_{m}(f^{-1}\mathcal{W}\otimes_{W}A)\simeq f^{-1}\mathbf{G}_{m}(\mathcal{W}\otimes_{W}A)
and

\mathbf{G}^{\wedge}_{m}(f^{-1}\mathcal{W}\otimes_{W}A)\simeq f^{-1}\mathbf{G}^{\wedge}_{m}(\mathcal{W}\otimes_{W}A)
.

Proof.

As completion functor
\Mod(X_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}},\mathbf{Z})\rightarrow\Mod(X_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}},\mathbf{Z}_{p})

and the sheafification functor
\Shv(X_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}},\Mod_{\mathbf{Z}}^{\mathrm{cn}})\rightarrow\Shv(X_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}},\Mod_{\mathbf{Z}})\simeq\Mod(X_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}},\mathbf{Z})

are compatible with inverse image functors, it suffices to check that the following square commutes:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Shv(S_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}},\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Shv(S_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}},\Mod^{\mathrm{cn}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Shv(X_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}},\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Shv(X_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}},\Mod^{\mathrm{cn}})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{-1}}

This follows from
[
37
, Proposition 4.1.7]
as the functor
\mathbf{G}_{m}
is represented by the compact object
\mathbf{Z}[t,t^{-1}]
by
Proposition

8.1
.
∎

Remark 8.7
.

Alternatively, one could work in the pro-étale topology, and define
\mathbf{G}^{\wedge}_{m}(\mathcal{A})
as the
p
-adic completion of
\mathbf{G}_{m}(\mathcal{A})
in
\Shv(X_{\textrm{pro\'{e}t}},\mathbf{Z})
.

8.2.
Bloch–Kato
n
-ordinary varieties

Let
k
be a perfect field of characteristic
p
, and
X
a smooth and proper scheme over
k
. Write
B^{r}_{X/k}
for the image of
d\colon\Omega^{r-1}_{X/k}\rightarrow\Omega^{r}_{X/k}
. Following
[
8
]
, one says that
X
is
Bloch–Kato ordinary
if
\mathrm{H}^{i}(X,B^{r}_{X/k})
vanishes for all
i
and
r
. Inspired by Achinger and Zdanowicz
[
4
]
, we introduce a weaker ordinariness condition:

Definition 8.8
.

We call
X

Bloch–Kato
n
-ordinary
if
\mathrm{H}^{\ast}(X,B^{r}_{X/k})\cong 0

for all
r\leq n
.

Proposition 8.9
.

Assume that the first
n
slopes of the Newton and the Hodge polygon of
X
agree.
Then
X
is Bloch–Kato
n
-ordinary. If
\mathrm{H}^{\ast}_{\mathrm{cris}}(X/W)
is torsion-free, the
converse holds true.

Proof.

This follows from
[
8
, Lemma 7.1(iv)]
.
∎

Remark 8.10
.

In the terminology of Achinger and Zdanowicz
[
4
, Section 2.1]
, this becomes the following statement: If
\mathrm{H}^{\ast}_{\mathrm{cris}}(X/W)
is torsion-free, then
X
is Bloch–Kato
n
-ordinary if and only if the
F
-crystal
\mathrm{H}^{i}_{\mathrm{cris}}(X/W)
is
n
-ordinary

for all
i
.

Bloch–Kato
1
-ordinary and
2
-ordinary varieties will play an important role in
Theorem

B
. Writing
S=\Spec(k)
, and

f\colon X\rightarrow S
for the structure map, we have:

Proposition 8.11
.

The smooth and proper scheme
X
over
k
is Bloch–Kato
1
-ordinary if and only if the following natural map is an equivalence in
\Mod(S_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}},k)
:

\mathcal{O}_{S}\otimes_{\mathbf{F}_{p}}\mathrm{R}f_{\ast}\mathbf{F}_{p}\rightarrow\mathrm{R}f_{\ast}\mathcal{O}_{X}.

It is Bloch–Kato
2
-ordinary if and only if it is Bloch–Kato
1
-ordinary, and the map

\mathcal{O}_{S}\otimes_{\mathbf{Z}}\mathrm{R}f_{\ast}\mathbf{G}_{m,X}\rightarrow\mathrm{R}f_{\ast}\Omega^{1}_{X/k}

induced by
\dlog
is an equivalence.
Here
\mathcal{O}_{S}
is the étale structure
sheaf of
S=\Spec(k)
.

Proof.

It suffices to check that both maps induce equivalences on
\Spec(\overline{k})\rightarrow\Spec({k})
. Here, the claim
follows from
[
8
, Lemma 7.3(3)]
. We have used that all sheaves in sight are coconnective and hence hypercomplete, so we can work in the derived
\infty
-category of the ordinary category of discrete sheaves
of
k
-modules on
S_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}}
.

∎

Remark 8.12
.

For
k=\overline{k}
, the sheaf
\mathcal{O}_{S}
in
Proposition

8.11
is
the constant sheaf
\overline{k}
.

8.3.
Construction of Serre–Tate coordinates

Let
f:X\rightarrow\Spec(k)
be a smooth, proper, and Bloch–Kato
1
-ordinary scheme over a perfect field
k
of characteristic
p
. In this section, we will construct a map from the formal moduli problem
\Def_{X}
to an auxiliary (derived) formal group
\ST^{\prime}_{X}
. In the subsequent
Section

8.5
, we will then construct the Serre-Tate period domain
\ST_{X}
from

\ST^{\prime}_{X}
.
The key ingredient for our construction is the fact for any deformation

\mathcal{B}
of
\mathcal{O}_{X}
over
A\in\CAlg_{W}^{\mathrm{an},\mathrm{art}}
,
the cohomology of the
p
-completed sheaf of principal units
\fib(\mathbf{G}_{m}^{\wedge}(\mathcal{B})\rightarrow\mathbf{G}_{m}^{\wedge}(\mathcal{O}_{X}))

only depends on
A
, see
Remark

8.22
.

To algebraise our formal lifts, we will also want to lift line bundles. To this end, we will construct a formal moduli problem
\Def_{X,\lambda}
for any class
\lambda
in
\Pic(X)
, encoding deformations of the pair
(X,\lambda)
. We will then construct a map from
\Def_{X,\lambda}
to an
auxiliary (derived)
formal group
\ST^{\prime}_{X,\lambda}
(and its variant
\ST_{X,\lambda}
in
Section

8.5
).

But first, let us construct the formal moduli problem
\widetilde{\Def}_{X,\lambda}
.

Construction 8.13

(
\widetilde{\Def}_{X,\lambda}
)
.

Fix a map
\lambda\colon\mathbf{Z}[-1]\rightarrow\mathbf{G}_{m,X}
in
\Shv(X_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}},\Mod_{\mathbf{Z}})
,
where
\mathbf{G}_{m,X}:=\mathbf{G}_{m}(\mathcal{O}_{X})
.
Recall from
Construction

3.39
the
\infty
-category

\widetilde{\mathscr{C}}_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}}:=\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}_{W}\times_{\Fun(\Delta^{1},\Shv(X_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}},\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}))}\Fun(\Delta^{2},\Shv(X_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}},\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}))

whose objects are pairs consisting of

(1)

an object
A
in
\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}
;

(2)

a morphism
A\rightarrow f^{-1}\mathcal{W}\otimes_{W}{A}\rightarrow\mathcal{B}
in
\Shv(X_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}},\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}})

Using the functor
\mathbf{G}_{m}\colon\Shv(X_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}},\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}})\rightarrow\Shv(X_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}},\Mod_{\mathbf{Z}})
above, we define

\widetilde{\mathscr{C}}_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}}^{\prime}:=\widetilde{\mathscr{C}}_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}}\times_{\Shv(X_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}},\Mod_{\mathbf{Z}})}\Fun(\Delta^{1},\Shv(X_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}},\Mod_{\mathbf{Z}}))\times_{\Shv(X_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}},\Mod_{\mathbf{Z}})}\{\mathbf{Z}[-1]\}

as the
\infty
-category of triples consisting of
A
and
(A\rightarrow f^{-1}\mathcal{W}\otimes_{W}{A}\rightarrow\mathcal{B})
as in (1)–(2) above,
together with

(3)

a map
\mathbf{Z}[-1]\rightarrow\mathbf{G}_{m}(\mathcal{B})
in
\Mod(X_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}},\mathbf{Z})
.

We have projections

\widetilde{\mathscr{C}}_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}}^{\prime}\overset{q^{\prime}}{\rightarrow}\widetilde{\mathscr{C}}_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}}\overset{q}{\rightarrow}\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}},

and we readily check that every arrow in
\widetilde{\mathscr{C}}_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}}^{\prime}
is
q^{\prime}
-cocartesian.
The triple
(k,\mathcal{O}_{X},\lambda)
fixed above determines an object of
\widetilde{\mathscr{C}}_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}}^{\prime}
, and we obtain left fibrations

(\widetilde{\mathscr{C}}_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}}^{\prime})^{\mathrm{cocart}}_{/(k,\mathcal{O}_{X},\lambda)}\overset{q^{\prime}}{\longrightarrow}(\widetilde{\mathscr{C}}_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}})^{\mathrm{cocart}}_{/(k,\mathcal{O}_{X})}\overset{q}{\longrightarrow}\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}_{/k}

by
[
44
, Corollary 2.4.2.5]
; the notation
(-)^{\mathrm{cocart}}
is used as in
Construction

3.22
.
These straighten to a morphism
\widetilde{\Def}_{X,\lambda}\rightarrow\widetilde{\Def}_{X}\simeq{\Def}_{X}

in
\Fun(\CAlg^{\mathrm{an},\mathrm{art}}_{W},\widehat{\mathscr{S}})
, where we have used the equivalence

in
Proposition

3.40
.

Remark 8.14

(Informal description)
.

Objects of
\widetilde{\Def}_{X,\lambda}(A)
are
pairs consisting of a composition

\left(A\rightarrow f^{-1}\mathcal{W}\otimes_{W}A\rightarrow\mathcal{B}\right)
lifting
\left(k\rightarrow f^{-1}\mathcal{W}\otimes_{W}k\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{X})\right)

and a commutative triangle in
\Mod(X_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}},\mathbf{Z})
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{G}_{m}(\mathcal{B})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Z}[-1]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{G}_{m}(\mathcal{O}_{X}).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{\lambda}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda}

Lemma 8.15
.

The functor
\widetilde{\Def}_{X,\lambda}
is a formal moduli problem.

Proof.

This follows from
Proposition

3.25
,
Proposition

3.40
,
and the fact that for any
A
in
\CAlg^{\mathrm{an},\mathrm{art}}_{W}
and
\mathcal{B}
in
\Shv(X_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}},\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}_{A})
, the functor

\CAlg^{\mathrm{an},\mathrm{art}}_{A}\rightarrow\Mod(X_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}},\mathbf{Z}),\,A^{\prime}\mapsto\mathbf{G}_{m}(A^{\prime}\otimes_{A}\mathcal{B})

preserves pullbacks of pairs
A_{0}\rightarrow A_{01},A_{1}\rightarrow A_{01}
inducing surjections on
\pi_{0}
. Indeed, let us fix such a pair; write
(\ast)
for the associated pullback square in

\CAlg^{\mathrm{an},\mathrm{art}}_{A}
.
For any étale open
U\rightarrow X
,

\pi_{0}
-surjectivity implies that
(\ast)\otimes_{A}\mathcal{B}(U)
is a pullback in
\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}_{A}
whose leg
A_{0}\otimes_{A}\mathcal{B}(U)\rightarrow A_{01}\otimes_{A}\mathcal{B}(U)
(and also
A_{1}\otimes_{A}\mathcal{B}(U)\rightarrow A_{01}\otimes_{A}\mathcal{B}(U)
) induces a surjection with nilpotent kernels on
\pi_{0}
.

A truncated geometric series shows that
\mathbf{G}_{m}(A_{0}\otimes_{A}\mathcal{B}(U))\rightarrow\mathbf{G}_{m}(A_{01}\otimes_{A}\mathcal{B}(U))
is also surjective on
\pi_{0}
. This implies that
\mathbf{G}_{m}((\ast)\otimes\mathcal{B}(U))
is a pullback, i.e. a pushout, in
\Mod_{\mathbf{Z}}
-valued presheaves.
Its sheafification
\mathbf{G}_{m}((\ast)\otimes\mathcal{B})
is a pushout in
\Mod_{\mathbf{Z}}
-sheaves and so a pullback by stability.
∎

Construction 8.16

(
\ST^{\prime}_{X,\lambda}
and
\ST^{\prime}_{X}
)
.

Using the composite functor

\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}\xrightarrow{\ A\mapsto f^{-1}\mathcal{W}\otimes_{W}A\ }\Shv(X_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}},\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}})\xrightarrow{\mathbf{G}^{\wedge}_{m}}\Mod(X_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}},\mathbf{Z}_{p})\xrightarrow{\mathrm{R}f_{\ast}\ }\Shv(S_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}},\mathbf{Z}_{p}),

we define the
\infty
-category

\mathscr{D}:=\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}\times_{\Mod(S_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}},\mathbf{Z}_{p})}\Fun(\Delta^{1},\Mod(S_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}},\mathbf{Z}_{p}))

whose objects are pairs consisting of

(1)

an object
A
in
\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}
;

(2)

a map
\mathrm{R}f_{\ast}\mathbf{G}^{\wedge}_{m}(f^{-1}\mathcal{W}\otimes_{W}A)\rightarrow N
in

\Mod(S_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}},\mathbf{Z}_{p})
.

We also define the
\infty
-category

\mathscr{D}^{\prime}:=\mathscr{D}\times_{\Mod(S_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}},\mathbf{Z}_{p})}\Fun(\Delta^{1},\Mod(S_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}},\mathbf{Z}_{p}))\times_{\Mod(S_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}},\mathbf{Z}_{p})}\{f_{\ast}\mathbf{Z}_{p}[-1]\}

whose objects are pairs as in (1)–(2) above, together with

(3)

a map
\mathrm{R}f_{\ast}\mathbf{Z}_{p}[-1]\rightarrow N
in
\Mod(S_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}},\mathbf{Z}_{p})
.

Consider the projection maps
\mathscr{D}^{\prime}\overset{s^{\prime}}{\longrightarrow}\mathscr{D}\overset{s}{\longrightarrow}\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}.

A map in
\mathscr{D}
is
s
-cocartesian if and only if the underlying square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{R}f_{\ast}\mathbf{G}^{\wedge}_{m}(f^{-1}\mathcal{W}\otimes_{W}A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{R}f_{\ast}\mathbf{G}^{\wedge}_{m}(f^{-1}\mathcal{W}\otimes_{W}A^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ulcorner}

is a pushout in
\Mod(S_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}},\mathbf{Z}_{p})
. Every map in
\mathscr{D}^{\prime}
is
s^{\prime}
-cocartesian.

Let
\mathscr{D}^{\mathrm{cocart}}
be the subcategory of
\mathscr{D}
spanned by all objects and only
s
-cocartesian morphisms. Write
\mathscr{D}^{\prime\mathrm{cocart}}
for the subcategory of
\mathscr{D}^{\prime}
spanned by
(s\circ s^{\prime})
-cocartesian morphisms. Set
\mathbf{G}^{\wedge}_{m,X}:=\mathbf{G}_{m}^{\wedge}(\mathcal{O}_{X})
.
As in
Construction

3.15
, we have left fibrations

\mathscr{D}^{\prime\mathrm{cocart}}_{/(k,0\rightarrow\mathrm{R}f_{\ast}\mathbf{G}^{\wedge}_{m,X},\widehat{\lambda})}\longrightarrow\mathscr{D}^{\mathrm{cocart}}_{/(k,0\rightarrow\mathrm{R}f_{\ast}\mathbf{G}^{\wedge}_{m,X})}\longrightarrow\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}_{/k};

we have used
Proposition

8.6
and that
k
is perfect to see
\mathrm{R}f_{\ast}\mathbf{G}^{\wedge}_{m}(f^{-1}\mathcal{W}\otimes_{W}k)\simeq 0
.

Straightening and restricting gives a morphism
\ST_{X,\lambda}^{\prime}\rightarrow\ST_{X}^{\prime}
in
\Fun(\CAlg^{\mathrm{an},\mathrm{art}}_{W},\widehat{\mathscr{S}})
.

Remark 8.17

(Informal description)
.

Objects in
\ST^{\prime}_{X}(A)
are fibre sequences

\mathrm{R}f_{\ast}\mathbf{G}^{\wedge}_{m}(f^{-1}\mathcal{W}\otimes_{W}A)\longrightarrow N\longrightarrow\mathrm{R}f_{\ast}\mathbf{G}^{\wedge}_{m,X}.

in
\Mod(S_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}},\mathbf{Z}_{p})
. Objects in
\ST^{\prime}_{X,\lambda}(A)
are such fibre sequences with a lift
\tilde{\lambda}
of
\lambda
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{R}f_{\ast}\mathbf{Z}_{p}[-1]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{R}f_{\ast}\mathbf{G}^{\wedge}_{m}(f^{-1}\mathcal{W}\otimes_{W}A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{R}f_{\ast}\mathbf{G}^{\wedge}_{m,X}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{\lambda}}

We can give a more direct description of
\ST^{\prime}_{X}
and
\ST^{\prime}_{X,\lambda}
. To this end, let us define
P_{X,\lambda}
in
\Mod(X_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}},\mathbf{Z}_{p})
by the cofibre sequence:

(20)

\mathbf{Z}_{p}[-1]\overset{\lambda}{\longrightarrow}\mathbf{G}^{\wedge}_{m,X}\longrightarrow P_{X,\lambda}.

Lemma 8.18
.

There is a commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ST^{\prime}_{X,\lambda}(-)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Map_{\Mod(S_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}},\mathbf{Z}_{p})}\big(\mathrm{R}f_{\ast}P_{X,\lambda},\,\mathrm{R}f_{\ast}\mathbf{Z}_{p}\otimes_{\mathbf{Z}}\mathbf{G}_{m}(\mathscr{W}\otimes_{W}-)[1]\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ST^{\prime}_{X}(-)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Map_{\Mod(S_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}},\mathbf{Z}_{p})}\big(\mathrm{R}f_{\ast}\mathbf{G}^{\wedge}_{m,X},\,\mathrm{R}f_{\ast}\mathbf{Z}_{p}\otimes_{\mathbf{Z}}\mathbf{G}_{m}(\mathscr{W}\otimes_{W}-)[1]\big)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

in
\Fun(\CAlg^{\mathrm{an},\mathrm{art}}_{W},\widehat{\mathscr{S}})
, in which the horizontal maps are equivalences.

Proof.

This follows from the definitions, together with the fact that the map

(\mathrm{R}f_{\ast}\mathbf{Z}_{p})\otimes_{\mathbf{Z}}\mathbf{G}_{m}(\mathscr{W}\otimes_{W}A)\longrightarrow\mathrm{R}f_{\ast}\mathbf{G}^{\wedge}_{m}(f^{-1}\mathscr{W}\otimes_{W}A)

is an equivalence in
\Mod(S_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}},\mathbf{Z}_{p})
by
Proposition

8.6
and the projection formula in
[
62
, 0F0F]
. We have used that
\mathbf{G}_{m}(A)
is perfect as
A\in\CAlg^{\mathrm{an},\mathrm{art}}_{W}
is Artinian.
∎

Corollary 8.19
.

\ST^{\prime}_{X}
and
\ST^{\prime}_{X,\lambda}
are formal moduli problems.

Proof.

As in the proof of
Lemma

8.15
, the functor
\mathbf{G}_{m}(\mathscr{W}(U)\otimes_{W}-)
sends

\pi_{0}
-surjective maps
A\rightarrow A^{\prime}
in
\CAlg^{\mathrm{an},\mathrm{art}}_{W}
to
\pi_{0}
-surjections in
\Mod_{\mathbf{Z}}
for each
U\in S_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}}
. The claim follows as pullbacks in sheaves are computed in presheaves.
∎

We now come to the construction of the maps
\st^{\prime}_{X,\lambda}
and
\st^{\prime}_{X}
. The key input is:

Proposition 8.20
.

Let
f\colon X\rightarrow S=\Spec(k)
be smooth, proper, and Bloch–Kato
1
-ordinary. Fix
a morphism
A^{\prime}\rightarrow A
in
\CAlg^{\mathrm{an},\mathrm{art}}_{W}
. Given a
pushout square

(21)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{-1}\mathscr{W}\otimes_{W}A^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{-1}\mathscr{W}\otimes_{W}A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{B}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ulcorner}

in
\Shv(X_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}},\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}})
, the induced square in
\Mod(S_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}},\mathbf{Z}_{p})
is a pullback:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{R}f_{\ast}\mathbf{G}^{\wedge}_{m}(f^{-1}\mathscr{W}\otimes_{W}A^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{R}f_{\ast}\mathbf{G}^{\wedge}_{m}(f^{-1}\mathscr{W}\otimes_{W}A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{R}f_{\ast}\mathbf{G}^{\wedge}_{m}(\mathcal{B}^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{R}f_{\ast}\mathbf{G}^{\wedge}_{m}(\mathcal{B}).}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}

Proof.

Call a square in
\Shv(X_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}},\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}})

good
if
applying
\mathbf{G}^{\wedge}_{m}
to it gives a pullback.

First, assume that
A^{\prime}\rightarrow A
arises as in
Corollary

3.4
as a pullback of a cospan
A\rightarrow\sqz_{k}(V[n+1])\leftarrow k
.
Then

A^{\prime}\rightarrow A
,
\mathcal{B}^{\prime}\rightarrow\mathcal{B}
sit in compatible
pullback squares

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{-1}\mathscr{W}\otimes_{W}A^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{-1}\mathscr{W}\otimes_{W}A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{B}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{-1}\mathscr{W}\otimes_{W}k}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{-1}\mathscr{W}\otimes_{W}\sqz_{k}(V[n+1])}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}_{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sqz_{k}(V[n+1])\otimes_{k}\mathcal{O}_{X}.}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}

These squares are both good by the same argument as in
Lemma

8.15
, as the functor
\mathrm{R}f_{\ast}\mathbf{G}_{m}(-):\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}\rightarrow\Mod^{\mathrm{cn}}
preserves limits and the lower horizontal maps are
\pi_{0}
-surjective.
We now observe that the square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{R}f_{\ast}\mathbf{G}^{\wedge}_{m}(f^{-1}\mathscr{W}\otimes_{W}k)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{R}f_{\ast}\mathbf{G}^{\wedge}_{m}(f^{-1}\mathscr{W}\otimes_{W}\sqz_{k}(V[n+1]))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{R}f_{\ast}\mathbf{G}^{\wedge}_{m}(\mathcal{O}_{X})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{R}f_{\ast}\mathbf{G}^{\wedge}_{m}(\sqz_{k}(V[n+1])\otimes_{k}\mathcal{O}_{X})}

is a pullback square in
\Mod(S_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}},\mathbf{Z}_{p})
. Indeed, the decomposition (
19
) above shows
that the induced map between the cofibres of the horizontal maps is given by

V\otimes_{k}\mathcal{O}_{S}\otimes_{k}\mathrm{R}f_{\ast}k\rightarrow V\otimes_{k}\mathrm{R}f_{\ast}\mathcal{O}_{X},

which is an equivalence by
1
-ordinariness, see
Proposition

8.11
.
The ‘pasting law for pullbacks’ shows that the proposition holds true for all
A^{\prime}\rightarrow A
as above.

Inducting on the length via
Corollary

3.4
proves proposition for all
\pi_{0}
-surjections.

We can write a general
f:A^{\prime}\rightarrow A
as a composition
A^{\prime}\rightarrow A^{\prime}\otimes_{W}A\rightarrow A
, where the first map is the inclusion and the second map is given by
(f,\id_{A})
. By our previous considerations, it then suffices to prove that the square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{-1}\mathscr{W}\otimes_{W}A^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{-1}\mathscr{W}\otimes_{W}(A^{\prime}\otimes_{W}A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{B}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{B}^{\prime}\otimes_{W}A.}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ulcorner}

is good. Let us write
A
as a pullback of a cospan
A_{0}\rightarrow\sqz_{k}(V[n+1])\leftarrow k
as in
Corollary

3.4
, where
A_{0}
has shorter length.
We now consider the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{-1}\mathscr{W}\otimes_{W}A^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{-1}\mathscr{W}\otimes_{W}(A^{\prime}\otimes_{W}A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{-1}\mathscr{W}\otimes_{W}(A^{\prime}\otimes_{W}A_{0})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{B}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{B}^{\prime}\otimes_{W}A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{B}^{\prime}\otimes_{W}A_{0}.}

The right square is good by
\pi_{0}
-surjectivity of

A^{\prime}\otimes_{W}A\rightarrow A^{\prime}\otimes_{W}A_{0}
.
As the outer square is good by induction on the length, the left square is good.
∎

Construction 8.21

(The maps
\st_{X}^{\prime}
and
\st_{X,\lambda}^{\prime}
)
.

Let
X\rightarrow S
be smooth, proper and Bloch–Kato
1
-ordinary, and let
\lambda\colon\mathbf{Z}[-1]\rightarrow\mathbf{G}_{m,X}
be a map in
\Mod(X_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}},\mathbf{Z})
.

By construction, we have the following commutative square over
\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathscr{C}}_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{D}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathscr{C}}_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{D}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{R}f_{\ast}\mathbf{G}^{\wedge}_{m}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{R}f_{\ast}\mathbf{G}^{\wedge}_{m}}

The lower horizontal map first
sends
(A\rightarrow f^{-1}\mathcal{W}\otimes_{W}A\rightarrow\mathcal{B})
to
(f^{-1}\mathcal{W}\otimes_{W}A\rightarrow\mathcal{B})
and then applies
\mathrm{R}f_{\ast}\mathbf{G}^{\wedge}_{m}
.

Restricting to fibres over

\CAlg^{\mathrm{an},\mathrm{art}}_{W}
and only the cocartesian
morphisms
, we obtain a diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathscr{C}}_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}}^{\prime\mathrm{cocart},\mathrm{art}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{D}^{\prime\mathrm{cocart},\mathrm{art}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathscr{C}}_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}}^{\mathrm{cocart},\mathrm{art}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{D}^{\mathrm{cocart},\mathrm{art}},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{R}f_{\ast}\mathbf{G}^{\wedge}_{m}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{R}f_{\ast}\mathbf{G}^{\wedge}_{m}}

where we have used that by

Proposition

8.20
, all functors in the above square preserve cocartesian edges over
\CAlg^{\mathrm{an},\mathrm{art}}_{W}
.
Taking slice categories, straightening, and using
Proposition

3.40
, we obtain a commutative square of formal moduli problems in
\Moduli^{\mathrm{an}}_{W}
:

(22)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\Def}_{X,\lambda}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ST^{\prime}_{X,\lambda}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Def_{X}\simeq\widetilde{\Def}_{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ST^{\prime}_{X}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\st^{\prime}_{X,\lambda}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\st^{\prime}_{X}}

Remark 8.22

(Informal description)
.

The map
\st^{\prime}_{X,\lambda}(A)
takes a

pair

\big(\mathcal{B},\,\mathbf{Z}[-1]\overset{\tilde{\lambda}}{\rightarrow}\mathbf{G}_{m}(\mathcal{B})\big)

in
\widetilde{\Def}_{X,\lambda}(A)
to the resulting diagram in
\Mod(S_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}},\mathbf{Z}_{p})
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{R}f_{\ast}\mathbf{Z}_{p}[-1]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{R}f_{\ast}\mathbf{G}^{\wedge}_{m}(f^{-1}\mathcal{W}\otimes_{W}A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{R}f_{\ast}\mathbf{G}^{\wedge}_{m}(\mathcal{B})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{R}f_{\ast}\mathbf{G}^{\wedge}_{m,X}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{\lambda}}

The horizontal sequence is a fibre sequence by
Proposition

8.20
as
\mathbf{G}^{\wedge}_{m,X}(k)\simeq 0
.

8.4.
Tangent to the Serre–Tate map

Let
X
be as in
Section

8.3
.
We will now compute the effect on tangent fibres of the maps

\st^{\prime}_{X}
and
\st^{\prime}_{X,\lambda}
in
Construction

8.21
.

Notation 8.23
.

Fix
V\in\Mod_{k}^{\mathrm{cn}}
perfect and
write
A:=\sqz_{k}V
for and
\mathcal{B}:=A\otimes_{k}\mathcal{O}_{X}
.

The base point in the space
\ST^{\prime}_{X}(A)
corresponds to the fibre sequence

(23)

\mathrm{R}f_{\ast}\mathbf{G}^{\wedge}_{m}(f^{-1}\mathcal{W}\otimes_{W}A)\longrightarrow\mathrm{R}f_{\ast}\mathbf{G}^{\wedge}_{m}(\mathcal{B})\longrightarrow\mathrm{R}f_{\ast}\mathbf{G}^{\wedge}_{m,X}.

The canonical section of
A\rightarrow k
induces a section

\mathrm{R}f_{\ast}\mathbf{G}^{\wedge}_{m,X}\rightarrow\mathrm{R}f_{\ast}\mathbf{G}^{\wedge}_{m}(\mathcal{B})
of the right map in (
23
),
which in turn induces an equivalence

\Omega\ST^{\prime}_{X}(A)\overset{\simeq}{\rightarrow}\Map^{\prime}_{\Mod(S_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}},\mathbf{Z}_{p})}\big(\mathrm{R}f_{\ast}\mathbf{G}^{\wedge}_{m,X},\,\mathrm{R}f_{\ast}\mathbf{G}^{\wedge}_{m}(\mathcal{B})\big),

Informally, it sends an automorphism
\alpha
of the
extension (
23
) to the composite

\mathrm{R}f_{\ast}\mathbf{G}^{\wedge}_{m,X}\rightarrow\mathrm{R}f_{\ast}\mathbf{G}^{\wedge}_{m}(\mathcal{B})\overset{\alpha}{\rightarrow}\mathrm{R}f_{\ast}\mathbf{G}^{\wedge}_{m}(\mathcal{B})

in
\Mod(S_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}},\mathbf{Z}_{p})
. Unravelling the construction of
\st^{\prime}_{X}
(cf.
Construction

8.21
),
we find:

Lemma 8.24
.

There is a commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega\Def_{X}(A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Map^{\prime}_{\Shv(X_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}},\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}_{k})}\big(\mathcal{O}_{X},\,\mathcal{B}\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega\ST^{\prime}_{X}(A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Map^{\prime}_{\Mod(S_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}},\mathbf{Z}_{p})}\big(\mathrm{R}f_{\ast}\mathbf{G}^{\wedge}_{m,X},\mathrm{R}f_{\ast}\mathbf{G}^{\wedge}_{m}(\mathcal{B})\big).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\st^{\prime}_{X}(A)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{R}f_{\ast}\mathbf{G}^{\wedge}_{m}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

The top equivalence appears in
Proposition

3.24
; the bottom equivalence is as above.

Using this we can now compute the

effect of
\st^{\prime}_{X}
on tangent fibres:

Proposition 8.25

(Tangent to
\st^{\prime}_{X}
)
.

Assume
k
is algebraically closed. Then there is a commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega\Def_{X}(A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Map_{\Mod(X_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}},\mathcal{O}_{X})}(\Omega^{1}_{X/k},\,V\otimes_{k}\mathcal{O}_{X})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega\ST^{\prime}_{X}(A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Map_{k}\!\big(k\otimes_{\mathbf{Z}}\RGamma(X_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}},\mathbf{G}_{m,X}),\,V\otimes_{k}\RGamma(X,\mathcal{O}_{X})\big)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\st^{\prime}_{X}(A)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dlog}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

in which the horizontal maps are equivalences.

Proof.

Proposition

8.3
gives rise to a commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Map^{\prime}_{\Shv(X_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}},\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}_{k})}(\mathcal{O}_{X},\mathcal{B})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Map_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}(L_{X/k},\,\mathcal{O}_{X})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Map^{\prime}_{\Mod(X_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}},\mathbf{Z})}(\mathbf{G}_{m,X},\mathbf{G}_{m}(\mathcal{B}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Map_{\Mod(X_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}},\mathbf{Z})}(\mathbf{G}_{m,X},\,V\otimes_{k}\mathcal{O}_{X}).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{G}_{m}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dlog}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

Postcomposing with
Rf_{\ast}
, we obtain a commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Map^{\prime}_{\Shv(X_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}},\CAlg^{\mathrm{an}}_{k})}(\mathcal{O}_{X},\mathcal{B})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Map_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}(L_{X/k},\,\mathcal{O}_{X})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Map^{\prime}(\mathrm{R}f_{\ast}\mathbf{G}^{\wedge}_{m,X},\mathrm{R}f_{\ast}\mathbf{G}^{\wedge}_{m}(\mathcal{B}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Map(\mathrm{R}f_{\ast}\mathbf{G}^{\wedge}_{m,X},\,V\otimes_{k}\mathrm{R}f_{\ast}\mathcal{O}_{X}).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{R}f_{\ast}\mathbf{G}^{\wedge}_{m}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

Here the bottom mapping spaces are computed in
\Mod(S_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}},\mathbf{Z}_{p})
. As
k
is algebraically closed and
V\otimes_{k}\mathcal{O}_{X}
is killed by
p
, the bottom right space is
equivalent to

\Map_{k}\!\big(k\otimes_{\mathbf{Z}}\RGamma(X_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}},\mathbf{G}_{m,X}),\,V\otimes_{k}\RGamma(X,\mathcal{O}_{X})\big).

Combining this with
Lemma

8.24
gives the result.
∎

Next, we compute the effect of the map
\st^{\prime}_{X,\lambda}:\widetilde{\Def}_{X,\lambda}\rightarrow\ST^{\prime}_{X,\lambda}
on
tangent fibres.

To begin with, note that the restriction of any formal moduli problem to
k
-algebras is naturally pointed, and therefore defines a functor
\CAlg^{\mathrm{an},\mathrm{art}}_{k}\rightarrow\mathscr{S}_{\ast}
. We write
\widetilde{\Def}_{\lambda}
for the fibre of the restriction of
\widetilde{\Def}_{X,\lambda}\rightarrow\widetilde{\Def}_{X}
to
\CAlg^{\mathrm{an},\mathrm{art}}_{k}
, and define
\ST_{\lambda}
analogously.
Evaluating at
A=\sqz_{k}V
, we obtain a commutative diagram

(24)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\Def}_{\lambda}(A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\Def}_{X,\lambda}(A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\Def}_{X}(A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ST^{\prime}_{\lambda}(A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ST^{\prime}_{X,\lambda}(A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ST^{\prime}_{X}(A)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\st^{\prime}_{\lambda}(A)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\st^{\prime}_{X,\lambda}(A)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\st^{\prime}_{X}(A)}

of pointed spaces, in which both rows are fibre sequences.

Proposition 8.26

(Tangent to
\st^{\prime}_{\lambda}
)
.

There is a commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\Def}_{\lambda}(A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Map_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}(\mathcal{O}_{X},V[1]\otimes_{k}\mathcal{O}_{X})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ST^{\prime}_{\lambda}(A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Map_{\mathcal{O}_{S}}\big(\mathrm{R}f_{\ast}\mathcal{O}_{X},V[1]\otimes_{k}\mathrm{R}f_{\ast}\mathcal{O}_{X}\big)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\st^{\prime}_{\lambda}(A)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{R}f_{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

in which the horizontal maps are equivalences.

Proof.

Using the decomposition
\mathbf{G}_{m}(\mathcal{B})\simeq\mathbf{G}_{m,X}\oplus(V\otimes_{k}\mathcal{O}_{X})
induced by (
19
), we find a commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\Def}_{\lambda}(A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Map_{\Mod(X_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}},\mathbf{Z})}\big(\mathbf{Z}[-1],\,V\otimes_{k}\mathcal{O}_{X}\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ST^{\prime}_{\lambda}(A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Map_{\Mod(S_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}},\mathbf{Z}_{p})}\big(\mathrm{R}f_{\ast}\mathbf{Z}_{p}[-1],\,V\otimes_{k}\mathrm{R}f_{\ast}\mathcal{O}_{X}\big)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\st^{\prime}_{\lambda}(A)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

in which the horizontal maps are equivalences, and the right map is induced by

\Mod(X_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}},\mathbf{Z})\longrightarrow\Mod(X_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}},\mathbf{Z}_{p})\overset{\mathrm{R}f_{\ast}}{\longrightarrow}\Mod(S_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}},\mathbf{Z}_{p}).

The lower right space is equivalent to

\Map_{\mathcal{O}_{S}}\big(\mathrm{R}f_{\ast}\mathcal{O}_{X},V[1]\otimes_{k}\mathrm{R}f_{\ast}\mathcal{O}_{X}\big)
by

Proposition

8.11
.
∎

8.5.
Canonical coordinates for ordinary Calabi–Yau varieties

Let
X/k
be proper, smooth of relative dimension
d
, and Bloch–Kato
1
-ordinary, where
k
is a perfect field of characteristic
p
. Let
\lambda\colon\mathbf{Z}[-1]\rightarrow\mathbf{G}_{m,X}
in
\Mod(X_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}},\mathbf{Z})
.
Consider the truncation
H:=\tau_{\leq-d}\mathrm{R}f_{\ast}\mathbf{Z}_{p}
in
\Mod(S_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}},\mathbf{Z}_{p})
.
Proposition

8.11
shows that
H
is concentrated in cohomological degree
d
, and

that its stalk at
\bar{k}
is
\mathrm{H}^{d}(X_{\bar{k},\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}},\mathbf{Z}_{p})[-d]
.

Definition 8.27

(Serre–Tate period domain and period maps)
.

Consider the formal moduli problems
\ST_{X}
and
\ST_{X,\lambda}
defined by

\displaystyle\ST_{X}(A)

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\Map_{\Mod(S_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}},\mathbf{Z}_{p})}\big(\mathrm{R}f_{\ast}\widehat{\mathbf{G}}_{m,X},\,H\otimes_{\mathbf{Z}}{\mathbf{G}}_{m}(\mathcal{W}\otimes_{W}A)[1]\big)

\displaystyle\ST_{X,\lambda}(A)

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\Map_{\Mod(S_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}},\mathbf{Z}_{p})}\big(\mathrm{R}f_{\ast}P_{X,\lambda},\ \,H\otimes_{\mathbf{Z}}{\mathbf{G}}_{m}(\mathcal{W}\otimes_{W}A)[1]\big),

where
P_{X,\lambda}
is defined by the cofibre sequence (
20
) above.

The truncation map,
Lemma

8.18
, and
Proposition

3.40
give a commutative diagram in
\Moduli^{\mathrm{an}}_{W}
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\Def}_{X,\lambda}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ST^{\prime}_{X,\lambda}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ST_{X,\lambda}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Def_{X}\simeq\widetilde{\Def}_{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ST^{\prime}_{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ST_{X}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\st^{\prime}_{X,\lambda}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\st^{\prime}_{X}}

The horizontal compositions are the
Serre–Tate period maps

\st_{X,\lambda}
and
\st_{X}
.

Theorem 8.28

(Theorem B)
.

Let
X/k
be smooth, proper, and geometrically irreducible
of dimension
d
with
\omega_{X}\cong\mathcal{O}_{X}
. Let
\lambda\colon\mathbf{Z}[-1]\rightarrow\mathbf{G}_{m,X}
in
\Mod(X_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}},\mathbf{Z})
. Assume moreover that

(1)

X
is Bloch–Kato
2
-ordinary;

(2)

\mathrm{H}^{d}(X_{\bar{k},\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}},\mathbf{Z}_{p})
is torsion-free.

Then
\st_{X,\lambda}
and
\st_{X}
are equivalences.

Proof.

Using
Proposition

3.9
and the fibre sequences in (
24
) it suffices to show that the maps
\st_{X}(\sqz_{k}V)
and
\st_{\lambda}(\sqz_{k}V)
are equivalences for all perfect
V\in\Mod_{k}^{\mathrm{cn}}
. First, let us assume that
k
is algebraically closed.

By (2) and
Proposition

8.11
, the natural map
k\otimes_{\mathbf{Z}_{p}}H\rightarrow\mathrm{H}^{d}(X,\mathcal{O}_{X})[-d]
is an equivalence.
Using
Proposition

8.25
, we see that
\st_{X}(\sqz_{k}V)
is given by the map

\Map_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\!\big(\Omega^{1}_{X/k},\,V[1]\otimes_{k}\mathcal{O}_{X}\big)\longrightarrow\Map_{k}\!\big(k\otimes_{\mathbf{Z}}\RGamma(\mathbf{G}_{m,X}),\,V\otimes_{k}\mathrm{H}^{d}(\mathcal{O}_{X})[1-d]\big)

induced by
\RGamma
,
\dlog
, and the truncation. By (1) and
Proposition

8.11
, the map

\dlog\colon k\otimes_{\mathbf{Z}}\RGamma(X_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}},\mathbf{G}_{m})\rightarrow\RGamma(X,\Omega^{1}_{X/k})

is an equivalence in
\Mod_{k}
. Hence,
\st_{X}(\sqz_{k}V)
can be identified with the map

\Map_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\big(\Omega^{1}_{X/k},\,V[1]\otimes_{k}\mathcal{O}_{X}\big)\longrightarrow\Map_{k}\!\big(\RGamma(\Omega^{1}_{X}),\,V\otimes_{k}\mathrm{H}^{d}(X,\mathcal{O}_{X})[1-d]\big)

induced by
\RGamma
and truncation. As the canonical bundle is trivial, this map is an equivalence by Serre duality, so
\st_{X}(\sqz_{k}V)
is an equivalence.

Using
Proposition

8.26
, we see that
\st_{\lambda}(\sqz_{k}V)
identifies with the map
