\Map_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}(\mathcal{O}_{X},V[1]\otimes_{k}\mathcal{O}_{X})\longrightarrow\Map_{k}\!\big(\RGamma(\mathcal{O}_{X}),\,V\otimes_{k}\mathrm{H}^{d}(\mathcal{O}_{X})[1-d]\big)

induced by
\RGamma
and truncation. This is again an equivalence by Serre duality, and the assumption that the canonical bundle is trivial.

For a general perfect field
k
of characteristic
p
, it suffices to check that
\st_{X}
and
\st_{X,\lambda}
induce equivalences on tangent fibres after applying the functor
\overline{k}\otimes_{k}(-)
.
By
Remark

3.11
, this amounts to showing that the transformations
\Omega\st_{X}(\sqz_{k}\overline{V})
and
\Omega\st_{X,\lambda}(\sqz_{k}\overline{V})
are equivalences for all perfect
\overline{V}\in\Mod_{\overline{k}}
.
But
Proposition

4.36
,
Lemma

8.24
,
Proposition

8.26
,
and
Proposition

8.11
together imply that these transformations are equivalent to

\Omega\st_{\overline{X}}(\sqz_{k}\overline{V})
and
\Omega\st_{\overline{X},\lambda}(\sqz_{k}\overline{V})
, respectively, where
\overline{X}
is the base change of
X
to
\overline{k}
.
Hence they are equivalences by our previous discussion.
∎

Remark 8.29
.

If
\mathrm{H}^{d-1}(X,\mathcal{O}_{X})=0
, then
Condition (2) in

Theorem

8.28

is satisfied.

Remark 8.30

(Structure of
\pi_{0}\ST_{X}
and
\pi_{0}\ST_{X,\lambda}
)
.

Assume that
k
is algebraically closed.
If
X
is Bloch–Kato
2
-ordinary, then by
[
8
, Lemma 7.1]
, we have

\mathrm{H}^{\ast}(X_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}},\mathbf{G}^{\wedge}_{m})\cong\mathrm{H}^{\ast}(X,W\Omega^{1}_{X/k})^{F=1}

as
\mathbf{Z}_{p}
-modules. The group
\pi_{0}\ST_{X}(A)
can then be described explicitly as

\pi_{0}\ST_{X}(A)\cong\Hom_{\mathbf{Z}_{p}}\!\big(\mathrm{H}^{d-1}(X,W\Omega^{1}_{X/k})^{F=1},\,\mathrm{H}^{d}(X_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}},\mathbf{Z}_{p})\otimes_{\mathbf{Z}}\mathbf{G}_{m}(A)\big).

Moreover, the homomorphism
\varphi\colon\pi_{0}\ST_{X,\lambda}(A)\rightarrow\pi_{0}\ST_{X}(A)
satisfies

\displaystyle\ker(\varphi)

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\Hom_{\mathbf{Z}_{p}}\!\big(\mathrm{H}^{d-1}(X_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}},\mathbf{Z}_{p}),\,\mathrm{H}^{d}(X_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}},\mathbf{Z}_{p})\otimes_{\mathbf{Z}}\mathbf{G}_{m}(A)\big)

\displaystyle\coker(\varphi)

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\Hom_{\mathbf{Z}_{p}}\!\big(\mathrm{H}^{d-2}(X_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}},\mathbf{Z}_{p}),\,\mathrm{H}^{d}(X_{\mathrm{\text{\'{e}t}}},\mathbf{Z}_{p})\otimes_{\mathbf{Z}}\mathbf{G}_{m}(A)\big).

If
X
is a K3 surface, then this recovers Deligne’s description
[
14
, 2.2.2]
of the relation between canonical coordinates and deformations of line bundles on K3 surfaces.

Definition 8.31

(Canonical lifts)
.

Let
X/k
be as in
Theorem

8.28
. For every
n
, the unit of
\ST_{X}(W/p^{n})
corresponds to a lift
X_{n}
over
W/p^{n}
, and we denote the formal scheme
\lim_{n}X_{n}
over
W

by
X^{\mathrm{can}}
. We refer to it as the
canonical lift
of
X
.

Corollary 8.32

(
Theorem

C
)
.

If
X
is projective, then so is
X^{\mathrm{can}}
.

Proof.

Assume
\lambda\colon\mathbf{Z}[-1]\rightarrow\mathbf{G}_{m,X}
corresponds to an ample line bundle
\mathcal{L}
on
X
. For each
n
, the unit section of
\ST_{X,\lambda}(W/p^{n})
corresponds to a lift
\lambda_{n}\colon\mathbf{Z}[-1]\rightarrow\mathbf{G}_{m,X_{n}}
of
\lambda
, and hence to a (necessarily ample) line bundle
\mathcal{L}_{n}
on
X_{n}
. These form a compatible system, giving rise to an ample line bundle
\mathcal{L}^{\mathrm{can}}
on
X^{\mathrm{can}}
.
∎

Remark 8.33

(Canonical lifts of Brauer classes)
.

There is nothing special about the shift
[-1]
in the definition of
\ST_{X,\lambda}
, and in the proof of
Theorem

8.28
. The same reasoning can be applied to deformations of maps
\mathbf{Z}[-2]\rightarrow\mathbf{G}_{m,X}
, and this shows that Brauer classes on
X
admit canonical lifts to
X^{\mathrm{can}}
.

Remark 8.34

(Complex multiplication)
.

Choose an embedding
W(k)\rightarrow\mathbf{C}
. Then every
X/k
satisfying the conditions of
Theorem

B
gives rise to a complex variety
X^{\mathrm{can}}_{\mathbf{C}}
. If
X
is an abelian variety or a K3 surface, then
X^{\mathrm{can}}_{\mathbf{C}}
is known to have complex multiplication. More generally, this should be the case for families parametrised by Shimura varieties.
We do not expect canonical lifts of arbitrary ordinary Calabi–Yau varieties to be of CM type. In fact, this would contradict conjectures on CM points in variations of Hodge structures. Indeed, consider for example quintic threefolds. There exist an algebraically closed field
k
of positive characteristic, and a quintic threefold
X/k
for which the Hodge and Newton polygons coincide, and by Grothendieck’s semicontinuity theorem
[
32
, Theorem 2.3.1]
, this is true for an open dense subset of the quintic threefolds over
k
. By
Theorem

C
, they all have canonical lifts which are quintic threefolds. If these are all CM, then the moduli space of quintic threefolds over
\mathbf{C}
would have a dense collection of CM points, contradicting
e.g.
a conjecture by Klingler
[
34
, Conjecture 5.6]
.

9.
Spectral lifts

In the previous sections, we have used techniques from derived algebraic geometry to prove that large classes of Calabi–Yau varieties admit lifts to the Witt vectors. One could ask if one can use spectral algebraic geometry to prove that these even lift to the spherical Witt vectors, as is the case for ordinary abelian varieties. We will show that this rarely happens.

Let
k
be a perfect field of characteristic
p
. Let
\SW(k)
be the
\mathbf{E}_{\infty}
-ring spectrum of spherical Witt vectors
[
46
, §5.2]
. This is a
p
-complete
\mathbf{E}_{\infty}
-ring spectrum, flat over the sphere spectrum
\mathbf{S}
, such that
\mathbf{F}_{p}\otimes_{\mathbf{S}}\SW(k)\simeq k
. We have
\pi_{0}\SW(k)\cong W(k)
.

Proposition 9.1
.

Let
X
be a smooth projective irreducible scheme over an algebraically closed field
k
of characteristic
p
with
\omega_{X/k}\cong\mathcal{O}_{X}
. If
X
lifts to a formal spectral scheme over
\SW(k)
, then
X
admits finite étale cover by an abelian variety.

This shows in particular that a simply connected Calabi–Yau variety such as a K3 surface or a quintic threefold cannot lift to
\SW(k)
.

The proof is based on the fact that flat commutative
\SW(k)
-algebras carry canonical Frobenius lifts.
Let us write
\CAlg_{\SW(k)}^{\mathrm{fl}}\subset\CAlg_{\SW(k)}
for the full subcategory spanned by all
\mathbf{E}_{\infty}
-
\SW(k)
-algebras which are flat in the sense of
[
45
, 7.2.2.10]
.
Write
\CR_{W(k)}
for the category of ordinary (commutative)
W(k)
-algebras, and

\CR_{W(k)}^{\mathrm{fl},\delta}
for the category of flat
W(k)
-algebras
A
equipped with a ring homomorphism
\phi\colon A\rightarrow A
such that
\phi(x)\equiv x^{p}\bmod{p}
.

Lemma 9.2
.

The functor
\pi_{0}\colon\CAlg_{\SW(k)}^{\mathrm{fl}}\rightarrow\CR_{W(k)}
admits a lift to
\CR^{\mathrm{fl},\delta}_{W(k)}
.

Proof.

Let
K
be the Morava
E
-theory corresponding to the formal multiplicative group over
k
. We have
\pi_{*}K\cong W(k)[u,u^{-1}]
with
|u|=2
. Using the universal property of the
\mathbf{E}_{\infty}
-ring of spherical Witt vectors, we obtain maps of
\mathbf{E}_{\infty}
-rings

\SW(k)\rightarrow\tau_{\geq 0}K\rightarrow K

inducing isomorphisms on
\pi_{0}
.
Consider the functor
\CAlg_{\SW(k)}^{\mathrm{fl}}\rightarrow\CAlg_{K}^{\wedge}
,

B\mapsto B^{\prime}:=(K\otimes_{\SW(k)}B)^{\wedge}_{p}
. Here
\CAlg_{K}^{\wedge}
denotes to the
\infty
-category of
p
-complete, i.e.
K(1)
-local,
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-
K
-algebras.
By
[
29
, §3]

[
48
]
, the ring
\pi_{0}(B^{\prime})
carries a natural Frobenius lift, functorial in
B^{\prime}
. As
B^{\prime}
is flat over
\SW(k)
, the map
\pi_{0}(B)\rightarrow\pi_{0}(B^{\prime})
is an isomorphism.
∎

Proof of
Proposition

9.1
.

Let
(X,\mathcal{B})
be a formal spectral scheme over
\SW(k)
lifting

(X,\mathcal{O}_{X})
. A similar argument as in
Corollary

3.35
shows that the
\SW(k)
-module
\mathcal{B}(U)
is flat for every affine open
U\subset X
. By
Lemma

9.2
, the rings
\pi_{0}\mathcal{B}(U)
acquire compatible lifts of Frobenius. In particular,
X
lifts to a scheme
(X,W_{2}(k)\otimes_{W(k)}\pi_{0}\mathcal{B})
over
W_{2}(k)
, equipped with a Frobenius lift. But then it follows from
[
3
, Proposition 3.3.1.c]
and
[
49
, Theorem 2]
that
X
admits a finite étale cover by an ordinary abelian variety.
∎
